# 14: Decision log

This log holds every decision from the planning conversation (2026-09-28 and 2026-10-11) and the blueprint session (2026-10-15), **including the options we rejected and the reversals**. It exists so a later agent understands how the design got here and doesn't argue its way back into an option already rejected. BK is the decision-maker:

- **BK:** his own call.
- **BK proposed; agreed:** his idea, refined in conversation.
- **Claude proposed; unchallenged:** raised by the assistant in the chat, built on by BK and never objected to. These are the design's working assumptions, and the easiest to revisit.
- **Claude, in the blueprint:** made while writing this blueprint, where the chat never reached the question. Each one has a product question in [15](15-open-questions.md), or is grounded in a research file.

Change a decision only by adding a new entry that replaces the old one.

Numbers are not contiguous. Ranges were set aside for parallel workers and some went unused (the largest gap is 25 numbers in the D-190s and D-200s). A missing number is not a lost decision.

## 2026-11-13 round 11: polish and the dev editor fixture

### D-563: A ZMK keymap with another board's number of keys names both boards and offers the one it fits; a Linux engine keyloom can't restart is named with the line that restarts it; on a phone the agent's pill rides in the open sheet's bar; the dev editor serves the configs routes and Restart keyloom by default. Refines D-488, D-511, D-544 and D-552. (Claude, from the round-11 dead-ends review, R11-09, R11-10, R11-11 and the fixture finding)

- **A keymap for another board** (R11-09). A 42-key Corne keymap dropped on the Keebart Corne Choc Pro BT's card was refused as "layer base has 42 bindings, but keebart/corne-choc-pro-bt has 46", in board ids, with Start blank as the only way on. The ZMK importer now throws `ZmkKeyCountError` with the keymap's count, and says "This keymap has 42 keys, and the Keebart Corne Choc Pro BT has 46.", only when every layer has that one count. Layers that disagree are a plain `ZmkImportError`: "This keymap's layers don't have the same number of keys (base has 46, nav has 42), and the Keebart Corne Choc Pro BT has 46 on every layer.", with Start blank and no board offered, because no board fits them. The first version named the first layer that differed and offered a board by that layer's count, so a Corne keymap with one short layer was offered the foostan Corne, whose card offered the Keebart Corne back (PR #318 review). `importLayout` (`keyCountRefusal`) looks for another ZMK board keyloom knows with that many keys. When there is exactly one, it refuses with `BoardRefusal("keymap_for_other_board", <board>)`, whose sentence adds "It fits the foostan Corne: pass --board foostan/corne." for the CLI. The import card maps the code as it maps `layout_for_other_board` (D-544): "corne.keymap has 42 keys, and the Keebart Corne Choc Pro BT has 46. It fits the foostan Corne." with **Import it as a Corne config**, which imports the same file to that board, and **Start blank**. With no board that fits, the refusal stays a usage error and its sentence shows with the card's own buttons.
  - **Product choice made without BK:** only another ZMK board is offered, because a QMK or kanata board can't read a ZMK keymap. When several ZMK boards have that count (a user-derived board can share a built-in board's), none is offered over the others: the usage error's sentence names each ("It fits the foostan Corne or the … ."), and the person picks a card. The first version offered whichever board was listed first (PR #318 review). The CLI's exit code is 2 in every case, as for any usage error.
  - Checked on a real `keyloom serve` with a scratch data dir: the 42-key fixture `test/fixtures/zmk/corne/corne.keymap` dropped on the Keebart card shows the words and both buttons, and Import it as a Corne config opens the new Corne config.
- **Linux's engine restart** (R11-10). On Linux, keyloom's forced restart knows only Colima (`forcedRestartEngine` returns null for the Docker service and the Linux desktop apps), so a stale mount or a stuck container said "Restarting your Docker engine clears this. Restart it, then build again." with nothing to press. The step now names the engine that answers, and gives the line that restarts it, with Copy, only where keyloom knows that line restarts what answers (`linuxRestart` in `build-failure-words.ts`):
  - **The system Docker service** gets `sudo systemctl restart docker` ("It needs your password, so keyloom doesn't run it."), only when readiness found it. A ready readiness now carries `systemService: true` (additive, optional in `DockerReadinessResponseSchema`) when, on Linux, no engine is named, the context's host is `unix:///var/run/docker.sock`, and systemd's system `docker` unit is active (`systemDockerService` in `docker.ts`: `systemctl is-active --quiet docker`, which needs no root). Rootless Docker, Lima, Docker Desktop and a remote context use another socket. Podman's Docker socket can sit at the system path, but then no `docker` unit runs.
  - **Docker Desktop** gets `systemctl --user restart docker-desktop`. Docker's own Linux docs start and stop Docker Desktop with `systemctl --user … docker-desktop`.
  - **Any other engine** (Podman Desktop, Rancher Desktop, rootless Docker, Lima, a remote context, or one keyloom can't name) gets no command: "Restarting Podman Desktop clears this, and keyloom can't restart it from here. Restart Podman Desktop the way you started it, then build again.", or "your Docker engine" when keyloom can't name it.
  - The first version gave every engine but the two desktop apps the root command, so Podman, rootless Docker and any unnamed engine were told to restart a service they don't use (PR #318 review). It also gave Rancher Desktop `systemctl --user restart rancher-desktop`, which assumes a user unit that nothing here shows Rancher Desktop installs on Linux. `knownEngine`'s start path (`docker.ts`) still makes that assumption; this round leaves it, and it stays unverified.
  - Build again stays the way on after each.
  - **Product choice made without BK:** a command with Copy, not a button. The system service needs root, which keyloom never asks for on Linux (the R8 limit stands). The user services need no root, and keyloom could restart them. That needs hardware evidence of their restart on Linux, which no spike has. Until then the command is the honest way on.
  - The page tells Linux from the browser's user agent (`linuxBrowser`), because the page runs on the computer keyloom serves (localhost only). Not `navigator.platform`: a server-side render in Bun answers that with the server's own platform, which put a Linux CI runner's render of the Mac words on the Linux path (PR #318 CI). A Mac whose engine keyloom doesn't know keeps the old words: there is no command to name for an engine nobody identified.
- **The agent's pill on a phone** (R11-11). The pill (Show agent work, Stop) was fixed at the bottom-right corner, over the bottom sheet's Apply in the key card, Combos and Settings. On a narrow screen with a side-panel sheet open, the pill now renders in the sheet's bar, beside the grabber (`SidePanel`'s `barAside`), where nothing else is. With no sheet open it stays in the corner. The lab's card and the proposals, review, lab and new-keyboard sheets float at the stage's foot with no bar, and their height follows their content, so on a phone the pill is measured above whichever is up (`usePanelLift`, a ResizeObserver on the panel), over the board, and never rises over the top bar. Found while checking: the lab's start card on a phone is not a sheet, and a first fix that lifted the pill by each sheet's CSS cap left it over that card's Restart keyloom.
  - **Rejected: lifting the pill above the side-panel sheet.** At full height the sheet reaches the caption, so the pill would sit on the caption's review chip and the half switcher.
- **The dev editor** (fixture). It served no configs routes, so every dev-editor page showed "Couldn't read proposals." with a Restart keyloom that said the keyloom was older, which the real `keyloom serve` never shows. `scripts/dev-editor.ts` now serves the configs routes over its one layout (`test/helpers/fake-configs-backend.ts`: the list, the versions as pending revisions, and no open proposals; writes are refused in words) and the restart and log routes by default. `KEYLOOM_FAKE_RESTART=0` leaves the restart routes out. New `KEYLOOM_FAKE_UNEXPECTED` entries fail what round 11 fixes: `configs=internal|unreadable` (the list read only; the versions and proposals reads still answer, so each failure can be checked on its own, PR #318 review), `proposals=internal|unreadable|gone|missing` (`missing` serves no configs routes, as before) and `apply=internal|unreadable`. Each of the dev editor's switches was started once on a scratch port, and the configs and restart routes answered as the switch says.
- **Checked by:** unit tests of the words and the refusal (the importer, `importLayout` against the real Corne fixture, the card's mapping) and the Linux words; screenshots at 390 px with the key card, Combos, Settings and the lab's card open, and at 1440 px of the import card and the Linux step (a Linux user agent and engine set in the browser), light and dark, in `.orchestrate/keyloom-dead-ends/evidence/r11-c/`. The pill's place has no unit test: it is layout, which only a rendered page shows.

### D-562: A read of a config's proposals, a layer's Delete or conditions change, and the config list keep their failure's code, so a request that fails the same way never keeps a Try again; a config deleted elsewhere offers the other layouts; the config card has a Try again; the repair recheck says where the request is. Extends D-534, D-540, D-551 and D-552. (Claude, from the round-11 dead-ends review, R11-05 to R11-08)

- **The open-proposals read** (R11-05). The proposals panel and the board review kept only a boolean, so an unreadable answer or keyloom's catch-all 404 kept **Try again** for ever. The caption dropped `generic`, so a config deleted in another window (the route's own `not_found`) said "Couldn't read proposals." with Restart keyloom, which D-534 rules out. The Viewer now keeps the `PageFailure`, and `proposalsReadWay` (`src/web/chat/proposals-read.ts`) picks the way on:
  - the route's own `not_found`: "keyloom no longer has this config. It may have been deleted in another window." with **Show your other layouts** (the layout page's `OtherLayoutsContext`) and no restart. The caption says "keyloom no longer has this config." with the same button;
  - an answer that fails the same way (`failsAgain`): Restart keyloom alone, in the caption, the panel and the review;
  - anything else: Try again, with Restart keyloom and the log added for an exception, whose sentence follows the screen's own (D-551).
- **A layer's Delete or conditions change** (R11-06). Both ran `quiet` and showed only `outcome.message`, so "The details are in keyloom's log." had no log button and Delete stayed on. `LayerRefusal` now shows the sentence, the validator's reasons and, for a failure no screen planned for, Restart keyloom and Show keyloom's log (`UnexpectedWayOut`). An answer that fails the same way turns Delete off, with "Deleting it again would fail the same way. Restart keyloom first." beside it, and Restart keyloom leads. Keep the layer stays on.
  - **Product choice made without BK:** the conditions view keeps its chips, Remove and Add on after such a failure. Each is a separate change, as D-551's mismatch page keeps its actions, and the refusal beside them says Restart keyloom.
- **The config list** (R11-07). `useConfigs` swallowed a failed read, so the switcher listed nothing, said nothing and kept **Archive** on for the config on the keyboard. It now keeps the failure. The switcher says "keyloom couldn't read this keyboard's configs." with **Read them again** (`RetryOrRestart`: Restart keyloom alone where reading again fails the same way, and the log for an exception). While the list is unknown, Archive is off with "keyloom can't tell whether this config is on your {board} until it reads your configs again." after a failed read, and "keyloom is still reading your configs, to see which one is on your {board}." before the first read answers (`loaded`; the first version left Archive on then, PR #318 review), because the server refuses to archive the config on the keyboard and only the list says which one that is. The chat's config card said "Couldn't load this config right now." with no button; it now says "keyloom couldn't read this config right now." with Try again (`useConfigCards`' `reload`), or Restart keyloom by the same rule. A card that showed its config before keeps it after a failed read.
  - The mismatch page's own `archiveChoice` is unchanged: it keeps Archive and lets the server refuse, as recorded there.
- **The repair recheck** (R11-08). "Your request is kept below." showed under the request's box. It now says "Your request is kept in the box above."
- **Checked by:** unit tests for each piece, including the switcher and its Archive driven through the real hook in a document (happy-dom, D-556), and dev-editor screenshots of the caption, the switcher and a layer's Delete with each failure, light and dark, in `.orchestrate/keyloom-dead-ends/evidence/r11-c/`. The proposals panel and the review can't be opened on a failed read in the dev editor (they open from a count), so their failure is checked by rendering their markup in tests.

## 2026-11-13 round 11: tool downloads without Homebrew

### D-561: Get Zapp downloads Zapp 1.0.2's pinned release on a Mac without Homebrew, and the flash finds it there. Extends D-489. (Claude, from the round-11 dead-ends review, R11-03; the orchestrator chose pinned downloads)

- **What changed.** Without Homebrew, the flash step's Zapp callout had **Get Zapp** as a link to Zapp's GitHub page and Check again. A Zapp the person downloaded landed in Downloads, and the flash looked only on PATH, so Check again showed the same step for ever. Now the button is **Get Zapp**, and it runs the install job, which downloads the release instead of running `brew`. The step shows "Getting Zapp from its release on GitHub. This takes a few seconds." with the log and Cancel. When the job ends, the dialog reads the build again and Flash appears.
- **The pins.** From `gh release view --repo zsa/zapp v1.0.2 --json assets` and the files themselves, 2026-11-13 (the repo is the one D-043 and chapter 05 name). Each archive was downloaded once into a scratch folder, its SHA-256 compared with GitHub's asset digest, and the `zapp` inside hashed:
  - arm64: `https://github.com/zsa/zapp/releases/download/v1.0.2/zapp-macos-aarch64.tar.gz`, 3,229,402 bytes, archive `5028e38c9976097ba8950c5ec297d2901ba3aee30ed5b504bc2f827390f0ab15`, `zapp` `12dac6da8d31cacfcd60405e1323345f1b6319a2631bdf422be6ec6d155df642`.
  - x64: `https://github.com/zsa/zapp/releases/download/v1.0.2/zapp-macos-x86_64.tar.gz`, 3,539,635 bytes, archive `e62a3c2cdabc7b2b2917e934499bcfad57610236d25a0333c8c30161b3167515`, `zapp` `b594fadc0ac33ef0923605c52ad741a41c97be4516722fd5d4005874033985f0`.
  - Both programs carry ZSA's Developer ID signature (`codesign -dvv`: "Developer ID Application: ZSA Technology Labs Inc. (V32BWKSNYH)"). The release also has a universal file; keyloom takes the one for this processor, which is half the size.
- **Where it looks.** `findTool("zapp")` (`src/firmware/toolchain/kept-tools.ts`) is Zapp on PATH, then keyloom's own copy (D-560's folder). The flash plan, `startFlash`'s missing-tool check, the flash itself and `keyloom doctor` all use it, so what is checked is what runs. keyloom's copy counts only while its bytes still hash to this processor's pinned `zapp` (PR #317 review, as for kanata in D-560). A changed copy is not found, so keyloom never runs it, and the flash step offers Get Zapp, which writes the pinned program over it. Zapp runs as the person, so this crosses no privilege boundary, and the step does not say why Zapp is missing. One `downloadsHere` (`src/app/tool-install.ts`) decides both the plan's Get Zapp and the install job's download, so the button and the job can't disagree.
- **Homebrew stays first.** With Homebrew, Install Zapp still runs `brew install zapp`, because Homebrew keeps Zapp up to date. keyloom pins no DFU tool, so dfu-util, dfu-programmer and avrdude keep their pages.
- **Failures.** The install state carries the download's codes. `download_failed`, `checksum_mismatch` and `write_failed` say "keyloom couldn't get Zapp." with the reason, Try again, **Open Zapp's page** and Check again. `no_asset` can't reach the job: Get Zapp is offered only where a release file exists. The flash plan's `canDownload` means "the download is the way keyloom gets it here", so it is false where Homebrew is. It is optional on the wire, so an older server's plan reads as before.
- **CLI (D-030).** `keyloom doctor --download zapp` runs the same download, and does nothing when a Zapp is already found. `FlashToolMissingError` and `keyloom doctor` name it beside `brew install zapp`.
- **Checked by:** unit tests with an injected fetcher and scratch folders (`test/app/tool-download.test.ts`, `tool-install.test.ts`, `firmware-flash-plan.test.ts`, `test/web/tool-words.test.ts`, `test/cli/download-tool.test.ts`). The opt-in `KEYLOOM_DOWNLOAD_TESTS=1` test downloaded all four real files into scratch folders and every pin matched. The dev editor's `KEYLOOM_FAKE_NO_BREW=1` was rendered through Get Zapp, its progress, and a mismatch, light and dark. Not observed: a real Zapp downloaded by keyloom flashing a Moonlander. That needs a Mac without Homebrew and a flash, and this round flashes nothing.

### D-560: Get kanata downloads kanata 1.12.0's pinned release on a Mac without Homebrew, checks it, and keeps it in keyloom's own folder, which findKanata reads last. Extends D-493, D-498 and D-503. (Claude, from the round-11 dead-ends review, R11-03; the orchestrator chose pinned downloads)

- **What changed.** Without Homebrew, the checklist said "Homebrew isn't on this Mac, so download kanata from its releases page." with a link. `findKanata` never looked in Downloads, so the step never went away. Now the step is **Get kanata**: "Homebrew isn't on this Mac, so keyloom downloads kanata 1.12.0 from its official release on GitHub, checks it and keeps it in keyloom's own folder." It runs as the checklist's install job with a new `download` action, so it has the same progress lines, **Cancel download** and failure handling as Install kanata. When it ends, the doctor finds kanata and the next step is Set up and start kanata. Start's reason says "Get kanata first." while the step is Get kanata. A failed Homebrew install now offers **Download kanata** as a button beside Try again, not a link.
- **The pins.** From `gh release view --repo jtroo/kanata v1.12.0 --json assets`, 2026-11-13. kanata 1.12.0 is the version the MacBook board writes for (`boards/apple/macbook-ansi/board.json`, D-231); a test fails if the two differ. Each zip was downloaded once into a scratch folder, its SHA-256 compared with GitHub's digest and with the release's own `sha256sums`, and the program inside hashed:
  - arm64: `https://github.com/jtroo/kanata/releases/download/v1.12.0/macos-binaries-arm64.zip`, 2,367,443 bytes, archive `839769d189911b5881e11550eaa2039705213fb725865d088f5a2e3a6c10de32`, member `kanata_macos_arm64` `ca1f6c52c66f1486e21eb31eb0aa4e0d5248239ff3eea887ecb7ebb80caa133b`.
  - x64: `https://github.com/jtroo/kanata/releases/download/v1.12.0/macos-binaries-x64.zip`, 2,377,539 bytes, archive `bdb8b2f7ae2a648336a2561c7c6c00a736cd76e012035df651114b304c5ffb01`, member `kanata_macos_x64` `c98b4d2e9edccc26a405a55183de880ce15a8d9087803951e80d487bba14a7bf`.
  - Each zip holds two builds. keyloom takes the plain one, never `kanata_macos_cmd_allowed_*`. `strings` shows the plain builds contain "kanata was compiled to never allow cmd" and not "DANGER! cmd action is enabled", and the `cmd_allowed` build the reverse. D-498's `kanata --check` probe still runs before the password dialog. The programs are ad hoc linker-signed (`codesign -dv`).
- **The rules.** HTTPS from the project's own GitHub release. keyloom follows GitHub's redirects itself (`redirect: "manual"`, at most five hops), and refuses a hop that isn't `https:` or isn't on `github.com`, `objects.githubusercontent.com` or `release-assets.githubusercontent.com` before it asks that place for anything (PR #317 review; GitHub sent both releases to `release-assets.githubusercontent.com` on 2026-11-13, by `curl -sI`). That check keeps keyloom off unencrypted and foreign hosts. The hashes below are what make the file trustworthy: a file from anywhere else can only fail them. The archive is read into memory, capped at the pinned size, and hashed. Only then is the one named program taken out of it (fflate for kanata's zip, `Bun.Archive` for Zapp's tar.gz) and hashed against its own pin. Only then is it written, mode 755, to a temporary name in the folder, and renamed into place. A mismatch writes nothing, and nothing downloaded runs before both checks pass. There is no "latest": a new version is a new pin in `src/firmware/toolchain/tool-pins.ts`. keyloom strips no quarantine attribute; a file it writes itself gets none.
- **D-498's root copy, and a kept copy that changed** (PR #317 review). The folder belongs to the person, so any program running as them can write over keyloom's copy after the download. The root script copies whatever file setup hashed, so the pin is checked again at every use:
  - `keptTool` (`src/firmware/toolchain/kept-tools.ts`) returns keyloom's copy only while its bytes hash to this processor's pinned program. `findKanata` and `findTool` go through it, so a changed copy is never found and never run, and the doctor never reaches `kanata_replaced` ("Set up with this kanata") for it.
  - The doctor adds `kanata_kept_changed` beside `kanata_missing` when the copy is there but changed. The checklist's kanata step then starts "keyloom's copy of kanata changed since keyloom downloaded it, so keyloom won't use it or set it up." and offers **Get kanata**, which writes the pinned program over it. No step offers the password dialog for it.
  - `daemonInstallFor` (`src/app/remapper.ts`) checks the bytes it reads for root once more, before the `cmd` probe runs them. If they come from keyloom's copy and aren't the pinned program, setup stops with `kanata_kept_changed`: "keyloom's copy of kanata changed since keyloom downloaded it, so keyloom won't set it up. Get kanata again to replace it." This catches a file changed between the lookup and the read, and a `KEYLOOM_KANATA_BIN` that names keyloom's copy.
  - A pinned copy needs no change to setup: its hash is the pinned one, so the root script's hash check copies exactly that file.
- **Where keyloom keeps them.** `~/Library/Application Support/keyloom/bin`, or `KEYLOOM_TOOLS_DIR`, which the test preload points at a scratch folder. It is not on PATH and not in `/usr/local`. It is not under the data directory either, though the brief suggested it. keyloom's data directory is XDG (`~/.local/share/keyloom`), and `findKanata` and the Zapp flash run with no data directory, so a tool belongs to the computer, as Homebrew's do. A scratch `--data-dir` shares the person's kept tools. To check by hand without that, set `KEYLOOM_TOOLS_DIR`. The module table now lets `firmware/kanata` import `firmware/toolchain` for this folder (`.dependency-cruiser.cjs`, chapter 01).
- **Order.** `findKanata` reads `KEYLOOM_KANATA_BIN`, PATH, Homebrew's two folders, then keyloom's copy. A kanata the person installed always wins, so a later Homebrew install is the one used.
- **Failures.** `download_failed` ("keyloom couldn't download kanata: GitHub answered 404. Check that this computer is online, then try again.") and `checksum_mismatch` ("…its checksum doesn't match. keyloom kept none of it. Try again, and if it happens again, get kanata from its release page.") show with Try again and **Open kanata's release page**. `write_failed` names the folder. A processor with no release file (`no_asset`) shows the releases page and no button. On macOS today that needs an architecture other than arm64 or x64, so it isn't reachable, but the words are tested.
- **CLI (D-030).** `keyloom doctor --download kanata` runs the same download.
- **Product choices made without BK.** The folder above, and not running the downloaded kanata to read its version: the pin fixes the version, and the doctor reads it as it does any kanata.
- **Known limit.** The doctor's `kanata_replaced` rule (D-498) treats a pinned kept kanata whose hash differs from the root copy as changed outside Homebrew, with "Set up with this kanata". That offer is safe, because the copy is the pinned release. A changed kept copy never reaches the rule (above). Treating the pinned copy as an update, as for Homebrew, needs a larger change to `doctor.ts`, which another worker edits this round.
- **Checked by:** unit tests with an injected fetcher and scratch folders: the download, both hash checks, a body larger than the pin, a 404, an offline fetch, a redirect followed to GitHub's storage host, a plain-HTTP or foreign hop after a good one (refused before it is fetched), a redirect loop, a kept copy changed after the download (not found, named by the doctor, refused by setup before the probe runs, replaced by Get kanata), Cancel, a folder that can't be written, `no_asset`, the preload's refusal, the job, the route's `download` action, the checklist's words, and the CLI. The opt-in `KEYLOOM_DOWNLOAD_TESTS=1` test downloaded the real files into scratch folders and every pin matched. The dev editor's `KEYLOOM_FAKE_SYNC=kanata_no_brew` was rendered through Get kanata, its progress, Set up and start kanata, and a mismatch, light and dark. Not observed: Get kanata on a real Mac without Homebrew, and the downloaded kanata run by the daemon. That needs S30's hardware run.

## 2026-11-13 round 10: keyboards, configs, import and first run

### D-557: The setup card stops saying "nothing of yours is on it"; a plugged-in keyboard's card remembers Not now across config switches and waits for the review and dialogs; Add after a race offers Put a config on it; a tall config dialog fits a phone. Refines D-336, D-544 and D-550. (Claude, from the round-10 dead-ends review, R10-04, R10-08, R10-13, R10-15)

- **The setup card's lead** (R10-04). It said "keyloom knows this keyboard, but nothing of yours is on it." That is false for most first runs: a Moonlander runs the person's Oryx layout, and a Glove80 or Corne runs their own ZMK. The heading already says "Your {board} has no config yet", so the lead now starts with what to do: "Import the layout you already use, or start from a blank one."
  - **Product choice made without BK.** The round-10 brief asked for "keyloom has no config for it yet." as the first sentence. On screen that repeats the heading word for word, so the sentence was dropped. The heading carries the fact.
- **The welcome's lead** (R10-04). "Connect a keyboard or choose it below" shows only while the list of keyboards shows (`welcomeLead(chosen, listed)`). While a keyboard is being set up, the list is hidden, so the lead is "Setup never flashes your keyboard." The MacBook keeps its kanata sentence.
- **Not now across a config switch** (R10-08). `dismissed` and `shown` lived in each `useNewKeyboard`. The editor remounts on every config switch, so a card put off with Not now, or one that had opened once, opened again by itself. App now keeps one `CardMemory` (`useCardMemory`) and passes it to the welcome and to every editor.
- **No card over the review or a dialog** (R10-08). A card doesn't open by itself while the board review is open (an import opens it), while one of the editor's config dialogs or its restart dialog is open, or while any `dialog[open]` is on the page, such as Your keyboards or Build and flash. It opens on the first render after the hold ends. A dialog that closes re-renders the page by itself: the hook listens for `close` on the document, in the capture phase because `close` doesn't bubble. Without that, Build and flash closed by its Close button or its backdrop left the card waiting for an unrelated change, because the release dialog closes itself in an effect that runs after the card's check (PR #314 review). The board switch's chip is the way in meanwhile.
  - **Known gap:** a card and a dialog that open in the same render both open, and the card ends up under the dialog. Closing the dialog shows the card. No `open` event exists to catch it, and the case needs a keyboard plugged in during the click that opens the dialog.
  - **Rejected: a hold list of every dialog by name.** Dialogs live in App, the release host and the editor. The page's own `dialog[open]` covers them all, and the editor's states cover the review, which is not a dialog.
- **Not now keeps the chip** (PR #314 review). Not now on a known board's setup card put the board in `dismissed`, and the board switch's chip read the same filtered list, so with App's memory both the card and the chip were gone until a reload. The chip now reads every known board with no config, as the unknown keyboard's chip reads every keyboard keyloom hasn't met. Only the card that opens by itself skips a board put off with Not now. This is what D-336's "stays one click away" asks.
- **Add after a race** (R10-13). Another tab or the CLI adds the same board between the preview and Add. Add is refused with `board_known`, and the page showed the server's sentence, which names `--force` and `keyloom keyboard put`. `add()` now maps `board_known` as the preview does (`addedElsewhere`): the Review step says "keyloom knows this keyboard, so there is nothing to add." with Put a config on it or Start a config, and Add goes.
- **A tall config dialog on a phone** (R10-15). Its `max-height` left out the 72 px top margin, so the footer of a tall dialog, such as the mismatch page's put-on with kanata's checklist, ran 24 px off a 390 px phone. It is now `calc(100dvh - 72px - var(--space-5))`.
- **Checked by:** unit tests of the words, which render the setup card for all five boards; tests that drive the hook in a document (happy-dom, D-556): Not now survives a remount with App's memory and didn't without it, Not now keeps the chip, a held card opens once the hold ends, a card waits for an open dialog, and a card held behind a dialog shaped like Build and flash opens once its Close button closes it, with nothing else re-rendering. The first-run setup card was rendered for all five boards on a scratch server at 1280 px and 390 px, light and dark. The race and the review hold were not rendered: no fixture adds a board from a second tab.

### D-556: Restart keyloom leads beside a button that went off; the mismatch page, the import card, the QMK and ZMK search and Restore {board} turn off a request that fails the same way again. Extends D-540, D-544 and D-551. (Claude, from the round-10 dead-ends review, R10-05, R10-07)

- **`FailureNote` takes `primary`** (R10-05). It always passed `primary={false}`, so Rename, New config, Delete and the new-keyboard steps showed a greyed confirm with no live primary button. They now pass their `stuck` flag, so Restart keyloom is the primary button exactly while the confirm is off. A screen whose control stays on keeps Restart keyloom second.
- **The mismatch page** (R10-05). A Go back or Archive that keyloom answers with its catch-all `not_found` or an unreadable answer goes off and stops being primary (`mismatchActionOff`). The page remembers which button the refusal answered, so the other buttons stay. Restart keyloom beside the sentence is the primary button.
  - **Product choice made without BK.** D-551 kept every mismatch control on, because each is a separate request. The round-10 review asked for Go back. Archive meets the same answer the same way, so it goes off too when it was the one refused. Show your other layouts and Put … on it stay, since they send other requests.
- **The import card** (R10-05). Read it from this Mac and Start blank go off after an answer that fails the same way again, and Restart keyloom becomes primary. The card remembers which control failed. A file the Mac read that then fails to import counts as Read it from this Mac's failure. The drop zone stays on: each drop is a new file.
- **The QMK and ZMK search** (R10-07). The failure is kept whole. Search again shows for a failure that may pass, Search again with Restart keyloom and the log for an exception, and Restart keyloom alone for an answer that fails the same way (`RetryOrRestart`).
- **Restore {board}** (R10-07). `restoreThenRetry` returns the failure with its code. Restore {board} goes off after an answer that fails the same way, with Restart keyloom primary beside it; an exception keeps Restore as the Try again, with Restart keyloom second.
- **Tests that drive the turned-off state.** No test clicked a confirm into its failure; the markup tests passed the state in. `@happy-dom/global-registrator` (a dev dependency) now gives a test file a document (`test/helpers/dom.tsx`). The tests press Put it on, Rename, Make, Delete and Restore into a catch-all `not_found`, an unreadable answer and an exception, and read the buttons the person then sees.
  - **Rejected: exporting each dialog's state for tests,** or a prop to start a dialog already failed. Both test a path the app never takes. A browser test under `agent-browser` would drive the real page, but CI doesn't run those.
  - **The helper puts back what it found** (PR #314 review): the document, `fetch` and React's `IS_REACT_ACT_ENVIRONMENT` flag, so a later file that mounts with `react-dom/client` doesn't inherit the act environment.
  - **Restore's own failure** (PR #314 review). A failure with no words of its own reads "keyloom couldn't restore the Glove80.", not "keyloom couldn't restore the Glove80. That didn't work.".
  - **Rejected: registering the document in the test preload.** Every other test file would then render with a document, and code that checks for one would behave differently from how it runs on the server.

## 2026-11-13 round 10: sync, kanata, Build and flash, and Docker

### D-555: Build and flash, Replace it and a running install keep a failure's code; the flashing-permission words name the app macOS lists; a ready Docker names its engine. Extends D-540, D-549 and D-551; refines D-501 and D-548. (Claude, from the round-10 dead-ends review, R10-05, R10-06, R10-07, R10-10)

- **Replace it carries the session token** (R10-05). kanata's Replace it confirm gets `sync.token`, so a failure no screen planned for shows Restart keyloom and the log, and Replace it goes off when it would fail the same way. D-551's exception for this dialog no longer applies.
- **Build and flash keeps the code** (R10-07). A failed read of the build on screen (`buildReadFailure`) says Try again only where a second read can pass, Restart keyloom for an answer that fails the same way, and Build the firmware again for a build that is gone. For a gone build, the dialog drops the old build from its steps, so Build firmware shows under the words and the old flash step doesn't (PR #315 review). A re-read that a newer build, flash or shown build overtook is dropped, so its "gone" never hides the newer build (PR #315 CodeRabbit). Check the keyboard again, Restart Docker then build, and Open … settings keep the failure (`pageFailure`). Check the keyboard again goes when it would fail the same way, and Restart keyloom becomes primary. A restart request that fails that way isn't offered again.
- **A running install that keyloom lost track of** (R10-07). After three failed reads in a row (`LOST_AFTER`), the install says "keyloom lost track of the install, so it can't say how it is going." A lost connection or an exception keeps reading by itself and offers Check again. An answer the page can't read, or keyloom's catch-all 404, stops reading and offers Restart keyloom. Before, "Installing…" stayed on screen with nothing said. A start or cancel the server answers starts the count afresh, so reads that stopped never keep a new install from being followed; a lost read replaces a failed Cancel shown before it, and its own code picks Check again (`installTrack`, PR #315 CodeRabbit).
- **What macOS lists** (R10-10). The removable-volumes and Input Monitoring words say "the app running keyloom (the keyloom app, or your terminal when keyloom runs in one)". The flasher already said "the app running keyloom". Claude chose one sentence for both cases over reading restart info's `canRestart`, because the sentence is true either way and needs no second request. In the Build and flash dialog, a permission failure shows its plain lead, with the flasher's sentence, which names the System Settings path, under Details beside the button that opens the pane. The split board's recovery result stays in Details.
- **A ready Docker names its engine, from evidence only** (R10-06, PR #315 review). `dockerReadiness` asks `answeringEngine` when Docker answers, so a full disk on Docker Desktop shows Open Docker Desktop (D-549). It names an engine only from what the daemon says it is (`docker info`: an operating system of "Docker Desktop" or "OrbStack", a host named `colima`, `colima-<profile>` or `lima-rancher-desktop`), or from a context that names one engine exactly (`desktop-linux`, `orbstack`, `rancher-desktop`, `colima*`, or a socket only that engine makes). The shared `/var/run/docker.sock` and an installed app name nothing, because OrbStack or Colima can answer there while Docker Desktop is installed, and Open Docker Desktop would then open and start an app that doesn't run the daemon. Anything else, or a read that fails, stays "Docker". `findDockerEngine`, which picks what to start for a daemon that is down, keeps its guesses. A start that brings Docker up keeps a name only from a ready readiness, then asks readiness again, so the candidate it started is never taken as the engine that answers (PR #315 CodeRabbit). Claude chose to ask the daemon first because it is the only source that can't name the wrong engine. A start that found Docker already up keeps the engine readiness named. The dev editor's ready Docker names `KEYLOOM_FAKE_ENGINE`, or the restart engine, as the real one does now.

### D-554: A config kanata refuses says so, with Undo or the keyboard's status; kanata's checklist puts permissions after Start; a Mac with no built-in keyboard says so. Refines D-493, D-501, D-548 and D-549. (Claude, from the round-10 dead-ends review, R10-01, R10-03, R10-14)

- **What happened** (R10-01). The sync engine stores a kanata apply that fails at `check`, `reload` or `generate` as `failure: "build"`. The pill and the toast read that as a failed firmware build and offered Build and flash…, which does nothing on the MacBook, or Apply now, which fails the same way.
- **The apply carries a code; the sync does what it did.** `applyToRemapper` returns `kanata_unwritable` for a layout keyloom can't write for kanata. For a check or reload that kanata itself turned down (`applyKanata` marks it `refused`: a failed check, a reload answered no, or kanata's `Error` answer) it asks the setup doctor: a kanata that is missing, another version, or changed since setup is `kanata_setup`, and anything else is `kanata_refused`. A reload that failed any other way is `kanata_reload_failed`: keyloom put the previous file back, so the line says keyloom couldn't finish loading the config and offers Apply now (PR #315 review). A timeout never reaches this: it is `not_connected`. The engine stores the code in `syncs.error_code`, and the run view carries it. The failure, the error and every step of the sync are unchanged. Claude chose the doctor over a version check of its own, so the code names a cause exactly when the keyboard's status shows a step for it.
- **The words and the way on.** For `kanata_refused`, the pill and the toast say "kanata refused the config keyloom wrote, so your MacBook keyboard still runs the previous one." with kanata's reason under Details. For `kanata_setup`, they say the kanata on this Mac isn't the one keyloom writes for, so it didn't take the config; the toast offers **Open keyboard status**, and the pill's checklist below has the step (Update kanata, Install kanata, set up again), with **Apply now** as a plain second button for after it. A sync recorded before these codes says only "keyloom couldn't put this config on kanata", because its cause can't be known (PR #315 review). Never Build and flash, and never an Apply now that fails the same way.
- **One undo, only when one undo gets there** (PR #315 review). The engine counts the changes between kanata's running revision and the head (`changes`). **Undo this change** ("to go back to the layout kanata runs") shows only when that count is 1 and the refused change is the head (`undoReachesKanata`). With more changes, one undo would land on a layout kanata never checked, which may hold the same problem, so the line offers **Open History** and says to press Go back to this on the version marked "On your MacBook keyboard". With no count (the first apply after setup, or a running revision off the head's line), it offers Open History "to go back to an earlier version" and promises no layout kanata runs. A refused head that was itself an undo is gone back from by its redo, which is the same one step.
- **Product choice made without BK.** Undo where one press reaches the layout kanata runs; History, not a one-press go-back to kanata's revision, everywhere else. The page doesn't hold kanata's running revision, and History already marks it and has Go back to this, so no new field joins the sync view. History is also where the person sees what each step back holds.
- **Install kanata while kanata runs.** The Install kanata step now shows while kanata's service runs too, because keyloom checks every config with the kanata on the PATH.
- **Permissions after Start** (R10-03). The checklist runs kanata, the driver, Start kanata, then Allow kanata in Privacy & Security. While Start is still to do, the step says "After the Start kanata step above, turn kanata on…", because the copy to allow doesn't exist until that step makes it.
- **No built-in keyboard** (R10-14). `ioreg` exits 0 and prints nothing when no `AppleEmbeddedKeyboard` matches, as on a Mac mini. The doctor reports `keyboard_name` as `fail` with "this Mac has no built-in keyboard". The checklist then shows only the note "This Mac has no built-in keyboard": kanata has nothing to remap and needs no setup, keyloom offers to set up a keyboard it supports when one is plugged in, and the contribution guide covers any other. Install kanata, Start kanata and the permissions don't show, because they would lead a Mac mini through a password dialog for a remapper with nothing to remap (PR #315 review). Claude chose the note in place of the steps over the note first, because no step below it has a purpose there. A name keyloom doesn't know stays `warn` with the guide. An older page shows no step for `fail`.
- **Dev editor.** `KEYLOOM_FAKE_SYNC=kanata_refused|kanata_setup|kanata_unwritable|kanata_reload_failed|kanata_first_run|kanata_no_builtin|kanata_foreign`, `KEYLOOM_FAKE_SYNC_CHANGES` (a number, or `null`) for the count behind a refusal, and `KEYLOOM_FAKE_REPLACE=internal`.

## 2026-11-13 round 10: chat, the editor's panes, the lab and the review

### D-553: Settings, Combos, the tuner, the keycode field, the lab, the layout re-read toasts, the review's provider switch and a reply stopped by keyloom's own exception keep a failure's code. Extends D-534, D-540, D-546 and D-551. (Claude, from the round-10 dead-ends review, R10-07)

- **A failed Apply in Settings, Combos, the tuner and Repeat** keeps the failure (`applyProblem`, `editor/ApplyProblem.tsx`). Before, Settings, Combos and the tuner kept only `error.message`, so "Restarting keyloom opens the page that matches it." showed with no button, and Apply stayed on for an answer that fails the same way. Now the line shows the sentence with Restart keyloom, and the log for an exception. Apply stays on as the Try again for an exception or a lost connection. For an unreadable answer or keyloom's catch-all 404, Apply goes off (`failsAgain`) and Restart keyloom is the primary button. A layout deleted elsewhere turns Apply off and offers Show your other layouts (D-552). A refusal the validator explained still says only that its problems are listed.
- **The keycode field** keeps a failed read with its code (`readFailure`, `panel/AnyField.tsx`) and shows `RetryOrRestart`: Try again reads the same text again. Its words for a lost connection are its own: "keyloom stopped answering, so it couldn't read that. Try again once it is back." The shared sentence says "This page reconnects by itself", which was untrue here, because the field reads only when its text changes.
  - Rejected: reading the field again by itself when keyloom comes back. A button is simpler, and the person can see that it did something.
- **The typing lab.** keyloom's catch-all 404 is kept as the page's own code `unexpected_not_found` (`labProblemCode`). It gets keyloom's sentence and Restart keyloom, never "It may have been deleted in another tab", which was false. A failed Cancel that would fail the same way again is the page's own `cancel_stuck` (`labCancelCode`): "keyloom couldn't stop listening…" with Restart keyloom, which closes the keyboard's stream. Its Stop and discard button would send the same request again. Any other failed Cancel keeps `cancel_failed` and that button.
- **Show the new layout and Show the latest version** (`rereadToast`, `editor/reread-toast.ts`). A read that can pass again, such as after a lost connection, keeps the button that reads again. An unreadable answer or keyloom's catch-all gets Restart keyloom, which opens the restart dialog, with the read again as the dialog's Try again for an exception only. Before, these toasts offered the same read for ever.
- **The review's provider switch** keeps the failure (`switchFailure`) and shows `RetryOrRestart` in place of a Try again that was always there.
- **Chat's `agent_error`.** It is the code a saved run keeps for the same keyloom exception that a live stream calls `internal` (`runFailure`). It now has the same box: "The reply stopped", keyloom's own words, Try again, Restart keyloom, and **Show keyloom's log**. Before, it had Try again only. Both codes now show the log button, because their words say the details are in the log (D-541).
- **Product choice made without BK.** Apply turns off, not only Restart keyloom beside it, as D-551 did for the dialogs' confirms.
- **CodeRabbit fixes (PR #313).** A re-read toast whose layout was deleted elsewhere (the route's own `not_found`) says "keyloom no longer has this layout…" and offers Show your other layouts, never the read again. A lab fix's stored reason is the validator's words for the person (`plain`), with its message as the fallback.
- **Review fixes (PR #313).** A failed lab action clears a failed list load's `loadFailed`, and `labFixes` treats `unexpected_not_found` like `not_found` there, so a catch-all 404 on Start listening offers Restart keyloom at once, not a Try again that reloads the lists. The re-read toast's Restart keyloom, used only when the page has no other layouts to show, opens a dialog that keeps the failure's code (`not_found`, `generic`), so the dialog draws Restart keyloom and not Close alone.
- **Left as is: `keyloom chat` in a terminal.** A turn that hits keyloom's own exception there saves `agent_error`, and the exception goes to that terminal's stderr, not to the server log that Show keyloom's log opens. If that conversation is then opened in the web app, the log button shows a log without the exception, and Restart keyloom does not help. Try again still works, and never sends the draft twice. Fixing it means writing the CLI's exception to the server's log file. That belongs with the CLI's logging, not with this round.

### D-552: The other Forget buttons come back after one lands; a layout deleted elsewhere offers your other layouts; a version that is gone drops its promises; a repair request's recheck says what happened in plain words; a lab fix the checks refused says why. Refines D-535, D-546 and D-551. (Claude, from the round-10 dead-ends review, R10-02, R10-09, R10-11, R10-12)

- **Forget** (R10-02). One `removing` state turns every Forget button in a turn off while one runs. It was cleared only on a failure, so after one Forget landed the others stayed off with no reason until a reload. `forgetPriority` now clears it in `finally`.
- **A layout deleted elsewhere** (R10-09). The change check and Repeat said "Restarting keyloom opens the page on the layouts it has." with Restart keyloom alone. Under `keyloom serve` in a terminal, that means Ctrl-C and a command. The layout page now provides App's **Show your other layouts** through `OtherLayoutsContext` (`editor/LayoutGone.tsx`). The box says "keyloom no longer has this layout. It may have been deleted in another window, so this change can't be saved." with Show your other layouts as the primary button and Restart keyloom beside it. Outside a layout page, the old words and Restart keyloom stay. The release dialog keeps its own words, which round 10's sync worker owns.
- **A version that is gone** (R10-09). Compare's header drops "Going back to it changes what's marked on the board.", and its footer (New config from here, Put on your board, Go back to this) goes too, because each of those would fail the same way. Back to History stays the way on.
  - **Product choice made without BK:** the brief asked only for the header line. The footer goes too, because the bar says never to offer a flow that can't be completed.
- **A repair request's recheck** (R10-11). Before a revised-proposal request goes out, keyloom reads the layout and the proposal again. When that read failed, the panel said "Could not recheck the recovery source. Nothing was sent; your request is kept." That line stayed after a later send went through, and "recovery source" is jargon. Now each send clears it, and a failure says "keyloom couldn't check the proposal again, so nothing was sent. Your request is kept below." inside the request, with the failure's code. An exception adds keyloom's sentence, Restart keyloom and the log, and Send request stays the Try again. An answer that fails the same way turns Send request off, and Restart keyloom is the primary button.
- **A lab fix the checks refused** (R10-12). The analysis stores the validator's first reason, in its words for the person (`plain`, D-101), with a candidate it couldn't propose. The read-out now sends it as the suggestion's `problem` (optional in the wire schema, for older keyloom). The list and an opened finding say "keyloom's checks refused this fix on your layout as it is now, so it can't be applied." and then the reason. Trying it shows the same words, hides Apply and offers **Type again**. The words don't promise that typing again passes, because a fix that breaks a priority the person pinned is refused again.
- **Review fixes (PR #313).**
  - **A fix keyloom never proposed.** Only a refusal stores `problem`, so the lab says "refused" only when `problem` is a string. `keyloom lab analyse --no-propose` stores none, and its fixes said a check refused them when no check ran. They now say "keyloom didn't propose this fix when it read this recording, so there is nothing to apply yet." The read-out offers **Read it again with fixes** under the fixes, which runs the web analyse route; that route always proposes. A refused fix gets no such button, because a second read would refuse it the same way.
  - **A repair request whose conversation or layout is gone.** The recheck reads the conversation, then the layout (or the layout and its open proposals). The route's own `not_found` from those reads now means the request is out of date for good, like the out-of-date path: Send request goes off, and the request says what is gone: "keyloom no longer has the conversation this proposal came from…" or "keyloom no longer has this layout…". Dismiss request is the only button. There is no Dismiss and show the proposal, because the proposal went with its conversation. keyloom's catch-all 404 stays a failed recheck with Restart keyloom.
  - **Product choices made without BK:** a fix with no proposal and no refusal gets a button that reads the recording again with fixes, not only Type again, because the page can propose them itself. A gone conversation or layout is said inside the request, where its Send went off, and not in the notice above it.
- **Checked by:** unit tests for each piece, and screenshots of the change check with a deleted layout, light and dark, in `.orchestrate/keyloom-dead-ends/evidence/r10-a/`. The dev editor fakes no versions, lab sessions or repair requests, so Compare, the lab and the repair request were checked by rendering their markup in tests only.

## 2026-11-13 round 9: keyboards, configs, import and first run

### D-551: The config dialogs, the import card, the new-keyboard steps, the welcome's reads and the mismatch page keep a failure's code, and a button that would send a request that fails the same way again goes off. Extends D-534, D-540 and D-544. (Claude, from the round-9 dead-ends review, R9-05)

- **Every one of these screens keeps the failure** (`pageFailure`), and `wayOutFor` picks its buttons. Before, each kept only the message, so "Restarting keyloom opens the page that matches it." or "The details are in keyloom's log." showed with no Restart keyloom and no way to the log.
  - **The config dialogs** (`ConfigDialogs.tsx`). Put it on, Put a version on, Rename, New config and Delete show the sentence with `UnexpectedWayOut` beside it. A refusal that `problemFor` maps keeps its own words and button. New config's failed read of the versions uses `RetryOrRestart`.
  - **The import card** (`CardProblem`): a failed drop, Read it from this Mac, or Start blank.
  - **The new-keyboard steps** (`FlowProblem`): Add, Start blank and Make. `addProblem` now returns the failure with its code, and keeps the page's words for a refusal it maps.
  - **The welcome's two reads** (`ReadFailed`): keyloom's board list, whose failure `App.tsx` now passes down as `boardsFailure`, and the keyboard collection. A failure no screen planned for adds keyloom's sentence after the page's own, because that sentence explains the button. A lost connection keeps the page's sentence and a plain Try again.
  - **The mismatch page.** A refused Go back or Archive, and a failed look for an earlier revision that matched, keep their code. One row shows Restart keyloom, for the refusal first, then the look (`mismatchUnexpected`), so two failures never show two Restart buttons. Look again is offered only when a second look can pass (`lookupFailedWords`).
- **A button that repeats a request that fails the same way goes off** (`failsAgain`, `src/web/shell/unexpected.ts`). The answers this covers are keyloom's catch-all `not_found` and an `unreadable` answer. Restart keyloom beside the sentence becomes the primary button, and the sentence says why. This follows R9-04's rule for Apply on a proposal card.
  - **Off:** a dialog's confirm (Put it on, Rename, Make, Delete), and the new-keyboard step's one way forward (Add, Make, Start blank, Start a config).
  - **Not off:** the import card's drop zone, which takes a new file each time, and the mismatch page, whose several actions are separate requests. These keep their controls, as D-540's `FailureNote` does.
  - **The exception:** a dialog opened without a token, today only kanata's Replace it in `SyncStatus.tsx`, can't show Restart keyloom. So it keeps its confirm.
- **Rejected: keeping every button on**, as D-540's first screens did. A confirm pressed again after an `unreadable` answer fails the same way. The bar says never to offer a Try again that can't pass, and a dialog's confirm is its Try again.
- **Product choice made without BK.** Turning the confirm off, and not only adding Restart keyloom beside it, was decided in this round because BK was asleep. The reason is above.

### D-550: Later on the Put step doesn't bring the board's setup card back at once. Refines D-336 and D-544. (Claude, from the round-9 dead-ends review, R9-08)

- **What happened.** A keyboard keyloom hadn't met was plugged in, added, and left with Later on the Put step. Detection then read it as a known board with no config. Its board id wasn't among the cards already shown, because the first card was keyed by its USB identity. So "Just plugged in · Your {name} has no config yet" opened straight away.
- **The fix.** Later, or any close of the panel on the Put step, marks the board id as shown (`shownAfterClose`). The card that opens by itself is chosen by `cardToOpen`, now a pure function. It opens each keyboard and each bare board once.
- **Rejected: adding the board to `dismissed`,** as the round-9 brief suggested. `dismissed` also hides the board switch's chip for a known board with no config (`setupWaiting` in `Viewer.tsx`), so the editor would lose its one-press way back. With the board marked as shown, the chip stays. On first run, the welcome lists the board (D-544's read after Add), and Your keyboards has Set up.
- **Checked by:** unit tests of the sequence (card shown, Add, Later, the board known with no config, no card), and that another bare board still opens its own card once. It was not rendered: neither fixture fakes a USB keyboard that becomes known after Add.

## 2026-11-13 round 9: sync, flashing permissions, builds and Docker

### D-549: Docker's build-cache outcomes, a daemon that stops mid-build, kanata's keyboard name, and the remaining sync and Docker screens keep a failure's code. Refines D-488, D-493, D-540 and D-543. (Claude, from the round-9 dead-ends review, R9-05, R9-07, R9-11, R9-12)

- **Free Docker's build cache** (R9-07). A prune whose request itself failed keeps its code (`pageFailure`). An exception keeps the prune button, as its Try again, with Restart keyloom and Show keyloom's log beside it; an answer the page can't read, or keyloom's catch-all 404, drops the prune button for Restart keyloom. Neither says "make room another way", because the prune never ran. A prune Docker's daemon didn't answer is `docker_unavailable` (`pruneFailure`, from "Cannot connect to the Docker daemon"), and says "Docker isn't running, so its build cache wasn't freed. Build again starts Docker." Build again starts the engine (D-486); if the disk is still full, that build says so again with the prune. An empty cache or a refused prune on Docker Desktop names Docker Desktop's Settings, under Resources, and offers **Open Docker Desktop** before Build again. Other engines keep the general words: Colima's disk is set when it starts, which keyloom doesn't do for the person.
- **A daemon that stops during a build** (R9-11). `classifyBuildFailure` gives the drivers' daemon sentence `docker_unavailable` for QMK, ZMK and west builds alike, so Build and flash shows its Docker callout, with Start, and the sync pill says Docker isn't ready. The QMK sentence (and the lab's emulator) no longer says "Start Docker Desktop", which is wrong for Colima. The inventory said ZMK already had the code; at 64f5f731 neither did, because `classifyBuildFailure` dropped it.
- **kanata's keyboard name** (R9-12). The doctor's `keyboard_name` warning is a checklist step, "kanata can't find this Mac's keyboard", shown while kanata runs too, so the list never reads as done while kanata remaps nothing. keyloom can't learn the name by itself: the MacBook board has to name it. So the step links the contribution guide, and its words never name `kanata --list`.
- **The remaining screens keep a failure's code** (R9-05, this worker's part). kanata's checklist read and its steps, a Docker start, restart or Fix Colima's files, a tool install (Docker and Zapp; Codex's install screen is chat's and only needs to render `install.failure`), and a lab fix's Apply. Each keeps `pageFailure`, renders `UnexpectedWayOut` beside its own button, and hides a Try again that would fail the same way. `Docker`, `Install` and `SyncApi` carry the session token for it.
- **The dev editor fakes these** (`scripts/dev-editor.ts`): `KEYLOOM_FAKE_SYNC=files_and_folders|files_split|input_monitoring|tool_missing|kanata_keyboard`, `KEYLOOM_FAKE_OPEN_SETTINGS=fail`, `KEYLOOM_FAKE_PRUNE=daemon|internal` and `KEYLOOM_FAKE_ENGINE`.
- **The pane note follows the pane (PR #312 review).** "System Settings didn't open" shows only while the pill still offers that pane, so a sync that went through after the person allowed it by hand never keeps the red note.

### D-548: A sync whose flash macOS refused offers the settings that allow it, and a sync without its flash tool offers Build and flash. The sync row keeps both codes. Refines D-492, D-501 and D-542. (Claude, from the round-9 dead-ends review, R9-01, R9-10)

- **The permission fix on the pill, the needs-hand line and the toast** (R9-01). A sync whose flash failed `permission_files_and_folders` (a UF2 drive macOS wouldn't let keyloom write: the Glove80 and both Cornes), or that waits for or ended on a hand because of `permission_input_monitoring` (the Moonlander's restart), now shows the Build and flash dialog's fix. The line says `permissionFix`'s `failed` and its new `sync` words ("macOS didn't let keyloom write to the keyboard's drive, so that part wasn't flashed. Allow keyloom to use removable volumes, then sync again."), and its filled button is **Open Files and Folders settings** or **Open Input Monitoring settings**, which opens the pane (D-492). **Sync now** is the second, plain button, once the sync has ended. While a Moonlander still waits for its reset key, pressing the key is the way on, so no Sync now shows then. The toast carries the same words and the settings button, which opens the pane from any page, because System Settings is the computer's, not a page's. The flasher's sentence names the System Settings path the button opens, so the line shows it only behind Details, where it still says which half wasn't flashed. A pane that doesn't open says so in the popover, which stays open, and in a toast, with where the setting is: "Open System Settings, then Privacy & Security, then Files & Folders." Before, the pill offered Sync now or Try again, which failed the same way until the person found the setting.
- **The needs-hand code is on the sync row** (R9-01). Migration 0023 adds `syncs.instruction_code`. The engine writes the flasher's `problem` with its needs-hand instruction, keeps it when the sync ends as `needs_hand`, and clears it on every other step. `deriveState` passes it on as the `needs_hand` state's `instructionCode`, optional in the wire schema for older senders. A flash that ended on the code carries it as the run's `errorCode`, which the page reads too.
- **The reason is said once, in keyloom's case** (R9-01). The needs-hand line no longer appends "Keyloom couldn't restart it by itself." when the instruction already says why ("macOS didn't let keyloom…" now counts, as "couldn't" did). A reason that starts with keyloom's name keeps its lower case.
- **A write keyloom couldn't confirm** says so first on the pill, as the dialog does, so every flash code a sync can carry has its own words: `write_timeout` (D-543), both permissions, `not_confirmed`, and `flash_tool_missing` below. `cancelled` never reaches a failed sync's line.
- **A Moonlander sync without Zapp** (R9-10). `FlashToolMissingError` is thrown before a flash row exists, so the sync's flash outcome now carries `flash_tool_missing`, and migration 0023's `syncs.error_code` keeps it. The run view uses it when no build or flash row has a code. The pill and the toast say "The tool that flashes this keyboard isn't installed on this computer, so keyloom couldn't flash the change. Build and flash installs it." with **Build and flash…** on the sync's build, whose flash step has Install Zapp (D-489). Before, the pill showed "Install it with brew install zapp" with Sync now. The CLI keeps its sentence.
- **Unknown sync refusals** (R9-05, this worker's part). A sync request that fails `internal`, `unreadable` or keyloom's catch-all 404 shows "keyloom couldn't do that" with **Restart keyloom**, which opens the direct-edit toast's restart dialog (D-540), now with its own title. Other codes keep the sentence alone.
- Rejected: two toast buttons (settings and Sync now). The toast takes one action; the pill, one tap away, has both.

## 2026-11-13 round 9: the change check, the editor, chat and review

### D-547: Update layout that keyloom's check refuses says why in plain words and offers Hide this, not the same button again. Refines D-193. (Claude, from the round-9 dead-ends review, R9-09)

- **Why the button goes.** `upgradeLayout` checks the updated document against the head before it writes (D-193): it must parse, break no rule the old one kept, and still generate. The check runs on the same head every time, so a second press is refused the same way until the layout changes. Before, the button stayed, with the server's lower-case sentence under it ("the upgrade was not saved: the upgraded layout doesn't parse").
- **What shows.** The route's refusal is `409 validation_failed`, and the page reads that code only (`upgradeFailure`, `settings/upgrade-words.ts`). The offer then says "keyloom can't update this layout: the updated version didn't pass keyloom's checks, so nothing changed. Your Oryx colours and tap dances still go to the keyboard as they are." That last sentence is true because the colours and tap dances stay in the layout as Oryx code, which is passed through to the firmware until the update reads them. **Update layout** gives way to **Hide this**, which hides the offer for this version of the layout. The Viewer keeps the refusal, so the summary, Settings and a native `TD()` key's card agree. A change makes a new head, which the update may pass, so the offer comes back with its button after a change.
- **Rejected: Ask keyloom.** The agent can't make the update pass either, and asking would suggest it can. The CLI keeps its sentence, which names the rule that failed.
- **Any other failure** keeps its code (D-540): the sentence with Restart keyloom and the log beside it (`FailureNote`), and Update layout off when the answer fails the same way every time.

### D-546: The change check, a failed Stop, a proposal's Apply, the board review's read, History and the editor's toasts pick their buttons with D-540's rule. Extends D-524, D-534 and D-540. (Claude, from the round-9 dead-ends review, R9-02, R9-03, R9-04, R9-05, R9-06)

- **The change check** (R9-02). The shared check box (the key card, Settings, Combos, the tuner and Repeat) keeps the failure's code in `CheckState` (`checkFailed`, `editor/useCheck.ts`) and picks its buttons with `wayOutFor` (`CheckWayOut`). An exception gets **Check again**, **Restart keyloom** and the log. An answer this page can't read, or keyloom's catch-all 404, gets Restart keyloom alone. A lost connection keeps Check again alone. The route's own `not_found` is a layout deleted in another window: the box says "keyloom no longer has this layout…" (the words Build and flash uses) with Restart keyloom alone. Before, every failure showed only Check again, which fails the same way for all but a lost connection or an exception. A stale check is unchanged (D-543).
- **A failed Stop** (R9-03) is its own problem kind, `stop` (`stopProblem`). The box is titled "keyloom couldn't stop the reply", and its button is **Stop again**, with Restart keyloom and the log for an exception, or Restart keyloom alone for an answer that fails the same way. Before, it was titled "That message didn't go through", and its Try again sent whatever was in the box. The box goes when the reply ends, because nothing is left to stop.
- **Apply on a proposal card** (R9-04) is off after an answer that fails the same way every time (`proposalFailureWay(...).apply`). The failure's sentence and Restart keyloom beside it are the reason. An exception or a lost connection keeps Apply on as the card's Try again.
- **The board review's read** (R9-06) decides with `wayOutFor` (`reviewLoadProblem`), not by status. keyloom's catch-all 404 said "It hit an unexpected error." with a Try again that failed the same way. It now says keyloom's own words with Restart keyloom alone. An exception gets Try again, Restart keyloom and the log. A config deleted in another window (the route's own `not_found`) says History's "keyloom no longer has this config…" with Restart keyloom. No answer keeps Try again. The review's other requests keep D-524's rule, because a refusal there reads the review again, and that read now follows this one.
- **The editor's toasts** (R9-05). "Couldn't archive it", "Couldn't restore it", "Couldn't undo" and "Couldn't go back" keep their code (`shell/failed-toast.ts`). For a failure no screen planned for, the toast's button is **Restart keyloom**, which opens the direct-edit toast's dialog (D-540), titled with the toast's words, with Try again for an exception only. Any other failure keeps its sentence, and the control that failed stays on the page.
- **History** (R9-05). Compare keeps its failure (`compareFailure`): an exception gets Try again and Restart keyloom, keyloom's catch-all gets Restart keyloom alone, and a version that is gone says "keyloom no longer has this version…" with **Back to History**, which reads what keyloom has. Name a version and Repeat keep theirs: the sentence with Restart keyloom and the log beside it (`FailureNote`), and Save the name or Apply off when the answer fails the same way every time.
- **Shared pieces.** `UnexpectedWayOut` and `RetryOrRestart` take `retryLabel`, so the box says Check again or Stop again in place of Try again. The chat's failure box lays its buttons out in a wrapping row with gaps.
- **Dev editor.** `KEYLOOM_FAKE_UNEXPECTED=check=internal|gone,upgrade=refused,undo=internal` fakes these failures for screenshots.

- **Review fixes (PR #310 review).** Back to History reads History again, so a version that is gone leaves the list instead of failing again. A refused Update layout names only what its offer was for. A Stop whose cancel fails after the reply has ended shows nothing. The four copies of "fails the same way again" use `failsAgain` (D-551).
- **CodeRabbit fixes (PR #310).** Stop cancels a run the server holds even when this page's view of it has dropped. Repeat's Apply after the layout was deleted in another window says so, turns off and offers Restart keyloom, as the check does. A Go back retried from its toast closes Compare once it lands.

## 2026-11-13 round 8: keyboards, onboarding and the phone

### D-545: The new-keyboard drawing fits a phone's width and shows every finger, and Review promises a sync only for a board keyloom can add. Refines D-336, D-538 and 18's 44 px rule. (Claude, from the round-8 dead-ends review, R8-02, R8-07, R8-08)

- **The drawing fits the width** (R8-02). The Confirm, Review and Put steps draw the board with `Keyboard`'s new `fitMin`, set to `MIN_UNIT.preview` (16 px), not the pointer's 44 px or 34 px. Nothing on this board is a key the person edits: Confirm's home-row pills do what a tap on the board does, and Review and Put only show. On a 390 px phone a split board shows a whole half (the Moonlander and Glove80 halves fit at 28 to 29 px), and the MacBook's 17-unit deck shows whole at 17 px. Before, a half was cut at the right edge and `touch-action: pan-y` blocked a sideways pan. A test fits every shipped board, and each half, into the phone stage. Rejected: keeping 44 px and letting the half pan sideways. Panning hides the finger marks that Review exists to show, and the Left and Right switcher already pages between halves. A swipe on a half now switches it, as in the editor.
- **The stage's own room.** The new-keyboard well no longer takes the editor's 132 px and 172 px of room for its caption and floor bar, which nothing draws there. With a 16 px floor that room would have left a 136 px strip. At 1280 px a derived Lily58 now draws 743 px wide in the 800 px stage. Before, it was held at the 34 px floor and overflowed the well's height. On a phone the well is 300 px tall, not 440.
- **Review shows each finger.** A blank key hides its print (`keyboard.css`), so Review's finger words were never seen, on a desktop either. Review now gives each cap a key's tone, so "pinky", "ring" and the rest print on it. Confirm's caps stay blank.
- **A finger word is never cut** (PR #307 review). At 16 px a key is too small for "middle" or "thumb" at the 10 px legend floor: a 12-column one-piece board on a 390 px phone showed "midd" and "inde" on 40 of its 48 caps. When any finger word would be cut at the drawn size, every key on the board shows the word's first letter instead (P, R, M, I, T), and Review's lead says what each letter means. A mix of words and letters on one board would read as two kinds of key, so the choice is the board's, not each key's. Letters were chosen over three-letter forms ("mid", "pky") because a letter fits at 16 px and the lead explains it, and over `shortenToFit` because its "pin" and "thu" are not words. The key's accessible name keeps the whole finger ("left middle finger"). `Keyboard` takes the short form as `legendOverrides[i].short` and decides with `tapsFit` (`fit.ts`), measured across a turned key's upright print box. At 390 px the Cornes keep their words (41 px keys); the Moonlander and Glove80 halves, the MacBook and the 12 × 4 board show letters. At 1280 px every shipped board keeps its words except the Moonlander, whose turned thumb keys are 0.5 px too narrow for "thumb".
- **The new board's halves follow the new board** (PR #307 review). In the editor, a phone halved the new board when the open config's board was split, so a one-piece board plugged in beside a Glove80 config showed only its left hand's keys, and a split board beside the MacBook showed whole at a size that cut its words. `newBoardHalf` now decides from the new board's own shape, in the editor and the welcome alike.
- **One message for a board keyloom can't add** (R8-07). When Add is refused for good (`addBlocked`, D-538), Review hides the first-flash words. Before, the step said "keyloom can sync your {name} for you" directly above "keyloom can't add it yet".
- **The flash step keeps its case** (R8-08, `first-flash-words.ts`). Only a first word in plain case starts lower, so QK_BOOT, RESET, RST and GND stay as written. A step of more than one sentence, such as caterina's, follows "do this when it asks:" as written, with its own capitals and stops. Before, the whole step was lower-cased and "when it asks" was added after its last sentence. A pair of central and peripheral halves (a dongle build) reads as one sentence too, as a left and right pair does: "on each half, double-tap its reset button when it asks." Every recipe in `flash-recipe.ts` is tested through `firstFlashLine`.

### D-544: Your keyboards, the import screen and the new-keyboard Reading step offer Restart keyloom for a failure no screen planned for; a keyboard left with Later stays reachable; Not now on first run can be undone; the import intro is true per card. Extends D-534, D-538 and D-539. (Claude, from the round-8 dead-ends review, R8-03, R8-06, R8-15, R8-18)

- **Restart keyloom** (R8-03, this worker's three screens). Your keyboards, the import screen's board list and the new-keyboard Reading step keep the failure's code (`unexpectedCodeOf`: `internal`, `unreadable`, and `not_found` only when the server marked it `generic`). For these codes they render `UnexpectedWayOut`: Restart keyloom, with Try again only for `internal`. A route's own `not_found`, such as a keyboard that is gone, keeps the screen's own way on. Before, each showed "Restarting keyloom opens the page that matches it." with only a Try again that failed the same way, which D-534 forbids. The helper sits in `src/web/keyboards/` because the shared one in `src/web/shell/` was not on main when this was built. Merge them when it lands.
- **A keyboard left with Later** (R8-06). After a successful Add, the welcome reads keyloom's boards again, so a board left with Later is in the list below the search without a reload. In Your keyboards, a keyboard with no config (`configCount` 0) has **Set up**, which opens its setup card: put a config on it, import one, or start blank. Before, the row had no way in, and the only route back was to search for the board again.
- **Set up over the import screen** (PR #307 review). Set up in Your keyboards, pressed while the welcome showed its own import screen, closed the dialog and left the import screen in place, so the button seemed to do nothing. Set up now closes the welcome's import screen, so its setup card shows.
- **Try again while another action runs** (PR #307 review). In Your keyboards, the Try again beside Restart keyloom is off while a remove, restore, resume or download runs, as the plain Try again is (`UnexpectedWayOut`'s `retryDisabled`). The list read again after a remove or restore keeps its failure's code, and its words no longer promise a Try again that an unexpected failure doesn't offer.
- **Not now on first run** (R8-15). The welcome shows "Set up the keyboard you plugged in ({name})" under its search for each plugged-in keyboard keyloom hasn't met. Pressing it opens the keyboard's card again, as the editor's chip does. A plugged-in board keyloom knows is already a row in the list below.
- **The import intro** (R8-18, `importIntro`). With one card shown, the intro says where that card's layout comes from: "Export your layout from Oryx…" or "…from the Layout Editor…", "Drop the file from your ZMK config on its card." for a Corne, and "Read your Karabiner-Elements settings from this Mac…" for the MacBook, which also says nothing changes on the MacBook until a change is applied. With every card shown, it says each card says where its keyboard's layout comes from. Before, it told every person to export from the keyboard's own editor.

## 2026-11-13 round 8: sync, builds, Docker and Colima

### D-543: A full Docker disk has a button that frees Docker's build cache, a held Colima command still shows Colima's stop, and the smaller dead ends in flashing, checking and kanata's setup get their own words and buttons. Refines D-523, D-529, D-533 and D-536. (Claude, from the round-8 dead-ends review, R8-04, R8-05, R8-10, R8-11, R8-12, R8-13, R8-14, R8-17)

- **A full disk** (R8-04). "No space left on device" in a build's output, in its first probe, or in the Glove80's firmware image build (`ensureImage`, the one step that writes BuildKit's cache, PR #306 review), is the build code `disk_full`. Before, it had no code, so the step offered Build again, which filled the same disk. The step now says "Docker ran out of disk space, so the build stopped." and that freeing Docker's build cache makes room, removes nothing the person made, and makes the next build take longer. **Free Docker's build cache** calls `POST /api/system/docker/prune-build-cache`, which runs `docker builder prune -f`: BuildKit's cache only, never an image, container or volume. Claude chose the build cache alone because it is the one thing keyloom can remove without asking about the person's own images. The prune takes D-511's engine lock as `prune`.
