A start, a restart or Fix Colima's files refuses it as `engine_busy` and is refused by it, and `ensureDocker` makes a build wait for it, so no build starts while the cache goes. Inside the lock it asks `buildCachePruneBlocker`, which waits for the same work as D-529's `engineRestartBlocker`, and is refused as `build_running` while a build, flash, keyboard write, lab analysis, review test or emulator run is under way. A server without that check refuses every prune. The refusal has the prune's own words, never the forced restart's: "keyloom is running a lab analysis. Free Docker's build cache once it has finished." A prune refused as `engine_busy` says the same way what keyloom is doing with Docker ("keyloom is restarting Docker. Free Docker's build cache once that has finished."), because the step keeps the prune's button and has no Check again (PR #306 review). Readiness never reports a prune as its `engineAction`, because a prune runs only on a Docker that answers. The answer carries Docker's own total ("1.2GB"), and the page says "Freed 1.2GB of Docker's build cache. Build again to continue; it takes longer this time." Then Build again is the filled button and the prune button goes. A cache that was already empty, or a prune Docker refused, says to raise Docker's disk limit or remove images the person no longer uses, then build again, and keeps Build again. A refusal that ends by itself (`build_running`, `engine_busy`) or no answer keeps the button. Ask keyloom is not offered, because a full disk is not the layout's fault. The CLI's sentence names `docker builder prune` for QMK too, which named `docker system prune` before.
- **A Colima VM another account runs, while Colima's command is held** (R8-05). The page said "Stop that copy of Colima, then press Fix Colima's files again." with nothing that stops it. Readiness now carries `access.stopCommand`, Colima's stop on its own (`sudo env HOME="$HOME" colima stop`, with `--profile` for a profile that isn't the default). The profile comes from the Docker context's host path, which nothing checks, so a name outside Colima's own pattern (`PROFILE`) is single-quoted in every printed line, the stop and the full command, in `doctor` and on the page. Claude chose quoting over leaving the line out, so a person with an odd profile still gets a command that works (PR #306 review). It changes no file's owner, so the linked-file hold doesn't apply to it. When the fix answers `vm_running` while the full command is held, the note says to stop that copy with the command below, then press Fix Colima's files again, and the callout shows that command with Copy above the button. A terminal step is the only way here, because keyloom won't run Colima as root (D-533). The root script's steps are unchanged. An older server sends no stop command and keeps the old words.
- **`keyloom doctor` in the held case** (R8-10). The held sentence names Fix Colima's files, which is a button, not a command. `doctor` now adds that Fix Colima's files is in Build and flash, in `keyloom serve` or the desktop app, and prints the stop on its own for a copy started with sudo that still runs.
- **A Moonlander write that timed out** (R8-11) is the flash code `write_timeout`. The page says "Flashing didn't finish within 3 minutes, so keyloom stopped it." and that the keyboard may be left in its bootloader, with the step's Try again. A sync's run view carries its failed flash's code too, so the pill and the toast say the same with Sync now, not the flasher's sentence. The CLI keeps its sentence and its `--build` flag. Claude left out Go back to the last version that built here: a stalled write says nothing about the build, and that Go back exists only for builds that fail the same way every time.
- **A build whose file is gone at flash** (R8-12) is `BuildFileGoneError`, `409 build_file_gone` at the route and still a usage error (exit 2) to the CLI. The dialog says "This build's firmware file is gone, so keyloom can't flash it. Build again to make a new one." with **Build again**, and the flash step waits for the new build ("Available once you build again."), even over an earlier flash's result, so no button flashes the missing file again.
- **A check against a moved head** (R8-13). `checkChange`'s `stale_base` error gets `plain`: "This layout changed. Showing the latest version…", because the page reads the newer head when it hears of it. The key card shows **Show the latest version** beside it (D-524's pattern), which reads the layout now for when the event never comes, and its footer says "Waiting for the latest version." The CLI keeps the precise message. Other panes that check (combos, settings, the tuner) show the plain sentence without the button.
- **The hold picker's own layer** (R8-14). The tile stays off, and now says "This key's own layer" beside its name.
- **kanata's attention steps** (R8-17). They keep **Check again**. After Open settings, the list reads the machine again when keyloom's window is back in front (window `focus`, or the page becoming visible), once per pane opened. The listener is set before the pane opens, so a quick return isn't missed, and removed if the pane didn't open.
- **Show the latest version in the key card closes the card** (PR #306 review). The read remounts the card on the newer revision without its draft, and parking the draft alone left the card open on the clean key with the draft dashed under it. The card now closes, its draft waits on its key, dashed amber, and the toast says "The latest version is showing now. Your draft waits on its key unless that key changed. Open the key to go on with it." Opening the key resumes the draft against the new head, and a key that changed meanwhile drops it with its own toast. Claude chose closing over resuming into the remounted card, because a resume needs a second remount keyed on it, in the Viewer, which three rounds share.

### D-542: A sync whose build failed says what the build's code means and opens Build and flash on that build; the Build and flash dialog keeps its error's code. Refines D-510, D-523 and D-534. (Claude, from the round-8 dead-ends review, R8-01, R8-03)

- **The pill and the toast** (R8-01). A sync's error is the CLI's sentence, with commands, backticks and a log path. Only `docker_unavailable` was read before. The run view now carries `buildId`, and every build code a sync can carry maps through `buildFailureView` to its own line (`syncBuildFailureLine`): a stale mount or stuck container ("…Build and flash shows how to clear it."), a folder not shared ("…says where to share it."), a full disk ("…can free Docker's build cache."), a compile error, a firmware too big or a source error ("…Building it again fails the same way. Build and flash shows the error, and can ask keyloom about it."), Docker not ready, and any other failure ("The firmware build failed. Build and flash shows its log and can build it again."). The toast is "Couldn't build the change for your {board}" with the same line and **Build and flash…**. A build the person stopped says so and offers Sync now, with no button in the toast. A flash failure keeps its sentence with the backticks taken out.
- **Opens on the failed build.** The pill's button and the toast's button pass the sync's `buildId`, and the dialog reads that build and shows it (`showBuild`), so the restart, Go back, cache prune or question is there at once. Before, the dialog opened empty, and the person had to build and fail again to see them. It never replaces a build that runs, a flash under way or starting, or a newer build (`mayShowBuild`), and a build or flash the person starts ends any read still out. The dialog takes the build's board revision with it (`Build.boardRevision`), so Build again builds for the same revision with no keyboard plugged in. A read that fails names only the buttons its code gets: Try again (which reads it again) or Restart keyloom, with the step's Build firmware beside them. A build that is gone (`not_found`: its config was deleted, which deletes its builds) can't be read again and a restart won't bring it back, so the dialog says "The build that failed is no longer there, so keyloom can't show it. Build the firmware again to put this layout on the keyboard." with no button of its own, and the step's Build firmware is the way on. A failed read that something newer replaced says nothing (PR #306 CodeRabbit and review).
- **A sync of another config of the same keyboard** (PR #306 review). The person can move to config B while config A's sync builds. A toast about A on B's page offered Build and flash, which opened B's dialog on A's build, hid it as an older version and said to build again. The toast now treats another config like another keyboard: "…main's page has Build and flash." with **Open main**, whose pill opens Build and flash on that build.
- **The dialog's error keeps its code** (R8-03). `useRelease` dropped the code when a build or flash couldn't start. It now keeps it beside the sentence, and the dialog picks its buttons from it (`errorWayOut`): `internal` gets Try again and **Restart keyloom**, `not_found` and `unreadable` get Restart keyloom alone (D-534's `UnexpectedWayOut` as it is on main), `build_file_gone` gets Build again (D-543), and any other refusal keeps its sentence and Try again.
- **Dev editor.** `KEYLOOM_FAKE_SYNC=build_failed` gives the Moonlander a fake sync whose build fails with the `KEYLOOM_FAKE_FAIL_BUILD` mode; `KEYLOOM_FAKE_FAIL_BUILD=disk` and `KEYLOOM_FAKE_PRUNE` fake the full disk and the prune; `KEYLOOM_FAKE_COLIMA_SCAN=incomplete` holds Colima's command back.

## 2026-11-13 round 8: unexpected errors and the shell

### D-541: "The details are in keyloom's log." comes with a way to read the log, and Restart keyloom on a keyloom older than the page says how to restart it by hand. Extends D-521 and D-534. (Claude, from the round-8 dead-ends review, R8-09, R8-19)

- **The log has an address** (R8-09). The desktop app already writes the server's output to `~/Library/Logs/<identifier>/keyloom.log` (`log.rs`), and only its problem window could open it. Now the shell names that file to the server it starts in `KEYLOOM_LOG_FILE` (`sidecar.rs`). `GET /api/log` answers `{ kind: "file", path, canShow }`, or `{ kind: "terminal" }` when nothing named a file, because then the server writes to the terminal it runs in. `POST /api/log/show` opens the file with macOS `open`, which is Console for a `.log`, as the problem window's Show log does. It creates an empty file first if the app hasn't written one yet. Its refusals have codes: `log_in_terminal` and `log_unsupported` (409), and `log_open_failed` (500), whose sentence names the path. It runs `/usr/bin/open` by its absolute path, as `log.rs` does, because the desktop starts the server with the login shell's PATH, where another `open` could come first (PR #305 review). A read of where the log is that fails for a reason a second read can pass (a lost connection, an exception) says "keyloom couldn't say where its log is." with Try again.
- **Beside an exception** (`internal`, the only words that name the log): in the desktop app, **Show keyloom's log**. In a browser, the same button, because the server opens the file on the same Mac, and "keyloom's log is this file:" with the path and Copy. Off macOS, the path and Copy only. A server started in a terminal says "keyloom's log is the terminal it runs in." An older keyloom without the route adds nothing, and the Restart keyloom beside it says what to do.
- **Why a route and an environment variable, not Tauri's IPC.** The main window loads keyloom's page from the server, not from the app, so giving it Tauri commands would mean opening IPC to a remote origin. The server already opens Finder for a build's file the same way (`revealBuildArtifact`), and the shell owns the path, so no second copy of Tauri's log-folder rule is needed. The variable is an optional input, so the desktop protocol stays at 1: the app and its server ship together, and an older server ignores it.
- **An older keyloom behind a newer page** (R8-19). `GET /api/restart` answered by keyloom's catch-all (or a bare 404 from an older keyloom) means the route doesn't exist there. An answer this page can't read means the two are different versions. Both are now `mismatch`: "This keyloom is older than this page, so the page can't restart it." (or "…are different versions…"), then, in the desktop app, "Choose Quit keyloom in the menu bar, then open keyloom again."; in a browser, both that and "In a terminal, stop keyloom with Ctrl-C, then run:" with `keyloom serve` and Copy. There is no Try again, because reading again fails the same way. The same applies when the POST meets that keyloom. A lost connection or an exception keeps "keyloom couldn't say how it was started." with Try again.

### D-540: Every screen listed below keeps a failure's code, so an answer that fails the same way every time offers Restart keyloom, never only Try again. Extends D-534. (Claude, from the round-8 dead-ends review, R8-03, R8-16; wording narrowed in round 9)

- **What the rule covers after round 9.** Round 8: Build and flash, History's read, Your keyboards, the import screen's board list, the new-keyboard Reading step, the chat's Conversations list, What keyloom remembers, the chat composer and its smaller paths, a proposal card, and the direct-edit toast. Round 9, the change check, editor and chat PR (D-546, D-547): the change check in the key card, Settings, Combos, the tuner and Repeat; a failed Stop; Apply on a proposal card; the board review's read; the editor's archive, restore, undo and go-back toasts; History's compare and Name a version; Repeat's Apply; and Update layout. Round 9's other two PRs cover their own screens in their own entries: Docker's start and install, unknown sync refusals, kanata's checklist read and the lab's fix apply (sync and Docker); the config dialogs, the import card's actions, the new-keyboard Add, Start blank and Make, the welcome's reads and the mismatch page (keyboards and configs). A screen not named here or in those entries is not yet covered.

- **One shared shape** (R8-03). `pageFailure(error, fallback)` (`src/web/shell/unexpected.ts`) keeps `{ message, code, generic }` from a caught error. `wayOutFor(failure, repeatable)` picks the buttons: Try again and Restart keyloom for `internal`, Restart keyloom alone for `unreadable` and keyloom's catch-all `not_found` (`generic`). A route's own `not_found` (something deleted) stays the screen's, so its words and buttons apply (D-534). `<UnexpectedWayOut failure>` renders those buttons, and `<RetryOrRestart>` replaces a bare Try again under a failed read. The other round-8 screens (Build and flash, Your keyboards, import, the new-keyboard steps) use the same pieces.
- **This worker's screens.** The chat's Conversations list and its failed Open, What keyloom remembers and each decision's Edit, Retire and Conversation failures. A decision's own button stays the Try again; the row adds Restart keyloom where repeating can't pass.
- **The chat composer and its smaller paths** (PR #305 review). The composer's failure box (`ProblemNotice`) keeps the code (`classify` keeps `pageFailure`): an exception gets Try again, when the message is back in the box, with Restart keyloom and the log; an unreadable answer or keyloom's catch-all gets Restart keyloom alone. A provider switch that failed does the same (`RetryOrRestart`; saving the choice again is safe). A failed forget, outcome answer or model choice keeps the control that failed as its Try again and adds Restart keyloom beside the sentence (`FailureNote`). A proposal card keeps Apply, or Read proposals again for a closed proposal, unless the last answer fails the same way every time, and adds Restart keyloom (`proposalFailureWay`). Reading the proposals again rejects with its code (`failureError`), not a bare `Error`. keyloom's catch-all on Apply is a version mismatch, not "This proposal is no longer open."
- **One rule on every round-8 screen.** Build and flash, History, Your keyboards, the import screen and the new-keyboard Reading step decide with `pageFailure` and `wayOutFor`, so the rule lives in `src/web/shell/unexpected.ts`; `unexpectedCodeOf` is gone. A route's own `not_found` stays the screen's, with the screen's own way on: Build again for a flash whose build is gone (`build_file_gone`), Restore and Try again in Your keyboards. Where nothing on the screen can bring the thing back (a layout deleted under Build and flash, a config deleted under History), the screen says so in its own words ("keyloom no longer has this layout/config. It may have been deleted in another window.") with Restart keyloom alone, which opens the page on what keyloom has. This narrows D-542's "`not_found` and `unreadable` get Restart keyloom alone": that holds for keyloom's catch-all `not_found`, and a route's own `not_found` follows this bullet. The `code`-only form of `UnexpectedWayOut`, which gives every `not_found` Restart keyloom alone, stays in three places: the app's layout list, read through `getJson` (`api.ts`), which can't tell the catch-all from a route's own 404; and the key card's Apply and the editor's "Couldn't read proposals", whose own `not_found` means the layout is gone, so Restart keyloom alone is already the screen's way on.
- **The direct-edit toast.** A board change refused with an unexpected code shows its toast with **Restart keyloom**. A toast closes after 12 seconds, and Restart keyloom has states that must stay on screen (the line to run in a terminal, the wait), so the action opens a small dialog with the sentence, Close, Restart keyloom, Try again for an exception (it applies the same change again), and the log (D-541). That Try again also does what the edit does once it lands, such as ending a Quick assign run or selecting a new layer (`perform`'s `onSuccess`).
- **Set up a model while the status is being read** (R8-16). ModelSetup takes `reading`. With no status yet and a read on its way, it says "keyloom is still reading which models are set up." and opens setup when the read ends. Only a read that ended without a status shows "keyloom couldn't read which models are set up." with Try again. The review's Set up a model no longer starts a second read while one runs.

## 2026-11-12 round 7: builds, Docker restart, editor and sync

### D-537: "Applied, not shown" stays in the editor until a read works, Disable remapping has a refusal code, and the phone's sync popover keeps its gutter. Extends D-530. (Claude, from the round-7 dead-ends review, R7-06, R7-08, R7-16)

- **A quiet line, not only a toast** (R7-06). D-530's toast, "Applied. keyloom couldn't show the new layout yet.", closes by itself after 12 seconds. The board then showed the old revision with no mark. Now the editor also shows "Not showing the latest version." with **Show it** in the stage's notes. The line stays while the page still shows the revision it showed when the read failed (`notShownLine`, `src/web/editor/not-shown.ts`). Any read that works shows a newer revision, because the write moved the head, or another config, so the line goes then. Show it reads again; a failed read keeps the line and shows the toast again. A review proposal applied but not shown marks the line too, beside the panel's own recovery. The stage's notes now sit above the caption (z-index 7, was 3): the caption's box spans the stage on a wide window, so it took the clicks meant for a banner's button. That fixes the same trap for the swap, combo and place banners. On a stage 900 px wide or narrower, the notes sit at the bottom, above the whole floor: the layer strip, and the view controls a row above it between 601 and 900 px. Lower down, at z-index 7, they covered the layer tabs and the view controls (PR #303 review).
- **Disable remapping refused** (R7-08). A kanata that doesn't take the plain map is `SyncRefusal("disable_failed")`. The page says "keyloom couldn't turn remapping off", that kanata didn't take the change, and that Left Control, Space and Escape stop a stuck kanata, with **Open keyboard status**. The CLI keeps its sentence. The page never shows the CLI's sentence for this case. Other uncoded refusals still fall back to "keyloom couldn't do that" with the server's sentence; the round-7 review found none of them reachable from the page.
- **The sync popover on a phone** (R7-16). At 520 px or less, the popover is fixed to the viewport with 16 px gutters and opens under the pill. Before, it was 300 px wide, right-aligned to a pill near the right edge, and ran off the left edge at 390 px.

### D-536: A build that fails on the engine's file sharing says where to share it, a forced restart waits for running work and shows its progress, and a failed restart points at Build again. Refines D-511, D-523 and D-529. (Claude, from the round-7 dead-ends review, R7-02, R7-03, R7-04, R7-12)

- **Two new build codes** (R7-02). `folder_not_shared`: Docker mounted an empty folder for keyloom's build checkout, because the engine doesn't share it. The QMK, Glove80 and `west` drivers raise it from the probe they already ran, when its sentinel test exits 86 (`NOT_SHARED_EXIT`), and when Docker Desktop refuses the mount. Docker Desktop refuses a path outside its File sharing list before the container starts: `docker run` exits 125 with "Mounts denied: … is not shared from the host". So a probe that fails with output matching `/Mounts denied|is not shared from the host/i` is `folder_not_shared` too, and its CLI sentence names Docker Desktop's Settings > Resources > File sharing, as QMK's did before (PR #303 review). A cleanup after the test that failed exits 87 and says keyloom couldn't clear the last build's output. Docker's own failure, such as a container it couldn't create, names its exit. Both have no code and keep Build again (PR #303 CodeRabbit). The build view carries `folderNotShared`: the checkout folder and the engine (`forcedRestartEngine`'s name, or null). The step says "Docker can't see the folder keyloom builds in ({folder}), so the build stopped before it started." For Docker Desktop it says where file sharing is and offers **Open Docker Desktop** (the `docker_desktop` entry in the settings list, which opens `/Applications/Docker.app`). Docker documents no link to its File sharing page, so the words name it. For Colima it offers **Open Colima's guide to mounts** (colima.run/docs/faq, "Docker bind mount showing empty?", checked 2026-11-12). Build again stays beside them, because the build passes once the folder is shared. The page never shows the build's sentence, which names `XDG_CACHE_HOME`; the CLI keeps it. `source_error`: keyloom can't write the layout as the firmware's source. It joins `DETERMINISTIC_BUILD_CODES`, so it gets D-523's `lastBuilt`, **Go back to the last version that built**, **Ask keyloom about this build** and no Build again, and the CLI adds `keyloom version restore`.
- **A forced restart waits for running work** (R7-04). After a `build_running` or `engine_busy` refusal, the page reads `GET /api/system/docker/restart`. Its `waitingFor` names the work a forced restart would wait for (`engineRestartWork`: "a build", "a lab analysis", "a keyboard write", and "a Docker start" or "a Docker restart" while keyloom holds the engine). While that is not empty, the step says "Restarts Colima when the lab analysis ends, then builds again." with **Cancel restart**, and asks again every 5 seconds. Once nothing is named, it asks for the restart. The restart still asks `engineRestartBlocker` inside the engine lock, right before the stop, so a request that comes early is refused again and the wait goes on. D-529's guards are unchanged: a restart never stops a build, a flash, a keyboard write or a container run. A refusal with nothing named (an older server, or work that ended between the refusal and the read) shows its reason with the button, as before, so the page never loops. After 30 minutes the page stops waiting and offers the button again. A build asked for while it waits, Cancel restart, or closing the dialog ends the wait, and the page checks for each after every read and before every restart request. Closing ends it because nothing on the page would show a restart that started later (PR #303 review). A restart already sent finishes, and builds nothing if the dialog closed meanwhile; a failure still shows when the dialog opens again, since it may leave the engine down (PR #303 CodeRabbit). The restart builds only the revision the person pressed it for: once the dialog shows another revision, it stops waiting, or, after a restart that went through, builds nothing, and the dialog offers Build firmware for the revision it shows. A known limit: when two tabs both wait on the same work, the first restarts and builds, and the second then waits for that build and restarts the engine again after it. The guards still hold, so only engine time is lost. A fix needs a restart count from the server that the wait compares, and was left out of this round.
- **The restart's progress** (R7-04). The system backend keeps the latest forced request's progress lines (the last 30), from the same `onProgress` the CLI prints. A refused request has none. The same GET returns them with `running`. The page reads them every second while the restart runs, once more when it ends, and shows them in the step's log tail as "Restart log", kept after a failure. There is no Cancel while the restart runs: stopping `colima stop --force` or a Mac app's quit halfway would leave the engine down.
- **A restart that failed** (R7-03). The words are "keyloom couldn't restart {engine}. Build again starts {engine} if it isn't running.", because a build starts an engine that is down itself (`ensureDocker`). Before, they said to restart it yourself. The page reads Docker again after the failure, and after a restart request that got no answer. When Docker doesn't answer, the step shows the Docker callout, whose **Start it now** opens the Mac app or starts Colima.
- **The flash confirm** (R7-12) says keyloom "asks you to put it in its bootloader when it can't". The Glove80 has no reset key: its bootloader is a key combination at power-on.
- **Fix Colima's files is waited for too** (merge with D-533). A running fix holds the engine lock, so a forced restart asked for meanwhile is refused as `engine_busy`. The restart's wait names it ("Fix Colima's files") like a start or a restart, and restarts once it ends.

## 2026-11-12 round 7: keyboards and onboarding

### D-539: The MacBook's onboarding talks about kanata, not flashing, and Import from a board's setup opens that board's card. Refines D-336, D-476 and D-531. (Claude, from the round-7 dead-ends review, R7-13)

- **Words by firmware** (`src/web/keyboards/onboarding-words.ts`). With the MacBook picked, the welcome says "keyloom remaps your MacBook's own keyboard through kanata. Setup changes nothing on this Mac." Before a board is picked, it says "Connect a keyboard or choose it below. Setup never changes a keyboard." A picked USB keyboard keeps "Setup never flashes it."
- **The setup card.** For the MacBook, the lead ends "Nothing changes on your MacBook until you apply a change." The footer says "Nothing changes on your MacBook from here. Your changes reach it through kanata, which keyloom helps you set up." It does not say keyloom loads a change by itself: on a first run nothing reaches kanata until kanata is set up and the person puts the change on. Its eyebrow has a laptop icon. A USB keyboard plugged in keeps "keyloom only reads the keyboard. Nothing is written to it." One picked from the list says "Nothing is written to the keyboard from here.", because keyloom never read it.
- **Import opens that board's card.** The setup card's Import a layout, the known-board step's Import one, and the Put step's Import an existing keymap instead open the import screen with only that board's card (`ImportScreen`'s `only`, `cardsFor`). "Your file is for another keyboard? Show every keyboard" brings back every card. An id the list doesn't have shows every card. The editor's setup card does the same through `onImportFor`. The MacBook's card alone has no activity tests, so they are not offered.
- **A zmk-config card names the board's own file.** `/api/boards` sends `keymapFile` (`config/<keymapName>.keymap`, from the board's `zmk-west-docker` recipe). The Keebart Corne Choc Pro BT's card says "Drop config/corne_choc_pro.keymap from your zmk-config repository.", which is the file in Keebart's zmk-config at the pinned SHA. A board without one says "the config folder of your zmk-config repository".

### D-538: The first-run keyboard steps offer only what can be done, and a derived board that fails its check is not offered Add again. Refines D-525, D-531 and D-532. (Claude, from the round-7 dead-ends review, R7-10, R7-11)

- **A known board with no config to put on it** (R7-10). When the keyboard picked as new is one keyloom has (`board_known`) and no config on another board exists, as on a first run, the step says "keyloom knows this keyboard, so there is nothing to add. Start a config for it, or import one." It offers **Start a config**, which makes a config on that board and opens it, **Import one**, and **Pick another keyboard**. A config on the same board doesn't count, because a put can't copy onto its own board. A refused start on a removed keyboard offers Restore.
- **The Put step with no configs** (R7-10) is titled "Start a config for the {board}". It has no empty config list and no footer Make button, so a disabled "Pick a config" never shows. Start a config in the body is the way on.
- **`board_check_failed`** (R7-11). `keyboard add` throws `KeyboardAddError("board_check_failed")` when the derived board fails `boards check`, and the server sends the code. The check reads the same pinned files, so the same answers fail it again. The Review step then shows "keyloom drew the {name}, but the board it made fails the checks every keyboard in keyloom must pass, so keyloom can't add it yet." with **Open the contribution guide** and **Pick another keyboard**, and no Add. Back stays, and other answers (another home row) are offered Add again. Any other blocked derive code that Add returns is shown the same way. The CLI keeps its sentence.
- **Limit.** The check reads from the cache that the read before it filled. If that cache can't be written and GitHub then fails, the check's error is a network one that is still sent as `board_check_failed`. This needs an unwritable cache and a failed second fetch together, so it is recorded, not split.

## 2026-11-12 round 7: unexpected errors, lab, review and chat

### D-535: The lab's capture refusals, the review's unread model list and an out-of-date proposal request each end at a button. Refines D-527 and D-528. (Claude, from the round-7 dead-ends review, R7-05, R7-07, R7-14)

- **Lab refusals** (R7-07). `not_connected` says "keyloom can't find your {board}. Plug it into this computer with a USB cable, then try again." with **Try again**, which starts the same passage. `permission_denied` (Linux, Windows, and a ZMK board's serial port on a Mac; a Mac's raw HID is still `permission_input_monitoring`) says "Your computer didn't let keyloom read your {board}." followed by the server's platform step, which names no command, with **Try again**. There is no settings pane keyloom could open for these: on a Mac, `accessRefused` (`src/app/lab.ts`) already turns a refused QMK stream into `permission_input_monitoring` with **Open Input Monitoring settings**, so a Mac's `permission_denied` is only a ZMK board's serial port, which no System Settings pane controls. A Stop that finds no recording running, because another tab's Stop or Cancel or a closed lab ended it, was a 400 `usage` "no recording is running". It is now 409 `not_recording`. The page says the passage typed was lost and offers **Start listening again**. A session another tab deleted (`not_found`) offers **Read the sessions again**.
- **The review's provider status** (R7-05). `useProviderStatus` reports a failed read (`failed`, kept while Check again reads, so the words don't flicker) and a read in progress (`reading`). After a Codex usage limit with the status unread, the review says "keyloom couldn't read which models are set up, so it can't offer another one yet." with **Check again** beside **Try again**. Chat still leaves its provider line out on a failed read, because there it is context.
- **The usage-limit words name the button the panel shows.** With another model ready, the words say "Switch to another model" and the buttons are **Switch to {model}**. With none ready, they say "Set up another model" and the button is **Set up another model**.
- **An out-of-date revised-proposal request** (R7-14). The words were "select its recovery action again", with only **Dismiss request**, and the action lived on a card the page didn't show. Now the request offers **Dismiss and show the proposal**. Opening the proposal's conversation would stop the page following a reply, so while keyloom replies the button is off, with "keyloom is replying. Show the proposal once the reply ends, or stop the reply first." beside it, as Send and New are off. A request from a chat card opens that conversation first, and keeps the request with the reason if the conversation can't be read. A request from the open proposals opens that list. A request that the Send found out of date gets the same words and button, not "Check the request before sending."

### D-534: A failure no screen planned for has a code, keyloom's own words and Restart keyloom; it never shows "keyloom answered 500." or "internal error". (Claude, from the round-7 dead-ends review, R7-01, R7-09, and the round-7 sweep's History 404)

- **The server codes every body.** `respond` and `notFound` in `src/server/start-server.ts` send `{ error: { code: "internal" | "not_found", message } }`, as every route file's `errorResponse(500, "internal", …)` already did. The layout list, the layout detail, every editor route and an unknown `/api/` address now answer that way. The server's detail still goes to its log only. The catch-all `notFound()` also sends `generic: true`, because its "not found" is no sentence for a person.
- **The page maps the code once.** `failure()` in `api-write.ts`, which every other API client calls, and `getJson` in `api.ts` read the code through `src/web/shell/unexpected.ts`. `internal` says "keyloom hit a problem it didn't expect. The details are in keyloom's log." Every other code keeps the server's sentence for its screen to map, as before, and that includes a route's own `not_found`, such as "This build has no firmware file. Build it again." The page uses its own words only when the server sent none: a body with no code, or the catch-all's `generic` one. Then a 404 is `not_found`, which says what was asked for is gone or the page is newer than keyloom. A bare 413 is `too_large`, because Bun refuses a body over the 8 MB cap before any route runs. A 5xx is `internal`, and any other refusal is `unreadable`. A layout read's 404 keeps its own "keyloom has no layout here" with **Show your other layouts**.
- **A coded `not_found` gets a way on that works** (#301 review). The manual flash step's Download and Show in Finder say "This build has no firmware file. Build it again." with **Build again**. Forgetting a priority or retiring a decision that is already gone takes it off the list (`removeOrGone`). Only a route's own `not_found` counts as gone: the catch-all's `generic` 404, or a 404 with no code, means this keyloom has no such address (an older keyloom behind a newer page), so the row stays and the error shows. Your keyboards' Remove and Restore for a keyboard keyloom no longer has offer Try again as a fresh read of the list, not the same request. A file over the cap on Import says "That file is too large to be a layout export." (`IMPORT_TOO_LARGE`), whether Bun or the route refused it, and the person drops another file.
- **An answer the page can't read is `unreadable`** (R7-09). It was "… Restart keyloom and reload." or "Reload the page or update keyloom.", with no button, and the desktop window has no reload. The answer is the same until keyloom and the page match, so it offers only **Restart keyloom**. That covers `request()`, the layout reads, the model setup's own requests and a chat frame the page can't read.
- **The buttons** (`UnexpectedWayOut`). **Try again** where the caller says repeating is safe and the code is `internal`, then **Restart keyloom** (`RestartKeyloom`, D-521) in every case. A missing thing or an unreadable answer fails the same way every time, so it never gets Try again. Wired into the layout list and layout page (`App.tsx`), History, the key card's Apply (Apply is its Try again: it carries the base revision, so a repeat after a write that landed is refused as stale), the model setup, the chat failure box (`internal`: Try again and Restart keyloom; `unreadable`: Restart keyloom only) and the lab (`internal` and `unreadable`: Restart keyloom, with Start listening still on the card; a failed load keeps Try again).
- **History's 404** (round-7 sweep). A History read that a real server answers 404, such as an older keyloom behind a newer page, said "keyloom answered 404." with a Try again that could never pass. It now says the `not_found` words with **Restart keyloom** and no Try again. An `internal` History read keeps Try again and adds Restart keyloom.
- **Open {config} from a wall tile that couldn't be read reads it again.** Before, the page cleared the failed read and kept the same `selectedId`, so no read started and the page stayed on "Loading layout" with no button (found while screenshotting this round, and on main at 92b38ba0). It now reads the layout again, and a failure shows its state with its buttons.
- **The open-proposals line.** A proposals read that answers `not_found` or `unreadable` says "Couldn't read proposals." with **Restart keyloom**, not a Try again. The review panel's and the proposals panel's own Read again stay as they were.
- **Left alone.** Screens that already map `internal` keep their words (the review's "It hit an unexpected error", the put step). A direct edit from the board or tabs (`useChange`) shows the new words in its toast, with no restart button.

## 2026-11-12 round 6: editor, builds, Docker and sync

### D-533: A Colima whose files belong to another account gets Fix Colima's files, through macOS's password dialog. Replaces D-499's "Colima keeps its copy-the-command fix, because that needs sudo". (BK, 2026-11-12, from the round-6 dead-ends review, R6-26)

- **The button.** When Docker refuses this account and the engine is a Colima on a Mac, readiness carries `access.fixFiles: true` and the page shows **Fix Colima's files** with "Colima's files belong to another account, usually because Colima was started with sudo. Fix Colima's files gives the files back to you and restarts Colima as you. macOS asks for your password first." Build says "Fix Colima's files first." Linux keeps the docker group command, and the Mac apps keep Restart {app} (D-499).
- **The same steps as the command it replaces, split by who runs them, and nothing the person can write runs as root** (#300 review). `POST /api/system/docker/fix-colima-files` (body `{}`) asks Docker again and does nothing unless it still refuses a Colima on a Mac. It then runs one root script through `osascript … with administrator privileges`, as kanata's setup does (D-493). The script runs only `/usr/bin`, `/bin` and `/usr/sbin` tools by absolute path, with `PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`, and never `colima`: the first version ran the person's `colima stop` as root with Homebrew's folder on PATH, and the person's account (or any admin) can replace `colima`, `limactl` or anything they run, which then runs as root at the next press. That root stop usually had nothing to stop: Homebrew's `limactl` refuses to run as root ("limactl: must not run as the root user"), so a `sudo colima start` mostly leaves root's files and no VM. If a VM is running from `~/.colima` as another account (a `limactl` whose ID is in `_lima/<instance>/ha.pid`, owned by another uid), the script stops with `colima_vm_running` before it changes anything: "A copy of Colima started as an administrator is still running, and keyloom won't run Colima as one to stop it, so nothing changed." keyloom makes the same check without root before it opens the password dialog (`colimaFilesPlan`, through the `hostAgentPids` and `processOf` probes; PR #300 CodeRabbit), so the person isn't asked for a password for a fix that would stop there; a pid file it can't read or that holds no number is passed over, as in the script, and the script's check stays the guard, because what runs can change while the dialog is open. Both reach the page as `vm_running`, which says the reason and "The command below stops that copy, gives the files back and starts Colima as you.", takes the button away and keeps the command to copy, whose `sudo … colima stop` stops it. After the root script, `colima stop` (with `--profile` when the context names one) runs as the person, then `colima start`, through the usual engine start, and keyloom waits for Docker: a copy of the person's that is still up keeps the socket root made, and a start of a running Colima does nothing. The stop runs after the dialog, not before it, so a closed dialog still changes nothing, and as the person it can read the files root just gave back. A stop that fails is logged and the start still runs. Nothing in the script comes from the page: the path comes from the account record, it is single-quoted, the uid is a number, and a profile with any character outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]` is refused before the dialog.
- **Handing files to the person as root, with no name the person can swap** (#300 review). The command it replaces ran `chown -R "$USER" ~/.colima`. The script is narrower, because a mistake there gives away more than Colima's files. `~/.colima` must be a folder and not a link; the script moves into it with `cd -P` and checks that `pwd -P` is its real path before it looks at anything, then walks `.` from inside it, because BSD `find` runs `-execdir` for its starting point from find's own folder (checked on macOS 27: from `/` it named `.colima` in `/`). `find -P -xdev` never follows a link or leaves the disk; `chown -h` changes a link itself; `-execdir` runs each change from the file's own folder, with `./` before the name so a name that starts with `-` is not read as an option. The first version looked at each file (`-links 1`, `! -user`) and then changed it by name in a separate `chown`. A process running as the person could rename the file away in that window and hard-link one of macOS's own files in its place (macOS has no protected-hardlinks rule; an unprivileged `ln` to a root file on the Data volume works), and root's `chown` would give that file to the person. So the change now runs in three steps, and no name is changed while the person can change it: (1) every folder goes to root, top down (`chown -h 0`, `chmod -N`, `chmod go-w`), so each folder's parent is already root's, without group or other write or an ACL, when the folder itself is changed; the shell checks that before it moves into the folder (#300 third review). Only folders go to root: root's `chown` keeps a set-user-ID bit, so a person's set-user-ID file given to root would run as root. (2) Each file with one name that isn't the person's goes to them, from its own folder, once that folder is checked. In a folder the person can't write, a name can't change between `find`'s look and `chown`, and a file with one name, found inside `~/.colima`, has no name elsewhere. Giving a file to the person can't give them more than the person. (3) Folders go back to the person bottom up (`find -depth`), each from a parent the same shell has checked is still root's and recorded (#300 third review), and a folder that had group or other write gets its whole old mode back (`%Mp%Lp`, so a sticky, setuid or setgid bit comes back too; `%Lp` alone turned a 1777 folder into 777). Only folders step 1 recorded (by device and inode) go back. A folder step 1 can't take (a flag macOS won't let root change) is pruned there, step 2 doesn't run, and only the folders taken go back, with `colima_chown_failed`. ACLs on folders inside `~/.colima` are removed and not put back; Colima sets none. A file with a second name could be a root file linked in from elsewhere, so the script stops with `colima_linked_file`, before anything changes, if one that isn't the person's is there; step 2 skips any that appears later. That stop names the file by its full path (`$dir/` before what `find` printed), reaches the page as `linked_file`, says "A file with two names can be one of macOS's own, so check what it is before anything changes its owner.", keeps the button and leaves out the command to copy, whose `chown -R` would change that file. Each stop prints `keyloom colima fix stopped (<code>): <reason>` (`colima_dir_missing`, `colima_vm_running`, `colima_linked_file`, `colima_chown_failed`) and the app shows the reason. The app reads that line before it looks for osascript's `(-128)`, and takes a cancel only from `(-128)` at the end of a line, so a file named `disk-128` is not a closed dialog; the number and not "User canceled.", because macOS words that line in the person's language. Tested against a real `/bin/sh` on macOS, as this account, on a temporary folder, with a stand-in `chown` that takes its owner's write from a folder as root's ownership would and records what it would change: the order of the three steps and the restored mode; a stand-in that renames a file and hard-links another in its place at the first file change, which macOS refuses because the folder is locked, so the change never reaches the other file (with step 1 made a no-op, the swap succeeds and the test fails); a folder step 1 can't take; a stand-in `limactl` running as another account; stand-in `colima`, `find`, `chown`, `chmod`, `stat`, `head` and `ps` first on the starting PATH, none of which runs; a home whose name a shell would misread, a linked `~/.colima`, a `~/.colima` reached through a linked home, a linked file and a refused change.
  - **A mounted disk image changes what a name means without a write to the parent** (#300 re-review). The person can mount a disk image on a folder they own (`hdiutil attach -nobrowse -mountpoint`, no administrator needed). With an image on `~/.colima/work`, the first version's step 1 ran `chown -h 0 ./work` on the image's root folder; the person then unmounted it, so the real `work` stayed theirs, and the `stat` that ran next recorded the real `work` as root's. `find` passed over the real `work` without an error, because its device and inode no longer matched what it had read, so step 1 reported nothing, and step 2 then changed files by name in a folder the person could still rename and hard-link in. The reviewer reproduced it with a real image and a stand-in `chown` that unmounted it. Two changes close it. Step 1 now moves into each folder (`cd -P`) and makes every change to `.`, in one shell, so the changes and the recorded device and inode are all the one folder that `.` is, whatever its name means later; a folder unmounted from under that shell fails its next change, so step 1 reports `colima_chown_failed`. Step 2 runs each change in a shell (`-execdir /bin/sh -c … {} \;`) that first checks `.`, the folder `find` is in, which no name change can move: it must be root's, have no group or other write (`stat`'s symbolic mode), have no ACL (`ls -lde .` prints one line), and have a device and inode step 1 recorded. Only then does the same shell `exec chown`, so nothing can change `.` between the check and the change. A folder that fails stops the step there (`-quit`), so nothing more changes, step 3 still runs, and the fix ends `colima_chown_failed`. A test mounts a real 2 MB image on `work` and has the stand-in `chown` unmount it when root's change reaches it, and swap each file for a hard link to a file outside `~/.colima`: on the old script the outside file's inode reached `chown` twice; now nothing outside is changed and the run ends `colima_chown_failed`. Other tests take each of the four checks away in turn (group write, an ACL and a folder step 1 never recorded, each made between two file changes, and an owner that isn't root), and each makes a test fail. The tests run as this account, so the script is told this account is root (`tools.root`, tests only); the app always checks for uid 0.
  - **Every root change by name runs from a folder root holds at that moment** (#300 third review). Step 2's check left step 3 and step 1 out. Step 3 is a new `find -depth` with no prune, so it walks every folder, the ones step 1 never took included: a folder root can't take (`uchg`) and the real folder under an unmounted disk image both stay the person's on purpose. It ran `stat ./L`, `chmod <mode> ./L` and `chown -h ./L` from each folder's parent without checking the parent. The reviewer's attack, reproduced as their own user with the real script, `find`, `stat` and `/bin/chmod`: with `~/.colima/Q/L/m`, `L` at 0777 and an unlocked `P` that `find` reads after `Q`, the person moves `Q/L` into `P` once `Q` has gone back, then keeps swapping `P/L` with a hard link to a root file. `stat` saw the real `L`, recorded, and root's `chmod 0777` and `chown` then landed on whatever `./L` was: in 8 of 30 runs the real `chmod` changed a file outside `~/.colima` to 0777, and in 9 `chown` reached its inode. With a symbolic link `chmod` follows it to any path. Root `chmod` of any path, or a root file given to the person, is a way to root. Step 3 now runs the same check as step 2 first, in the same shell as the `stat`, `chmod` and `chown` (`find -depth` ignores `-prune`, so the shell is the only place): `.` must be root's, with no group or other write and no ACL, and recorded by step 1. Otherwise the folder is passed over (`exit 0`): a folder there is the person's already, or got there only after step 3 gave it back. Reading the script again for every root action by name found the same gap in step 1: it ran `cd -P "./$1"` from the parent without checking it. `find` reaches a folder only through its parent, so the parent is normally one step 1 has just taken, but a disk image mounted on the parent after `find` listed it, and unmounted after root's change landed on it, lets `find` go into the real parent, which is still the person's. There `cd -P` follows a name the person has swapped for a symbolic link, and root's `chown -h 0`, `chmod -N` and `chmod go-w` land on any folder (`/private/tmp` would lose its other write). Step 1 now checks the parent the same way before the `cd`, except for the starting folder, and fails that folder (`colima_chown_failed`) if the parent isn't root's. It can't ask whether the parent is recorded, because the list is step 1's own output, but a root-owned folder without group or other write or an ACL is one the person can't change names in. What root still does by name in folders the person can write is read: `find`'s looks, the `stat` of each folder's mode before step 1, and each `ha.pid`, whose text reaches only `ps`, and only as digits. Two tests, run as this account with the real `/bin/chmod` and a stand-in `chown`: the reviewer's (a stand-in `uchg` `P`, `L` moved into it after `Q` goes back, and a loop that swaps `P/L` with a hard link to a file outside), which on 51ce62fd ran step 3 from `P/L`; and one where `C` stops being root's as root takes its first folder and its second becomes a link to a folder outside, which on 51ce62fd followed the link and ran step 1's `chown -h 0 .` in the outside folder. Both pass now, with nothing run from `P` and nothing outside changed.
- **What the page does with the answer.** While it runs: "Fixing Colima's files. macOS asks for your password first, then keyloom starts Colima as you, which can take half a minute." A closed dialog (osascript's -128) comes back as `cancelled`; the page says "You closed the password dialog, so nothing changed." and keeps the button. The stop ends whatever the other copy runs, so the fix asks the forced restart's check (`engineRestartBlocker`, D-529) inside the engine lock, right before the password dialog, and is refused with `build_running` while a build, flash, keyboard write or container run is under way; the button stays with "Fix Colima's files once it has finished." A server without that check refuses the fix. It holds the engine lock as `fix-files` (D-511), so it is refused with `engine_busy` while keyloom starts or restarts Docker, and while it runs a restart is refused and a start, a build or a replay waits for it. Readiness reports `engineAction: "fix-files"`, so another page shows the fix in its own words instead of "Starting Colima" while the dialog is open. Any other failure shows its reason and takes the button away, because pressing it again fails the same way; the command to copy becomes "Run this in Terminal to fix Colima's files yourself, then check again." After every answer but `engine_busy` the page asks Docker again by itself, so a Colima that didn't come up after the fix shows Restart Colima (D-511) and a fixed one shows nothing. After ten minutes keyloom stops osascript and says it stopped waiting, so a dialog nobody answers can't hold the engine lock for good. Not checked: whether stopping osascript closes its dialog, and a root step already running when time is up runs on after the lock is released.
- **After a linked file, the command stays hidden until a fix completes** (PR #300 CodeRabbit). The page held it back only while the last answer's code was `linked_file`, so a later press that the person cancelled, or that a build refused, showed `chown -R` again, which follows the hard link the fix refused. `useDocker` now keeps that in its own `linkedFile` state, which `linkedFileAfter` sets on `linked_file` and clears only when a fix completes; no other answer, and no start or restart, clears it. While it holds, the command is left out of every no-access view, and the button stays after any failure, because it is then the only way on and it checks for the linked file again. The page's state alone was lost on a reload, and a page opened fresh never had it (CodeRabbit's second review), so readiness holds the command back too: `access.command` is null and `access.linkedFile` names the file while the server remembers a `linked_file` from the fix and no fix has completed since, or while a look for one as the person (`colimaLinkedFileSeen`, `find -P ~/.colima -xdev ! -type d -links +1 ! -user <uid> -print -quit`, no root) finds one. The page then says "A file in Colima's folder, <path>, also has a name somewhere else, so keyloom doesn't offer Colima's command, which would change that file's owner too. Check what the file is. Fix Colima's files looks for it again and changes nothing while it is there." It says that beside whatever ended the latest press, so a cancel after a linked file still says why there is no command and which file (CodeRabbit's fifth review). The server's memory is lost when keyloom restarts; a linked file in a folder the person can't read is covered by the next bullet. The server reads its memory again after the look, so a fix that finds a linked file while the look runs holds the command back in that same answer. `keyloom doctor` runs the same look, so it leaves the command out too, and prints that sentence in place of the note that described the command; it printed "This stops that copy…" about a command it no longer showed (#300 third review).
- **Colima's command shows only after a look that finished and found no linked file** (PR #300 CodeRabbit's fifth review; the orchestrator's decision on BK's no-dead-ends bar). The look read only `find`'s output, so a folder the person can't read looked like "no linked file", and after a restart the page showed `chown -R` for a hard link it couldn't see. `colimaLinkedFileSeen` now has three answers: `found` (with the file), `none` (find exited 0 with nothing on stderr and nothing found), and `incomplete` (find exited non-zero, printed an error, timed out or couldn't start, or the folder and account checks failed, so the look never ran). Readiness shows the command only after `none`. After `incomplete`, `access.command` is null and `access.scanIncomplete` true, and the page and `keyloom doctor` say "keyloom couldn't look through all of Colima's folder for a file that also has a name somewhere else, so it doesn't offer Colima's command, which would change such a file's owner too. Fix Colima's files checks every file itself and changes nothing if it finds one." The button stays after every outcome while the command is held, because the root script reads every folder and refuses a linked file itself, so the page always has a way on. The look checks the same folder and account as the fix, but not for a running VM: another account's VM blocks the fix, not the command, and the command would still follow a linked file, so the look runs while the VM refusal applies and a linked file it finds holds the command back as usual. The cost: a Mac with a folder in `~/.colima` the person can't read gets no command to copy, only the button.
- **The root script's answer can't be forged by a file name** (PR #300 CodeRabbit's fifth review). The script printed the linked file's name in its `colima_linked_file` record, and a name can hold a carriage return or newline followed by a forged `keyloom colima fix stopped (colima_vm_running): …`; the parser took the last record, so the hold was never set. The script now sends every tool's stderr to `/dev/null` (`exec 3>&2 2>/dev/null`; `fail` writes to fd 3), because `find` and `chown` errors quote names too, and prints the linked file's name only as hex (`od -An -v -tx1`). `colimaFixFailure` takes only the code from the record and uses keyloom's own sentence for it; for a linked file it decodes the hex and escapes every control character (`\r`, `\n`, `\uXXXX`) before the name reaches the page or the terminal. A test runs the real script on a hard-linked file whose name holds `\r`, `\n` and two forged records: the code stays `colima_linked_file`, and the output holds exactly one record. With errors thrown away, a `find` that can't list a folder passed over it silently, and step 3 could leave that folder root's while the script reported success (CodeRabbit's sixth review). So each step's walk counts a non-zero `find` exit as a failure: in step 1 it stops step 2, and in steps 2 and 3 it ends the run with `colima_chown_failed`. A test walks a folder it can enter but not list (mode 0100).
- **Nothing from keyloom's environment reaches root** (#300 re-review). Refines D-493 and D-498, whose kanata setup shares `adminCommand`. macOS's `/bin/sh` is bash 3.2. It runs an exported function from the environment in place of a built-in (`env 'BASH_FUNC_cd%%=() { echo FUNC-RAN; }' /bin/sh -c 'cd /tmp'` printed `FUNC-RAN`), and with `SHELLOPTS=xtrace` it runs `PS4`'s command substitution before each command (observed as this account). Whoever starts keyloom chooses its environment, for example with `open --env`. Whether `do shell script … with administrator privileges` keeps that environment was not checked, because this round runs no root steps. So it no longer matters: `adminCommand` starts `/usr/bin/osascript` through `/usr/bin/env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin`, and the text it gives `do shell script` is `/usr/bin/env -i PATH=… /bin/sh -c '<script>'`. An `env -i` inside the script alone would not have done it: the outer shell still ran `PS4` under `SHELLOPTS=xtrace`. A test runs the built command line with `/bin/sh -c` in place of osascript's `do shell script`, under an environment with `BASH_FUNC_cd%%`, `SHELLOPTS=xtrace`, `PS4` and an ordinary variable, and checks that none of them runs or arrives; the same environment without the wrapper runs the function. `osascript` and a plain `do shell script` ran with an empty environment as this account; the password dialog under it was not run.
- **The command stays.** Below the button: "Or run Colima's command in Terminal yourself, then check again:" with the same Copy command as before. The CLI is unchanged: `doctor` prints the command, unless a linked file or a look that didn't finish holds it back, and runs nothing, as `keyloom remap setup` does for kanata (D-493).
- **Whose home folder.** The folder root changes is `.colima` in the home folder of the account record (`id -P`), not `$HOME`: under Bun, `os.homedir()` and `os.userInfo().homedir` both follow `$HOME`, and `HOME=/Users/Shared` would have pointed root at a folder another account can reach. Colima itself keeps its files under `$HOME`, so when the two differ the fix changes nothing and says "keyloom is running with its home folder set to <$HOME>, not your own, <home>, so it changed nothing. Start keyloom from your own account's settings, then try again." The look for a linked file can't vouch for that folder either, so the command to copy is held back too, and the button, whose press gives that sentence, stays.
- **Still open.** Not run against a real root-owned `~/.colima`, or a VM that root really runs: this round runs no root steps. A Mac with a Colima started by `sudo` would settle it. The `colima_vm_running` check finds a VM by Lima's `ha.pid`; a root VM that Lima doesn't record there is not seen, and its files are given back while it runs, which the start as the person then reports. The empty environment under the password dialog, and a real root `chown` racing a real unmount, were not run either.

### D-530: A direct edit that landed is never called failed, and the key card and kanata's checklist say why in place. Extends D-524 to direct edits. (Claude, from the round-6 dead-ends review, R6-03, R6-16, R6-22)

- **The read after a direct edit has its own catch** (R6-03). Every direct-edit path reads the new layout through `readAfterApply` (`src/web/editor/read-after-apply.ts`): the key card, the hold-tap tuner, Repeat, a combo, the settings page, Update layout, undo and every board or tab change (`useChange`). A change the server wrote whose read fails shows D-524's toast, "Applied. keyloom couldn't show the new layout yet.", with **Show the new layout**, and no "Applied" toast. The key card, combo and settings drafts clear, because the change is written. An Apply sent again from the old page names the old base revision, so the server refuses it as stale and the page reads the latest version. The Viewer's callbacks never reject for a failed read, so no pane can report it as "The change could not be applied." The key card closes after such a write, from Apply or the tuner, and drops its draft, which is written: left open on the old revision, it kept the draft with Apply on, and Swap, Copy and Paste acted on the old binding (PR #297 CodeRabbit). Show the new layout reads the layout again, and the key opens from there.
- **A stale edit says the latest version is showing only once it is.** Before, the toast said so and then read it with the error dropped. Now it reads first. A failed read says "Nothing was changed, and keyloom couldn't show the latest version yet." with **Show the latest version**, which tries again with the same words. A stale undo works the same way.
- **Swap, Copy and Paste** (R6-16). While the key has a draft, a line under the card's header says "Swap, Copy and Paste are off: apply or discard this key's change first." Paste with nothing copied stays pressable, and pressing it says "Copy a key first", so the reason is never only in a tooltip. ⌘C obeys the same guard as the Copy button, so it never copies the saved binding while the card shows a draft.
- **kanata's attention steps** (R6-22). A failed read of what kanata needs shows its reason with **Check again** in the sync popover's attention mode too, as setup mode already did. Before, the steps vanished.

### D-529: A build stopped by the engine's own state offers a forced restart, and a board keyloom sees only after its first flash says so. Refines D-499 and D-511. (Claude, from the round-6 dead-ends review, R6-09, R6-10, R6-17, R6-21)

- **Two build codes** (R6-09, R6-17). `stale_mount`: Docker still sees an old copy of what keyloom wrote after the settle time (Colima's sshfs share). `stuck_container`: a build container that an earlier keyloom left in the checkout can't be removed. Build again hits the same engine state, so the build step shows its own words and **Restart {engine} and build again**, and not the build's sentence. That sentence names `colima restart` and `XDG_CACHE_HOME`, so the page never shows it, and the log tail leaves out keyloom's closing "build failed" line. The CLI keeps the sentence. The build view carries `restartEngine`, the engine `forcedRestartEngine` finds, which is Colima or a Mac engine app. It is looked up only for these two codes. A hash probe that Docker couldn't run (an exit other than `sha256sum`'s 0 or 1, or a timeout) saw no copy, so it is not `stale_mount`: it fails with no code and **Build again** (PR #297 CodeRabbit). Any other engine, such as Linux's system service, gets "Restarting your Docker engine clears this. Restart it, then build again." and **Build again**.
- **A forced restart** is `POST /api/system/docker/restart` with `{ "force": true }`. It restarts an engine that answers or is slow, which D-511's restart never does. Colima gets `colima stop --force` and `colima start`; a Mac app is quit and opened again. It is refused with `build_running` while anything it would stop runs: a build, in this keyloom or another live one with the same data directory; a flash or keyboard write; and a lab analysis, a board review's tests or an emulator run in this keyloom (`engineRestartBlocker`). The refusal names what runs: "keyloom is running a build and a lab analysis, and restarting Docker now would stop them." A server built without that check refuses every forced restart. The button stays after a `build_running` refusal, with the reason beside it. A restart that fails shows its reason, the words "keyloom couldn't restart Colima. Restart Colima yourself, then build again." and **Build again**, and is not offered a second time (D-511). A restart that works starts the build at once.
- **The check runs inside the engine lock, right before the stop** (PR #297 review). The restart takes D-511's lock, finds the engine, then asks `engineRestartBlocker` and stops the engine only if nothing runs. `ensureDocker`, which every build and emulator run calls before it starts a container, waits while a restart holds the lock, even when Docker answers. A build registers itself before it calls `ensureDocker`, and an emulator run holds its name (`holdContainerWork`) from before it calls it. So a run that started before the check is seen and refuses the restart, and one that starts after it waits for the restart to end. Before, the check ran before the lock and a build that found Docker up started a container between the check and the stop. A forced request asks the same check before it stops an engine that read down or refused, too (PR #297 CodeRabbit). On the page, a build asked for while the restart runs is newer: the restart then starts no build of its own and shows nothing under the newer one.
- **What the guard can't see.** Another keyloom with its own `--data-dir` keeps its builds in its own database, and its lab and review runs in its own process, so a forced restart from this one stops them. Running two keyloom servers on two data directories is rare, so this is recorded, not guarded. The guard is also per process: a terminal `keyloom emulate`, `workload` or `lab` run on the same data directory is invisible to the page's restart, which stops its container, and a terminal `keyloom build` that starts between the check and `colima stop` is not held off. A keyboard write uses no Docker; the restart still waits for it, and the refusal says keyloom is writing to a keyboard rather than that the restart would stop it.
- **The CLI has no forced restart.** `keyloom doctor --restart-docker` leaves a Docker that answers running, and a build that fails with `stale_mount` or `stuck_container` prints the build's sentence, which names `colima restart`. A terminal user can run that command. This is a deliberate gap against D-030, because a CLI restart would need the same guard over every keyloom process, and the page is where these failures were reported.
- **A board keyloom can't see before its first flash** (R6-10). A firmware board whose revisions have no detect IDs (a stock foostan Corne) is absent until keyloom's firmware, which names it, is on it. When keyloom never wrote its firmware to it, the sync state is `not_connected` with `firstFlash`. A sync counts only once it reached the flash step: one that failed at its build wrote nothing (PR #297 CodeRabbit). The evidence is the whole board's, across all its configs (`keyloomFirmwareWrittenTo`): a version put on it, or a flash whose write finished (including a QMK flash keyloom couldn't confirm, `not_confirmed`). A flash of another config never brings the line back. The stock Corne is ZMK: a flash whose half didn't come back in time fails with no code, so after an unplug the line returns. That is deliberate, because keyloom's own error says the firmware may be incomplete. The line says "keyloom sees your Corne once keyloom's firmware is on it." and its button is **Build and flash…**. The popover then doesn't list Build and flash twice. `keyloom sync status` says `keyloom build`, then `keyloom flash`, puts it on, because a flash needs a build.
- **A failed build re-read** (R6-21) sets the dialog's error, "keyloom couldn't read this build again.", and its **Try again** reads it again. Before, it was dropped.
- **`lastBuilt` reaches the page.** The server's build adapter (`src/cli/editor-backend.ts`) did not copy `lastBuilt`, so R5-02's **Go back to the last version that built** never showed in the real app. It does now, beside `restartEngine`.

## 2026-11-12 round 6: keyboards, onboarding and import

### D-532: A refusal about a keyboard carries the board it names, and the page answers it with a button. Refines D-336, D-476 and D-525. (Claude, from the round-6 dead-ends review, R6-07, R6-08, R6-14, R6-18)

- **The board on the error.** `ApiErrorBody.error` has an optional `board`: the keyboard a refusal names. `board_known` also sends `boardName`, keyloom's own name for that board. `ApiError.board` and `ApiError.boardName` carry them to the page. The keyboards and editor routes fill it in. The page reads the code and the board, never the sentence, which stays the CLI's.
- **A keyboard keyloom already has** (R6-07). `previewKeyboard` checks `boardForCandidate` before it reads anything. A match is `KeyboardAddError("board_known")` with the board's ID, and `addKeyboard` refuses the same way. `--force` still re-derives a board derived before: the CLI passes it to the preview too. The Confirm panel says "keyloom already has the {board}" and offers **Put a config on it**, which opens the put step for that board, and **Pick another keyboard**. The heading and the put step name the board by keyloom's name ("Moonlander"), not the search result's ("Moonlander Mark I"). A candidate that matches a board keyloom has keeps its plain name. Only an unmatched candidate whose name another board uses gets "(new)". The "(new)" check uses the same boards as the refusal.
- **A removed keyboard** (R6-08). `importLayout` and `newConfig` throw `BoardRefusal("keyboard_removed", board)`. The CLI's sentence now names the board, not a literal `<board>`. The import card, the New config dialog, the setup card and the put step show the words "You removed the {board} from keyloom…" with **Restore {board}**. Restore is Your keyboards' own restore. Then the page does the refused import or new config again. The words and Restore name the board the server refused (`error.board`), which after Import it as … is that board, not the card's; the retry imports to the same board. Syncing stays paused (D-476), and the words say so.
- **The layout file** (R6-14). `readGeometry` tells three cases apart. A file it can't parse as a layout is `layout_unreadable`. That includes a 404 for a file the index names, since the commit is pinned. The page treats it as blocked, with the contribution guide (D-525). Any other failure to fetch is `firmware_unreachable`, with "Check that this computer is online" and **Try again**. `fetchPinned` caches only a download its caller can parse, and drops a cached copy that doesn't parse and downloads it once more, so a captive portal's page never blocks a keyboard for good. A failed cache write is only warned about: the file was read. Neither shows the server's sentence, so the page never shows keyloom's cache path. A network error from `fetch` was a 500 before. Now it is this code.
- **Another board's export** (R6-18). An export keyloom wrote, dropped on another board's card, is `BoardRefusal("layout_for_other_board", board)`. With that board in the list, the card offers **Import it as a {board} config**. That sends the same file to that board, past the card's own file-type check. With no such board, it says keyloom can import the file once it has that keyboard, and links the contribution guide.

### D-531: The new-keyboard and import screens never wait, refuse or point back without saying what to do. Refines D-336 and D-525. (Claude, from the round-6 dead-ends review, R6-04, R6-05, R6-11, R6-13, R6-15, R6-19, R6-24, R6-25)

- **The board list** (R6-04). App tracks whether `/api/boards` was read. First run says "Reading keyloom's keyboard list…" while it reads. If the read fails, it says "keyloom couldn't read its keyboard list." with **Try again**. "No supported board matches" shows only once the list and the collection have both been read (`catalogueNote`). A read again after one that worked keeps the list.
- **The Put step's map** (R6-05). A failed `put/preview` replaces "Mapping it…". No answer or a 5xx gives **Try again** (`putProblem`). A 4xx says why, without Try again. Both offer **Pick another config**, and the "Or start a config" line stays. While a map is read, the button says "Mapping it…", not "Pick a config". The put lists no config already on the target board. Copying within one board is `config new`.
- **The setup card's import line** (R6-11) comes from the board's own guide (`importLine`). The MacBook reads "Bring in what you set up in Karabiner-Elements."
- **Home row after layout** (R6-13). While a QMK keyboard's layout is still keyloom's guess, the Confirm step asks only for the layout. Picking one resets the home row, so the home-row question then shows.
- **Search again** (R6-19). A failed QMK and ZMK search has its own state. It says "keyloom couldn't search QMK and ZMK." with **Search again**, on the card and on the welcome screen.
- **Fill-in hints** (R6-24). Rename and New config show why the action is off: "Type a name to rename it.", "Type the new name to rename it.", "Type a name for the new config." and "Pick the version to start from." The fields point to these lines with `aria-describedby`.
- **First run's import** (R6-25). From the keyboard welcome, the import screen's way back says "Back to choosing a keyboard". Its title stays "Bring in your first layout" while there is no layout. Activity tests are offered "for keyboards with QMK or ZMK firmware" and are never sent with a kanata board's import, whatever is ticked. Each card filters them for the board it imports to, so Import it as a Glove80 config from the MacBook card still sends them. The import card's Copy is `shell/CopyCommand` (R6-15).

## 2026-11-12 round 6: review, chat, proposals and lab

### D-528: A typing-lab read-out that fails has a code and a way on, and a recording keyloom can't stop is stopped from the lab. Refines D-314, D-495 and D-519. (Claude, from the round-6 dead-ends review, R6-02, R6-20, R6-24)

- **Docker stopped the read-out** (R6-02). `emulateEvents` gives its Docker check's `BuildFailure` the `docker_unavailable` code, and `EmulateFailedError` carries it. The lab backend answers 503 `docker_unavailable` for it, and 500 `replay_failed` for any other replay failure, in place of 500 "internal error". Neither sends the emulator's own message to the page: it can name the checkout and lock directories, and the ZMK emulator's last output lines are the firmware's key log, which is the recording, so it would break `src/api/lab.ts`'s promise that the lab API never carries the raw trace. The page gets keyloom's fixed sentence ("The details are in keyloom's log."), and the server's log keeps the whole message with its log path. The lab shows the same Docker callout as Build and flash, through `NeedsDocker` (the activity tests' Docker view, now shared), and **Read it again** for that session once Docker answers. `replay_failed` says keyloom couldn't play the recording back and that the details are in keyloom's log, and offers **Read it again**. Both say the recording is saved.
- **A recording still running** (R6-20). A start refused because a recording runs is now `recording_running`, apart from `busy`, which stays for a keyboard another app holds. A Cancel that fails no longer resets to Start in silence: it says keyloom may still be recording, with the page's own code `cancel_failed`. Both offer **Stop and discard that recording**, which is the cancel route again. Neither offers Try again, since a start is refused until the recording stops. The stop discards what the other recording heard rather than saving it the way the lab's own Stop does: a session is saved with the text typed for it, which lives in the tab that started the recording, and the read-out compares the keys against that text, so a session saved without it would be read wrong. A recording whose Cancel failed was one the person chose to throw away. So the label and the words say that nothing it heard is kept.
- **Start listening with your own text** (R6-24) says "Paste the text you want to type, then start listening." beside the disabled button.

### D-527: A Codex usage limit in the review offers another model, a proposal handled elsewhere says it is no longer open, and the review's words match its buttons. Refines D-518, D-522 and D-524. (Claude, from the round-6 dead-ends review, R6-01, R6-06, R6-12, R6-15, R6-23, R6-24)

- **Usage limit in the review** (R6-01). `runReviewAi` counts `usage_limit` as an account failure, so the review ends `unavailable` with that code and Codex's reset sentence (D-522). The panel says "Your Codex usage limit is reached…" with the reset time in place, not behind Details. It offers **Switch to {provider}** for each other ready provider, which sets the choice chat and the review share and runs the review again. The list is built from the provider the look failed on (`usage_limit` comes only from Codex), not the one chosen now, so it never offers Codex back. When keyloom has moved off Codex since (a switch in chat), the words say Try again runs on the provider in use, and no switch is offered. Nothing is offered to switch or set up until the provider status is read, since a lock may forbid it; a setup panel opened before a lock loads closes when it does. Chat and the review share each status a switch, key or sign-in saves (a page event), so a switch in one shows in the other without a reload.
