With none ready it offers **Set up another model**, the D-496 setup for the other providers; once one is ready its Switch to appears. **Try again** is for after the reset. A model fixed by `--provider` or `KEYLOOM_PROVIDER` gets only Try again, and the words don't offer a switch. A failed switch says so with Try again.
- **A proposal no longer open** (R6-06). Applying or rejecting a change set that is no longer proposed throws `NotOpenError`, a `UsageError` (still exit 2 in the CLI) that the editor backend answers as 400 `not_open`. The card maps `not_open` and `not_found` by code (`proposalApplyFailure`); any other `usage` refusal keeps the server's words and Apply. Those are: "This proposal is no longer open.", Apply off, no Plan a revised proposal, and **Read proposals again**. In chat that reopens the conversation, which brings each card's status up to date; it is not offered while a reply streams. In OpenProposals it is the panel's existing read. A failed read says so and keeps the button.
- **Stop** (R6-12). Every running look has a Stop. It reads **Stop testing** only while the activity tests are pending or running, and **Stop the review** otherwise. The engine's stop aborts the whole run either way. A failed stop says "keyloom couldn't stop the review."
- **Copy in the model setup** (R6-15). `chat/ModelSetup.tsx` uses `shell/CopyCommand`, so a refused copy selects the command and says which keys copy it.
- **Repair requests** (R6-23). `repairWords` makes the words and buttons one decision. A request that can still be sent has **Cancel**, and its words don't say Dismiss. Any other has **Dismiss request**. A sent one says to open the chat history and offers **Open history**. A finding that went out of date says to dismiss it and run a fresh review, and that dismissing drops the request with its edits, and offers **Dismiss and run a fresh review**, which drops the request (Plan a repair waits while one is up), opens the review panel and runs it. A finding is out of date only once the review has settled (`findingRepairState`). While the review is busy or being read again (another item hidden, the AI part finishing) it is `checking`: Send is off, the words say the edits are kept, and only Cancel shows.
- **What a disabled Save waits for** (R6-24). The decision editor names its empty fields ("Fill in Goal and Why to save."), the outcome question asks for the note, and the activity tests ask for one activity, each as a visible line that also describes the button.

## 2026-11-11 round 5: builds, review and proposals

### D-524: Every failed review request has its button, activity tests can be stopped, and an Apply that landed is never called failed. Refines D-340, D-473 and D-518. (Claude, from the round-5 dead-ends review, R5-03, R5-08, R5-09, R5-10, R5-11, R5-17)

- **A failed review request keeps its button** (R5-03, R5-10). `useReview` remembers which request failed (read, run, stop, hide, show again). The panel shows its words with one button in every state, including before the first review: no answer or a server fault gives **Try again**, which repeats that request. A 400 or 404 means the review changed under the page, such as a run already going or an item gone, so asking again would be refused too. That gives **Read the review again** with the server's reason. A server fault's own text ("internal error") is not shown; the words say it hit an unexpected error. Only a failed read withholds the results and marks the review unavailable.
- **Stop for activity tests** (R5-17). `POST /api/layouts/:ref/review/stop` aborts the engine's run for the config with a `ReviewStopped` reason. The emulator's `run` already removes its container by name when its signal aborts, so Stop needs no container code of its own. The review ends `failed` with "You stopped this review…", and the test evidence says "You stopped the tests". The route answers once the run has ended, or after 5 s while a container is still being removed, since the server drops a request that stays quiet for 10 s and the page hears the end from the `review` event. With nothing running it answers with the review as it is. `finish` picks the stopped words for any failure while Stop has aborted the run, so no step can say keyloom closed instead. The panel offers **Stop testing** while the look runs with tests. The CLI's `review --run` already stops on Ctrl-C.
- **An Apply that landed** (R5-08, R5-09). Only the `applyChangeSet` refusal is an Apply failure. `onProposalApplied`, which every Apply on the page goes through (proposals panel, lab, review panel and chat), catches a failure to read the new layout afterwards itself. It shows "Applied. keyloom couldn't show the new layout yet." with **Show the new layout**. A second failure says so again with the same button, in the toast and in the review panel.
- **Unread proposals on the board** (R5-11). The board's notice slot shows **Couldn't read proposals · Try again** instead of hiding, so an unread list is never taken for an empty one.
- **A refused Copy** (R5-17). `CopyCommand` selects the command and says which keys copy it. `chat/ModelSetup.tsx` has its own copy button, which this round's chat worker owns, and is unchanged here.

### D-523: A build that fails the same way every time offers the last version that built and a question to keyloom, not Build again. Refines D-488 and D-502. (Claude, from the round-5 dead-ends review, R5-02)

- **Two new build codes.** `compile_error`: the source keyloom wrote doesn't compile. This covers a QMK keymap error located to a layer and position, a compiler error in another file in the keymap's folder, a ZMK keymap or devicetree error, and a `.conf` setting ZMK rejected. One function, `classifyBuildFailure`, holds the order for all three drivers, and each passes only its own locators. A key code the firmware doesn't know is one of these, because GCC reports it as undeclared. `too_large`: GNU ld's `region … overflowed by N bytes` or `will not fit in region`, or QMK's AVR `The firmware is too large!`. The drivers check Docker's own trouble (a full disk, a stopped daemon) first, since it can pass. Any other failure keeps no code and keeps **Build again**. The patterns come from recorded lines in `test/fixtures/build-logs/`, whose README names each source.
- **The way back.** For those codes the build view carries `lastBuilt`: the newest revision before the failed one, in its own history, with a build for the same board revision that succeeded. The step says building again fails the same way. It offers **Go back to the last version that built**, which is D-502's undo with `to`. That makes a new revision, so the failed one stays in History. A refused Go back follows D-506: a moved head gets **Show the latest version**. With no earlier revision that built, or for a build of an earlier revision chosen in History, the step offers only the question.
- **Ask keyloom about this build.** The dialog closes and the chat opens with "Question about a failed build". It is the repair request the review already uses, holding the error and up to the 30 log lines the build keeps (the lines the dialog shows) within the request's 8000 characters. The person checks it and sends it, and the panel says it sends the build log to the model. A request already waiting in the chat is kept, and the page says so. This touches two lines of `ChatPanel.tsx`, the request's title and scope.
- **CLI** (D-030). `buildAndWait` adds, for these codes, that building again fails the same way, with `keyloom version list` and `keyloom version restore`.

## 2026-11-11 round 5: keyboards, editor and the mismatch page

### D-526: The Delete layer dialog decides before it closes, Make a combo says why it is off, and the mismatch page drops a detail that points elsewhere. Refines D-520. (Claude, from the round-5 dead-ends review, R5-12, R5-13, R5-14)

- **Open combo from Delete layer** (R5-13). `LayerActions.openCombo` now returns a refusal or null, and the dialog acts on it before it closes (`openReference`). While another combo's card holds edits, the dialog stays open, says "Your changes to the combo “Go” aren't applied yet. Apply or close them before you open another combo." (or "The combo you're making…"), and offers **Show the combo you're editing**, which closes it and shows that card. It never closes into a toast with no button. A key's unapplied draft can't block here: it turns the layer menu off (`undoBlock`), so the dialog can't open while one is held.
- **Make a combo** (R5-12). With one key selected, "Select a second key to make a combo" shows beside the disabled button and describes it, in place of a title only a mouse hover shows.
- **Put-on refused while kanata's setup shows** (R5-14). `ConfirmDialog` leaves out a refusal's detail when `extra` is up and the refusal is marked `extraFixes` (`shownDetail`). Only `kanata_not_running` is marked: the setup checklist is the way past it, and "The keyboard's status lists what is missing" named a page the dialog doesn't open. Every other refusal keeps its detail while the checklist stays up, such as a later busy or not_connected. The toast for the same refusal keeps the detail beside its Open keyboard status button.

### D-525: A keyboard keyloom can't add yet says why in words and offers the contribution guide, and a QMK keyboard with several layouts asks which is yours. Refines D-336. (Claude, from the round-5 dead-ends review, R5-06, R5-16)

- **Codes, not flags** (R5-06). `DeriveError` carries a code at each of its four sites: `controller_unknown` (a shield needs a controller and none fits that keyloom can flash: MakerDiary m60, nice!view, nice!view adapter, TesterRpiPico, ZMK Uno), `boot_drive_unknown`, `layout_macro_missing` and `home_row_out_of_range`. `KeyboardAddError` (a `UsageError`, exit 2) adds `no_key_positions`, for a firmware tree with no key positions keyloom can read (40 of the index's 69 ZMK entries). The preview carries `boardProblemCode`; add and a refused preview answer 400 with the code. The CLI keeps its sentences and flags.
- **The Confirm step** says it in plain words (`derive-words.ts`): "keyloom doesn't know a controller for the MakerDiary m60 yet…". It offers **Open the contribution guide** (CONTRIBUTING.md) and **Pick another keyboard**, and hides Show me the board, which could never work. A refused preview with one of these codes gets the same, in place of Try again. Prepare contribution isn't offered: it packages a stored board, and there is none to package.
- **Several layouts** (R5-06). 1,127 of the index's 3,687 QMK keyboards have several layouts and none called LAYOUT, so the preview ended at "choose one with --layout" with a Try again that failed the same way. The preview now draws the one with most keys, lists every layout in `layouts`, and adds `--layout` to `needs`. The Confirm step asks "Which layout is yours?" first whenever a QMK keyboard has more than one. The preview names the guess in `guessedLayout`, so it stays marked after a pick. Picking one draws it and resets the home row to the guess for it. A guess is never stored. `keyboard add` refuses without `--layout`, lists the layouts and marks the drawn one as keyloom's guess. On the page, no layout is preselected and the next step reads "Pick your layout", disabled, until the person picks one (`reviewStep`). A keyboard with a default layout goes on with that default.
- **Start a config on the Put step** (R5-16). With configs to copy, "Or start a config from the {board}'s own keys" sits under the list, so a keyboard none of them maps to still has a way on.

## 2026-11-11 round 5: desktop and chat

### D-522: A Codex plan that is used up says when it resets and offers another model, a refused Try again keeps its code, and a message keyloom never confirmed offers Open history. Refines D-517. (Claude, from the round-5 dead-ends review, R5-04, R5-05, R5-07)

- **A used-up plan** (R5-04). Codex's "You’ve hit your usage limit…" and its workspace "spend cap" are now the model error `usage_limit` (`model_usage_limit` on the wire, 429), not `rate_limit`. Waiting a moment doesn't help a plan's allowance, so "The model is busy" with only Try again was a dead end. The box is "Your Codex usage limit is reached", with **Switch to …** for each other ready provider, **Set up another model** when none is ready, and Try again for after the reset. The sentence is keyloom's, not Codex's, because Codex names upgrade pages and settings: "Your ChatGPT plan has used up its Codex allowance until 3:45 PM." The time is read from Codex's own sentence (codex-rs/protocol/src/error.rs @ f096185f, `format_retry_timestamp`: "3:45 PM", or "Nov 12th, 2026 3:45 PM" on a later day, in this computer's time zone). When Codex gives none, the sentence says so. A workspace spend cap has its own sentence, because an admin sets it and it may never reset by itself: "Your ChatGPT workspace has reached the Codex spend cap its admin set. It stays reached until an admin raises it." The real Codex wording is checked against that source and its tests, not against a live usage limit.
- **A refused Try again** (R5-05). The failed turn keeps the server's code (`missing_api_key`, `model_*`) instead of `retry`, so a signed-out Codex shows "The model isn't set up" with Set up the model and Switch to, not a terminal command beside Try again.
- **A message keyloom may have** (R5-07). A request that was neither refused nor seen starting is `unconfirmed`, a page-only code. A refusal is any 4xx, or a 5xx with the server's own code other than `internal` (`key_store_unavailable`, `model_overloaded`): the server sends `start` as soon as it has stored the message, so a typed failure before it stored nothing, and the message goes back to the composer. An `internal` 500 and a lost connection stay `unconfirmed`. The box: "keyloom didn't confirm your message", with **Open history** (the conversation list) and **Ask again**. The prepared-work note is left out unless the reply counted proposals or priorities, because the box already says to check before asking again.

### D-521: Restart keyloom is a route the desktop shell already watches: the server exits 11 and the supervisor starts it again at once. (Claude, from the round-5 dead-ends review, R5-15, R5-01)

- **The ask.** A lab capture refused as `restart_required` (a keyboard connection only a restart clears) said "Quit keyloom and open it again" with no button.
- **The design.** `POST /api/restart` answers 202, then stops the server the way Ctrl-C does, so a keyboard write in progress finishes, and `serve` exits with code 11 (`RESTART_EXIT_CODE`, `src/api/restart.ts`). The shell already starts a server it owns again when it exits (D-507). Exit 11 sets a flag before the exit is reported, and the supervisor then starts the server at once, without the back-off and without counting it toward the three-in-ten-minutes cap. The window follows the new server as it does after any restart.
- **Rejected: a new IPC command or a custom URL scheme from the page.** The page is remote content with no Tauri IPC (capabilities/default.json), and the problem window's `keyloom-desktop://` links exist only in that window. A route works the same from the page and from the menu bar, needs no page-to-shell channel, and an older shell restarts an exit-11 server anyway, after its back-off.
- **Rejected: the shell sends SIGTERM itself.** The menu bar could, but the page can't reach the shell, and a SIGTERM exit counts as a stop.
- **Who can restart.** Only a server started with both `--client-protocol` and `--exit-on-stdin-close` (only the desktop app passes them) answers 202. Both, so a terminal that passes `--client-protocol` alone keeps its server. A server started in a terminal answers 409 `restart_unsupervised`, because nothing would start it again. `GET /api/restart` says which, with the terminal line (`keyloom serve` with the data directory, made absolute, and the port and provider it was started with). A terminal server started with `--port 0` gets `--port 0` again; the new server prints and opens its own link, because each start has a new session token anyway. The lab shows **Restart keyloom** in the app, and in a terminal "Stop it there with Ctrl-C, then run this to start it again:" with Copy.
- **A failed cleanup is still a restart.** The lab asks for a restart when the keyboard helper can't be closed, and that helper fails cleanup again on the way out. `serve` still exits 11 when its drain or the helper cleanup fails, and still prints the `cleanup_unconfirmed` line with the drain's failure as its cause. Exit 1 would count as a stop, and three would open the problem window.
- **No time limit on the server.** The server lets a keyboard write finish before it exits, however long it takes (D-506). The page says so while it waits ("If it is writing to a keyboard, it finishes that first"). After 45 s the page asks `GET /api/restart` again on its own link. A server that answers never restarted (the request was lost on the way), so the page says "keyloom didn't restart, and it is still running." with **Restart keyloom** again. One that doesn't answer gets "keyloom isn't back on this page yet. It may still be writing to a keyboard, so don't unplug it. Choose Open keyloom in the menu bar to see it. If it doesn't open, choose Quit keyloom there, then open keyloom again." The desktop window follows the new server by itself; a browser tab can't, because the new server has a new session link. That state has no button: the page has no channel to the shell. An OK answer whose body the stop cut off counts as a restart, and so does no answer at all; the 45 s check catches the case where it wasn't. In the menu bar, a request that got no answer gives the notice "keyloom didn't confirm the restart", which says it may already be restarting and to quit and open keyloom if it doesn't come back.
- **Rejected: the shell applies Quit's 30 s rule to a restart.** Quit sends SIGTERM and kills the server after 30 s. A kill during a write is the one outcome the drain exists to avoid, so the shell never does it unasked. Quit stays the person's choice.
- **Menu bar.** **Restart keyloom** posts the same route. It is on for a server the shell started, reads "Restart keyloom (it runs in a terminal)" and is off for one it attached to, and is off while there is no server (the problem window's Try again is the way then).
- **Checked** in a real `serve` (`test/cli/serve-process.test.ts`: 202, then exit 11; a terminal server refuses and keeps running) and in a debug build of the shell with its own identifier and a scratch `HOME`, `XDG_DATA_HOME` and data directory: four restarts in a row each logged "stopped to restart, as asked; starting it again" and "server started", with no problem window.
- **Drag and drop** (R5-01). The main window is built with `.disable_drag_drop_handler()`. Tauri's handler answered every native drag itself, for Tauri drag events that nothing here listens to, so WKWebView never saw it (wry 0.57 `wkwebview/drag_drop.rs`: the handler's `true` skips `super`). Checked in the same kind of debug build with a temporary, uncommitted hook. The hook drove WKWebView's own `draggingEntered:`, `draggingUpdated:`, `prepareForDragOperation:`, `performDragOperation:` and `concludeDragOperation:` with a fake `NSDraggingInfo` whose pasteboard held a file; no real mouse moved. With the fix, a Moonlander source zip dropped on the import card reached the page's `drop` with its file and imported the layout. A drop on a layer tab, after a `dragstart` the page dispatched, moved Layer 4 to third place. Without the fix (control build), the same drop on the import card fired no page event at all. The `dragstart` of a real mouse drag was not driven: WebKit starts it from a real mouse, and Tauri's handler never touched the drag source.
- **A drop outside a drop zone is accepted as it is.** The page handles drops only on the import card, the key panel and the layer tabs. Elsewhere WebKit navigates to what was dropped, and the shell's `navigation_route` (`external.rs`) decides: a web link opens in the default browser, as a clicked one does, and a file or any other scheme is blocked. The window stays on keyloom either way. This follows from the code and was not driven in a build.

## 2026-11-11 round 4: sync, MacBook, lab, Docker and editor dead ends

### D-520: A Docker start already running is followed, a Colima a restart can't fix blocks the build, and the editor says why its controls are off. Refines D-511. (Claude, from the round-4 dead-ends review, R4-04, R4-04b, R4-09, R4-12, R4-13, R4-16, R4-17, R4-19, residual 3)

- **Restart refused as busy** (R4-04). `restartDocker` refused while keyloom starts the engine answers with `code: "engine_busy"`, and the readiness answer carries `engineAction` (`start`, `restart` or null) from the same in-process lock (D-511). The page shows the starting view ("Starting Colima…") for an action it didn't start, asks again every 2 s until it ends, and doesn't record the refused restart as its last action, so Restart Colima isn't lost.
- **Colima dead after a restart** (R4-04b). The readiness answer for a dead Colima lists `alternatives`, this platform's other engines without Colima (Docker Desktop and OrbStack on a Mac with Homebrew). After the page's own restart failed, the callout keeps Open Colima's guide and Check again and adds **Download Docker Desktop** and "Or install OrbStack". Build firmware is disabled with "Docker won't start, so a build would stop the same way…", because a build would only fail the same way. A first failed start still leaves Build enabled: Restart Colima is the way on.
- **Delete layer** (R4-09). `layerReferences` gives each combo reference its `comboId` and marks a key whose tap dance switches to the layer. The dialog lists each reference with **Open key**, **Open tap dance** (the key that carries it) or **Open combo**, and Delete is disabled with "Change what switches to {layer} first." beside it, because the validator refuses it while any remain. A tap dance that no key on another layer carries still stops the delete, so it gets its own way out (#290 review): **Open tap dance** on a key of the layer itself that carries it, else **Open combo** for a combo that runs it, else **Remove tap dance** for a dance nothing uses, which removes it in the dialog and lets Delete come back. Every listed reference has a button. Encoder turns aren't listed: a turn can't hold a layer action (`SensorActionSchema`). Open combo never drops a combo card's edits (#290 review): the same combo shows its card as it is, and another combo waits with "Apply or close the combo you are editing first".
- **Empty keyboard search** (R4-12). The QMK and ZMK search says "Searching…", then "QMK and ZMK have no keyboard called “…”", with the contribution guide, in the welcome list and on the new-keyboard card. An empty answer counts only once the search for that text came back.
- **Polish.** A restored keyboard's note has **Resume syncing** (R4-13). A blank config's default name ("{Board} blank", "{Board} daily") takes the first free name, as the import's does (R4-16). Undo's reason while a key is being edited is said under the button in the top bar, and a two-key combo's chips say a combo needs two keys (R4-17). Build firmware waiting for the board revision says "Choose which Moonlander this is first." (R4-19). The sync refusals quote a layout name a shell would split (`commandArg`, residual 3).

### D-519: A keyloom.kbd someone else changed is replaced from the page, the mismatch page sets kanata up, and a dead keyboard helper is replaced on the next try. Refines D-433. (Claude, from the round-4 dead-ends review, residuals 1, 2 and 8, R4-03, R4-14)

- **`foreign_file`** (residual 2). An apply refused because keyloom.kbd on disk isn't keyloom's (`applyKanata` step `not_keyloom`) is now its own sync failure, `foreign_file`, which never retries by itself. The sync line, its toast and the after-Apply card offer **Replace it…** in place of Apply now or Turn remapping on, which would be refused the same way. `replaceForeignRemapperConfig` accepts this case as well as a foreign config kanata runs: kanata reads keyloom's config, or remapping is off, and the board's last sync failed with `foreign_file`. A Disable remapping after that failure makes it stale (#290 review): the engine then drops the run's `errorCode: "foreign_file"`, which is what the page reads, and refuses the replace. The engine keeps that in memory, so a restart forgets it. The after-Apply card offers kanata's setup, not Replace it, while kanata isn't running. The safety rules of chapter 21 stay: only the button replaces it, `applyKanata` keeps the old file as keyloom.kbd.before-keyloom, and a confirm that went stale any other way is still refused as `kanata_not_foreign`. The copy is made only after `kanata --check` passes the new config, so a refused replace changes nothing, and it never writes over an earlier copy (#290 review): the same text is already kept, and different text goes to `keyloom.kbd.before-keyloom.<UTC time>`, so the first original survives a second replace.
- **Mismatch page put-on to a MacBook without kanata** (residual 1). While the sync engine reads kanata as not running, or after a put-on was refused as `kanata_not_running`, the put-on dialog shows kanata's setup checklist (`RemapperSetup`) and hides Put it on, with "Put it on comes back when keyloom sees kanata running." It comes back by itself when kanata answers.
- **Dead helper** (R4-03). `CandidateDeviceOwner` replaces a child whose channel died after setup (`helper_exited`, `protocol_error`, `timeout`) on the next `transport()`, after `shutdown()` of the dead one confirms its exit. Two callers share one replacement. Setup failures stay latched, nothing is replayed, sessions on the dead child stay failed, a dead child whose exit can't be confirmed latches `HelperCleanupUnconfirmedError` and starts nothing, and a replacement never starts after shutdown began. The lab's code for a dead helper is now `connection_lost` with **Try again**. `restart_required`, with no Try again, is kept for the unconfirmed case and for a dead-channel error from a setup the owner latched, which a try would only repeat (#290 review). A helper that dies during the QMK START write reaches the lab inside a `CaptureError`; the lab reads its cause, so that is `connection_lost` too. Replacements back off (#290 review): a helper that dies after every replacement is respawned after 1 s, then 2 s, doubling to 30 s, instead of on each 5 s device poll. Until the wait ends, the dead child's transport is returned and its calls fail as `connection_lost`. A child seen alive resets the wait.
- **Incomplete recording** (R4-14). The session list carries `incomplete`. An incomplete recording in Earlier sessions says it can't be read and is not a button, because analysis refuses it every time.
- **Test leak** (residual 8). `test/cli/flash-interrupt.test.ts` records every fake zapp's pid and kills each child's and each fake zapp's process group after every test.

## 2026-11-11 round 4: desktop links, downloads and updates

### D-516: The desktop shell offers only the ways out that can work: a too-new database gets the update, a download can be stopped, and Sync now waits through busy. Refines D-507 and D-514. (Claude, from the round-4 dead-ends review, R4-05, R4-06, R4-15)

- **A database a newer keyloom upgraded** (R4-05). `keyloom serve` exits 10 with the error code `schema_too_new` (D-455). The shell reads the code from the CLI's JSON error line, never the sentence, because exit 10 also means a protocol mismatch. The problem window says "This app is older than your keyloom data" and offers the same update actions as a newer server in the way: install the update, Download the latest keyloom, Check for updates. It offers no **Try again**, in the window or the menu bar, because the upgraded database stays upgraded and a retry fails the same way. When the device helper's cleanup also fails, the CLI's one JSON error document has the code `cleanup_unconfirmed` and carries the command's own failure as `cause: { code, message }` (#289 review). The shell reads every code on every line, `cause` included, so a cleanup failure can't turn this into Try again.
- **An update download can be stopped and can't hang** (R4-06). While an update downloads, the problem window and the menu bar offer **Stop download**. A download that receives nothing for 60 seconds stops by itself. Both end as a failed install with the reason ("you stopped the download", or "nothing arrived for a minute…"), which offers the same version again and the download page (D-514). There is no limit on the whole download, so a slow connection that keeps receiving still finishes. Once the download is in, Stop is ignored: stopping while the app is being replaced could break it. A stop the person chose sends no failure notification. The problem window's spinner shows while it downloads: the page set `hidden` as a property on its SVG icons, which SVG lacks, so a busy window had shown the warning icon instead.
- **Menu-bar Sync now while keyloom builds or flashes** (R4-15). The server refuses with `busy`. The menu bar now does what the page does (`busy-retry.ts`, R3-18): one notice, "keyloom syncs this keyboard when that finishes", then the request again every 3 seconds for up to 15 minutes. It stops quietly when nothing is waiting to sync any more, and after 15 minutes says it stopped waiting and opens keyloom. Only one Sync now runs at a time, and a panic in it frees the slot (#289 review). A second click logs whether the first is still being sent or is waiting for the build or flash.

### D-515: The desktop window sends other sites to the browser and saves downloads itself. (Claude, from the round-4 dead-ends review, R4-01)

- **The cause.** wry 0.57 refuses a new-window request and a download unless the app sets a handler, and the main window set neither. Every external link in the page is `target="_blank"`, so Download Docker Desktop, Get Codex, Add credits, the key pages, the DFU tool and kanata driver downloads, and the guides did nothing. Firmware Download and Prepare contribution did nothing either, and Prepare contribution then said "Downloaded…".
- **Links** (`desktop/tauri/src-tauri/src/external.rs`). A new-window request for an `http(s)` URL opens in the default browser; nothing else opens, and the window never opens a second page. A link to keyloom's own origin that asks for a new window is refused, not sent to the browser, because its address carries the session token (#289 review). A navigation in the main window stays when it is keyloom's own origin (or, before the first server is up, a loopback page), `about:` or `blob:`; another `http(s)` site goes to the browser and the window keeps keyloom; any other scheme is refused. Checked in the same debug build: a new-window request for the contribution guide and an in-window navigation to github.com each logged "opening https://github.com in the browser" and left the window on keyloom, and a `file:` new-window request was refused. A script can't make WebKit treat its click as the person's, so the build under test let scripts open windows; a real click reaches the same handler. The shell opens the browser with `/usr/bin/open`, as Show log and Download the latest keyloom already do (D-507). The shell plugin's `open` is deprecated in Tauri 2 and would add a second way to do the same thing.
- **Downloads.** Rejected: a server route that writes the file and reveals it, like Show in Finder. It needs a second code path for each download (firmware, contribution) and a way for the page to know it runs in the app. The shell's `on_download` handles every download the page starts, with no page change. It saves only downloads from keyloom's own origin, which are the `blob:` files the page makes, and refuses any other (#289 review): wry hands a `<a download>` link straight to the download handler, past the navigation check. It saves to Downloads under the file's own name, never over an existing file or a dangling symlink ("name (1).zip", as wry numbers it), and Finder shows the saved file. Checked in a debug build of the app on a scratch home and data directory: two Prepare contributions saved `keyloom-board-contribution.zip` and `keyloom-board-contribution (1).zip` (valid zips with the board data and CONTRIBUTION.md), and the shell logged both as saved. A download that fails sends a notification naming the file; a refused one ends without a word. macOS doesn't say where a finished download went, so the shell keeps the path it chose by URL. A second download of the same URL replaces that entry, so the first to finish shows a file and the other is logged as saved, not reported as failed.
- The contribution note names the file it downloaded, so the person can find it.

## 2026-11-11 round 4: chat, board review and proposals

### D-518: A board review the account refused offers the model setup and Try again, its Apply says why it changed nothing, and an unread proposals list is not an empty one. (Claude, from the round-4 dead-ends review, R4-07, R4-08, R4-10)

- **The account, not the review** (R4-10). A review whose model call fails with `auth`, `no_credits`, `permission` or `missing_key` now ends `unavailable`, like a missing provider, instead of `failed`. Trying again with the same key fails the same way; the model setup is the fix. The review keeps `ai.model` for these and keeps it null when no model was set up, so the page tells them apart without reading the reason: "Suggestions need a model, and none is set up." or "The model provider refused this key or account…". Both offer **Set up a model**. For a refused account it opens that provider's key field (the chat's `replacing`). The rest still fail with **Try again**. `ai_status` already allows `unavailable`, and a claim retries it as it retries `failed`. The CLI already reads `unavailable` as "keyloom's own look needs a model".
- **A refused review can run again** (#288 review). An account can be fixed where keyloom can't see it: credits bought at the provider, access granted, an environment key, or Codex signed in again in a terminal. So a refused review also offers **Try again**, and one out of credits offers **Add credits** first, for Anthropic and OpenRouter. A review with no model set up still offers only the setup, because a second look can't succeed until there is one. The page tells "out of credits" by a code, not the words: migration 0022 adds `board_reviews.ai_code`, the model error kind of a refused look (`auth`, `no_credits`, `permission`, `missing_key`), sent as `ai.code`. A claim, a reset and an invalidation clear it. A Codex that is signed out says "Codex isn't signed in…" in the review, not Codex's sentence, which names `codex login` and says to "send the message again".
- **No flags in the page's sentences.** The out-of-credits sentence no longer ends "or switch provider with --provider"; the CLI adds that line itself, as it does for a refused key (D-512). 403, 5xx and other 400 sentences quote the provider's own `error.message` instead of the SDK's "<status> <raw JSON>".
- **Apply on a suggestion** (R4-07). A refusal shows beside Apply, by the server's code: `stale_base` or `validation_failed` say "This suggestion no longer fits your config, so nothing was applied." `not_found` and `usage` say it is no longer open. All four offer **Run a fresh review** (a forced run), never the same Apply. A lost connection or any other refusal offers **Try again**. Pure mapping: `applyFailureWords` in `src/web/review/review-words.ts`.
- **Proposals that couldn't be read** (R4-08). The config's waiting-proposals read no longer becomes `[]` on failure, which read as "Nothing is waiting". The panel says "keyloom couldn't read this config's proposals. Nothing was applied or set aside." with **Try again**, which reads again. An aborted read is ignored. After an Apply the list is read again the same way, so a failed refresh says so too. The board review says the same above its suggestions, with **Try again**, and each suggestion says its change wasn't read, not that it is invalid (#288 review).
- **An Apply whose outcome is unknown** (#288 review). Only an answer of 400 or more proves nothing was applied. No answer, an answer the page couldn't read, or a failure after the Apply landed says "keyloom didn't confirm this Apply, so it may have gone through", keeps Apply off, and offers **Check again**, which reads the proposals again: an Apply that landed takes the suggestion off the list. Until that read answers there is no Apply to press. An Apply that landed but whose new layout couldn't be read says "Applied. keyloom couldn't show the new layout yet." with **Show the new layout**, never "nothing was applied".

### D-517: A chat turn that fails after keyloom accepted it keeps its reason with the run, and the page shows the same way out as a streamed failure. (Claude, from the round-4 dead-ends review, R4-02, R4-11, R4-18)

- **Where the reason lives** (R4-02). Before, a run the provider failed was marked `failed` and the error was dropped (`conversations.ts`, `agent-runs.ts`). The page that watches the run (D-411's saved runs, the default since 26b) had no reason and no button, only "Work stopped with a problem". Now `runPreparedMessage` saves `failure: { code, message }` on the run's presentation as it finishes it failed. The code is the one the chat route already sends for a refusal: `model_<ModelErrorKind>`, or `missing_api_key`. A failure the model's classifier doesn't know is a keyloom bug; it is saved as `agent_error` with a fixed sentence, so no internal text reaches the page. The presentation is chosen over a new `agent_runs` column because it is already the run's saved record. It is in the run snapshot and in the conversation's history, so a reload shows the same box, and it needs no migration. Older recordings have no field.
- **One map from code to buttons.** `presentedTurn` sets `turn.failure` from a failed presentation, for a watched run, a reopened conversation and a streamed `presentation` frame alike. `failureView` (`src/web/chat/failure-words.ts`) gives each code its title and actions. Account failures (`model_no_credits`, `model_auth`, `model_permission`, `missing_api_key`) get **Add credits** where there is a billing page, the setup (**Use another key**, **Change the key** or **Set up the model**), **Switch to …** for each ready provider, and **Try again**. `missing_api_key` shows the page's own sentence, because the server's can name `codex login`. Busy, unreachable, 5xx and other request failures get **Try again**. In a reopened conversation only the last reply keeps its box; an earlier failure's buttons would act on a later message.
- **A full conversation** (R4-11). Anthropic's 400 "prompt is too long" and OpenRouter's "maximum context length" are now `context_full`, not `request`. The same request can never fit again, so the box offers **Start a new conversation** and no Try again. The CLI adds "Start a new conversation (omit --conversation)."
- **Provider switches say when they fail** (R4-18). The three switches in the chat (Switch to … after a failure, Use … in a new conversation, the bar's ready providers) no longer swallow a refused or lost request. The panel says "keyloom couldn't switch to {provider}", that the conversation and its model are unchanged, and offers **Try again**. It scrolls into view, because the switch is pressed higher up. Its Try again belongs to that conversation and is dropped when another opens, and a switch that lands after BK typed something new keeps his words instead of putting the failed message back (#288 review).
- **Try again after prepared work** (#288 review). Sending the message again starts the request over, and the model doesn't see what the stopped reply prepared, because a failed turn's messages are not stored. So it can prepare the same proposals and priorities again. The box now says what this reply prepared: "Before it stopped, this reply prepared 2 proposals and a priority. They are above. Asking again starts over and can prepare the same again." The button reads **Ask again**. A run whose `effectsMayHaveOccurred` is set with nothing shown asks BK to check first, also with **Ask again**. A run that prepared nothing says nothing more and keeps **Try again**. A reply with no record of its effects keeps the careful sentence. Pure mapping: `preparedWords` in `src/web/chat/failure-words.ts`. Telling the model about the earlier proposals was not chosen: it needs a server change to every resend, and the honest words fix the dead end now.
- **Smaller ways out** (#288 review). A `model_request` refusal can repeat in the same conversation, so it also offers **Start a new conversation**. Start a new conversation puts the message that couldn't be answered back in the composer, unless BK has typed newer words. The box's buttons are off while a reply is running. Codex's "ran out of room in the model's context window" (`codex-rs/protocol/src/error.rs`) is `context_full`, as it is for the other providers. A failed switch scrolls into view once per failure, not on every render.
- **Reproducible without a model.** `KEYLOOM_FAKE_CHAT=fail_after_accept:<kind>` in `scripts/dev-editor.ts` accepts each message as a saved run and fails it with that provider failure. The kinds are auth, no_credits, permission, rate_limit, overloaded, server, connection and context_full. The failure is built as the SDK raises it and classified by the real classifier (`test/helpers/model-failures.ts`), the same failures `test/app/agent-run-failure.test.ts` runs through a real accepted run.

## 2026-11-11 round 3: Docker restarts, builds stop, and the CLI names its next step

### D-512: The CLI's failures name the command that gets past them, and the page's shared sentences name none. (Claude, from the round-3 dead-ends review, R3-11, R3-25)

- `keyloom flash` for a MacBook layout says to use `keyloom sync <layout>`. The QMK bootloader timeout names the wait `--wait` set. `flash --check` adds a `next` row: `keyloom flash --build <build>`, or plug in and check again. A removed keyboard's sync refusal names `keyloom keyboard restore <board>`. The kanata doctor's Karabiner fix names `keyloom import --board apple/macbook-ansi <karabiner.json>`, and its keyboard-name fix no longer says "S36"; nor does the board check. A refused kanata config names `keyloom remap apply <layout> --overwrite` in the terminal only; `applyKanata`'s own sentence reaches the sync page, so it says "Move it aside, or replace it and keep a copy" and names no command (#285 review). A layout name in a suggested command is quoted when a shell would split it (`commandArg`). The piped-key example names the provider being set.
- **A layout that no longer fits its board** fails `keyloom build` with `keyloom version list <config>` and `keyloom version restore <config> <version>`. Only `buildAndWait` (the CLI) adds this; the page has its own Go back (D-502). `version restore`'s help no longer mentions a CLI `undo --to`, and D-502's CLI line now names `version restore`.
- **A refused API key.** The model's sentence is shared with the chat page, so it names the environment variable and no command. The CLI's error output and `doctor` add `keyloom provider key set`.
- **Zapp on Linux.** Homebrew is the Mac's install. On Linux the missing-Zapp sentences (the flasher, `FlashToolMissingError`, doctor) name ZSA's releases page and its udev rules, as Zapp's README does (checked 2026-11-11). The other DFU tools name their own pages there.

### D-511: A Colima that won't start is restarted from scratch, and a running build can be cancelled. Refines D-486 and D-501. (Claude, from the round-3 dead-ends review, R3-04, R3-10, R2-13, R3-13, R3-14, R3-20)

- **Restart Colima, not the same start again** (R3-04). A failed `colima start`, or one whose daemon is still down at the deadline, failed for a reason another start doesn't change. The readiness answer carries `restartEngine: "Colima"` for a down Colima, and the page offers **Restart Colima** where it offered Try again: after a failed Start, after an install whose first start failed, and after a build that stopped at Docker. `POST /api/system/docker/restart` now also takes this case. It runs `colima stop --force` and then `colima start` (with the context's `--profile`) and waits for Docker. That is Colima's own fix for a VM that won't start (colima.run/docs/faq, checked 2026-11-11) and what restored the daemon in docs/research/workload-evaluator.md. A stop that fails is logged and the start still runs, because a VM that never came up has nothing to stop. A Docker that answers, or refuses this account, is never restarted this way: that could stop an auto-sync's build. **Nor is a slow one** (#285 review): `docker version` timing out means the daemon is there and may be busy (D-497), so readiness offers no restart for `slow`, `restartDocker` refuses it with "Docker is answering slowly, so it may be running a build", and a start that ends with Docker still slow says so instead of offering a restart. If the restart fails too, the page shows its reason without the terminal hint, **Open Colima's guide** and **Check again**, and no button that repeats it.
- **One engine action at a time** (#285 review). The page's Start, the page's Restart and a build's own start share one in-process lock (`holdEngine` in `src/app/toolchain.ts`). A restart runs `colima stop --force`, which would kill a start in progress, so a restart while anything holds the lock is refused with "keyloom is already starting Docker…". A start waits its turn, then finds Docker up and returns at once; a cancelled build stops waiting. The lock covers this process only; another keyloom's build still sees a restart's stop, as before.
- **No 10 s cut-off** (#285 review). Bun ends a request whose handler sends nothing for 10 s (its default `idleTimeout`). On Bun 1.3.14 that cut a request with no body and spared one with a JSON body, which is what the page sends, so the Docker start and restart routes call `server.timeout(req, 0)` rather than rely on that.
- **The terminal hint stays in the terminal.** `DockerStartResult` keeps the engine's `hint` ("run `colima restart` in a terminal") apart from its `reason`. The CLI (`doctor --start-docker`, `--restart-docker`) and the build log print both. The page's start answer leaves the hint out only where it offers Restart; other engines keep theirs. `doctor --start-docker` now prints why a failed start failed (R3-11).
- **Cancel build** (R3-10). `POST /api/builds/:id/cancel` aborts the build's own `AbortController`, which `startBuild` combines with any caller's signal. The build's Docker start, checkout or container stops, and the drivers' existing `onKill` removes the container by name (#123, #276). The route answers `202` at once with the build as it is, and the page's poll sees it end as `errorCode: "cancelled"` and "Build stopped."; the dialog keeps **Stopping** until then. A build waiting for another build's checkout lock stops waiting at once (`withLock` takes the build's signal) instead of after the 20–30 minute lock timeout. A build never flashes, so a cancel can't reach a keyboard; a sync whose build was stopped sees a failed build and doesn't flash. A finished build, or one running in another keyloom, answers `409 build_finished`. The dialog shows **Cancel build** while it builds, and a stopped build returns to the Build firmware step with "Build stopped."; the header reads "Build and flash" again.
- **A Cancel that didn't arrive says so** (R3-20). A flash's or a build's Cancel that never reached keyloom says "keyloom didn't answer…" and keeps Cancel on screen.
- **The poll recovers by itself** (R2-13). After three missed polls the dialog says keyloom stopped answering, keeps asking, and removes the line when keyloom answers again.
- **A running install is found on load** (R3-13). `useInstall` asks once when it mounts and follows an install that is already running, in the release dialog and in the board review's activity tests. A finished one isn't brought back.
- **One ready line** (R3-14). Docker installed from the activity tests says "Docker is installed and running. Test again to see the results." once.

## 2026-11-11 round 3: accessibility, desktop and web polish

### D-514: The desktop shell says why an update didn't install, shows the server's last lines, and answers a refused Sync now. Refines D-481 and D-507. (Claude, from the round-3 dead-ends review, R3-15 to R3-17)

- **A failed update install says why and stays offered** (R3-15). The download shows its progress in the menu item and the problem window ("Downloading version 0.80.0: 40%", in 5% steps). If it fails, the notification gives the updater's reason. The same version stays offered as **Couldn't install VERSION. Try again**, and the menu adds **Download the latest keyloom** (the releases page) until the next try. Before, the shell checked again at once and lost the reason. In the problem window the state is `InstallFailed`, with **Try installing VERSION again**, **Download the latest keyloom**, **Try again** and **Show log**.
- **"keyloom keeps stopping" shows the last lines** (R3-16). The shell keeps the last 12 lines the running server wrote, in memory, without blank lines or any line that carries the session token. The window shows them under "What keyloom said", opened. The whole output stays in the log.
- **A refused menu-bar Sync now is a notification and opens keyloom** (R3-17). The server's refusal sentences name CLI commands, so the notification doesn't repeat them. It says "keyloom didn't sync" and that keyloom is open with what to do, and the shell brings the window forward, where the keyboard's status has the button. A server that didn't answer gets the same, with "try Sync now again".
- Not exercised in a running app: checked by `cargo test`, `cargo clippy` (with and without `updater`) and the view tests.

### D-513: Every page has one h1 and named controls, and the last web dead ends get a button. Refines D-508. (Claude, from the round-3 dead-ends review, accessibility, R3-07, R3-08, R3-12, R3-23, R3-24, R1-23)

- **Accessibility.** The wall has a visually hidden h1 ("Your keyboards"). Whole-page errors (no token, a refused token, a layout that can't load, board dev) use `ErrorState level={1}`. The chat bar has a hidden h2 ("Ask keyloom"), so its h3s ("Chat needs a model") keep their order. **Ask keyloom** keeps its name on a phone, where its words are hidden. The phone top bar keeps the Home icon. The page links the app icon (`src/web/favicon.png`, a copy of the desktop app's 64 px icon), and `/favicon.ico` serves the same file instead of a 404. Result: the orchestrator's axe sweep went from 22 violations on 22 runs to 0.
- **A refused environment key in the desktop app** gives the profile line to copy and "quit keyloom and open it again", not a terminal `serve` command (R3-07), because D-443 reads the profile at launch.
- **A board preview that fails** stops "Reading…", says why and offers **Try again** (R3-08).
- **Read it from this Mac with no Karabiner settings** (`karabiner_missing`) offers **Start blank**, which makes "MacBook daily" from the board's blank layout and opens it (R3-12).
- **Install Codex** (R3-24). Replaces D-508's "installing Codex from the app waits". `codex` is an installable tool (`brew install --cask codex`, a cask since 2026). The chat status says `canInstall` for a missing Codex where Homebrew runs. A locked Codex that isn't installed then offers **Install Codex** through the install job, with its log, **Cancel**, and on failure **Try again**, **Get Codex** and **Check again**. Elsewhere it keeps **Get Codex**.
- **Smaller ways out** (R3-23, R1-23). A failed board.json reload in board dev says so above the last board, with **Try again**. Try again in Manage keyboards repeats the action that failed (remove, restore, the contribution download), not a reload of the list. Layer actions show why they are off on their own line, and **+** shows its reason beside it. The empty combos list takes an `onNew` for **New combo**. The Viewer still has to pass it. "Ask the chat" in Settings' read-only list opens the chat through `openPlace`.
- **Not done here, in R3-A's files:** the Viewer's `onNew` for combos, keeping Compare open when **Go back** fails, the configs dialog's silent version-list failure, and the lab delete that drops the row on failure.

## 2026-11-11 round 3: sync, configs, MacBook and lab

### D-510: Every refusal and failure in sync, configs, the MacBook and the typing lab offers its fix as a button. Extends D-495 and D-501. (Claude, from the round-3 dead-ends review, R3-01 to R3-03, R3-05, R3-06, R3-09, R3-18, R3-19, R3-21, R3-22 and R1-19)

- **A refused put-on keeps its code** (R3-01). The configs backend translates `SyncRefusal` before `UsageError`, as the sync backend does, so `/api/configs/:ref/use` answers with the engine's code. The put-on and put-version confirms render `refusalNotice(code)` and replace their confirm button with the fix: **Build and flash…**, **Replace it…**, **Open keyboard status** or **Restore** (which restores the config being put on). The CLI sentence, which names commands, is never shown. A put-on of another config refused as `foreign_firmware` offers **Open {config}**, because Build and flash on this page builds the layout on screen. On the mismatch page, which has neither keyboard status nor Build and flash, a refusal whose fix is elsewhere offers **Open {config}** for the config being put on, and the words name the button that page has; `kanata_not_running` there keeps its words only, since the keyboard's status lives on the keyboard's config, which is that page. While the page edits a config that isn't on the keyboard (EditingStatus in place of the pill), **Open keyboard status** opens the keyboard's config with its status open, and **Replace it…** opens its confirm there too. A successful Replace it makes the config it put on the board's config, so a put-on refused as `kanata_foreign` ends with that config on the keyboard and syncing.
- **After an Apply on a MacBook** whose kanata isn't running, the card offers **Open keyboard status** (where the checklist is; it fits a kanata that was never set up and one that stopped), and one whose kanata runs a foreign config offers **Replace it…** (R3-03). Apply now is offered only where it can work.
- **A failed auto-sync build carries its build's code** (R3-05). The sync run view gains `errorCode` from the build row (D-501's `docker_unavailable`). The line, the toast and the menu-bar notification say "Docker isn't ready" and the toast offers **Build and flash…**, whose callout has the button for each Docker state (D-499). A toast about a keyboard other than the one on screen offers **Open {keyboard}** instead, because Build and flash acts on the layout on screen. Docker's own sentence stays in the build log: it can name a terminal fix, and Colima's "quit your app" is wrong for Colima.
- **The mismatch page** (R3-06) reads the board's configs. The config on its keyboard has no Archive, says why, and offers **Put {other} on it…** (the most recently changed other config) through the same confirm. After that, Archive comes back.
- **Sync now while keyloom is busy** (R3-18) asks again every 3 s for up to 15 minutes, while the page is open, and the toast says "keyloom syncs this when that finishes." Past the limit it says it stopped waiting and offers **Open keyboard status**. A put-on refused as busy keeps its confirm, because pressing it later can succeed. The time limit is checked before every retry, so a tab the browser suspended past it never syncs late; an aborted wait cancels its request and drops a late answer; a wait that ends clears "keyloom syncs this when that finishes"; and leaving the layout page stops the wait. Rejected: queueing in the engine, because a CLI engine exits before the queue runs and a put-on would have to store the choice before it can run.
- **Typing lab.** A failed Apply of a lab fix says why beside the button (R3-02): a changed layout (`stale_base`, `validation_failed`) or a fix that is no longer proposed (`usage`) offers **Type again**, anything else **Try again**. A past session's fix that was applied, replaced or turned down says so and offers **Type again** in place of Apply, because the server refuses it every time. macOS refusing raw HID is `permission_input_monitoring` and offers **Open Input Monitoring settings** and **Try again** (R3-09, D-492). Firmware keyloom didn't build is `foreign_firmware`, and the lab offers **Build and flash…** only, because a sync never replaces it (R1-19); a ZMK central that isn't on USB is `not_connected`. A failed session delete keeps the row and says why.
- **Polish.** A restored keyboard's toast offers **Resume syncing**, and a failed restore **Open Your keyboards** (R3-19). Karabiner-Elements' rules step offers **Open Karabiner-Elements** when the doctor found the app, and stays in the view while kanata runs, when the conflict matters (R3-21); the open-settings route takes `karabiner_elements` as one more fixed target, never a path from the page. A wall tile keyloom can't draw offers **Open {layout}**, by the layout's name, as the tile that failed to load does (R3-22). With D-513 merged, the empty combo list's **New combo** opens a combo card with no keys yet and picks them on the board, and the mismatch page's title is its page's h1.

### D-509: The exit-chord proof counts only within ten minutes of keyloom seeing kanata run its config. Refines D-235 and D-497. (Claude, from the round-3 dead-ends review, R3-00)

- **The gap.** D-497 recorded the proof whenever kanata's port refused the connection. A kanata that never started refuses the same way, so on a fresh MacBook setup one click on "I held it and kanata stopped" turned auto-apply on without the chord ever being shown to work.
- **The rule.** The sync engine records `board_sync.kanata_seen_at` (migration 0021) on every read that finds kanata running a config keyloom wrote: `keyloom.kbd` with a keyloom revision, including setup's identity file, or `safe.kbd` with remapping off. It writes only while the board is unproven. The proof is recorded only when kanata's port refuses now and `kanata_seen_at` is no more than ten minutes old (`EXIT_CHORD_WINDOW_MS`); otherwise the button is refused with `kanata_not_seen` and nothing is recorded. A kanata running someone else's config doesn't count, because the proof is for the setup keyloom will auto-apply onto.
- **Why ten minutes.** Seeing kanata run takes one 5-second poll. Reading the steps, holding the chord and checking that the keyboard types plainly takes a minute or two. Ten minutes leaves room for that and for an interruption, and still ties the stop to a kanata keyloom saw. A shorter window would refuse a person who read the steps slowly; a longer one would accept a kanata that stopped for another reason long ago. A time in the future (a clock moved back) doesn't count.
- **The page.** The sync view carries `auto.exitChordReady`. The popover shows the button only while it is true, and otherwise says why beside the switch: start kanata first, or replace a config keyloom didn't write. `kanata_not_seen` offers **Open keyboard status**.
- **The CLI.** `keyloom remap confirm-exit-chord` is two runs: run while kanata runs keyloom's config, it records the sighting, says to hold the chord and run it again within ten minutes, and exits 0 (`--json` says `recorded: false`, `next: "hold_exit_chord"`); run after the chord, it records the proof. A stopped kanata keyloom hasn't seen is refused with what to do. This replaces D-497's note that the CLI's command was the person's own statement.

## 2026-11-11 round 2: the desktop app, chat setup and copy offer the way out

### D-508: A provider fixed at startup is the only one the setup offers, and copy that names a place goes there. Refines D-496. (Claude, from the round-2 dead-ends review, R2-04, R2-12, R2-16, R2-17, R1-23 to R1-30)

- **A lock leaves one provider.** With `KEYLOOM_PROVIDER` or `--provider` set, `ModelSetup` (chat's "Chat needs a model", the review's **Set up a model**, the picker) lists only that provider and says "keyloom was started with KEYLOOM_PROVIDER, so only Codex answers." Saving another provider's key could never change who answers. The picker lists the one provider, shows its setup, and offers **Close** instead of a choice to save. `lockedProvider`, `lockWords` and `setupProviders` in `src/web/chat/setup-words.ts` decide it from the status.
- **A locked Codex that isn't installed** shows "The Codex CLI isn't installed on this computer" with **Get Codex** (the Codex CLI page) and **Check again**. keyloom's install job (D-488) installs Docker and the flashing tools only; installing Codex from the app waits for that job to cover it.
- **Copy offers only what exists** (R2-12). "Chat needs a model" names Codex sign-in only when the Codex CLI is installed, and a pasted key only where there is a keyring (`setupLead`).
- **A refusing key store has a way round it** (R2-16). On `key_store_failed` the field stays, so **Save and use it** is the Try again, and the setup adds the environment variable to copy: in the desktop app `export ANTHROPIC_API_KEY=<your key>` for the shell profile, then quit and reopen (D-443 reads the profile at launch); in a browser the `serve` command with the variable.
- **An import whose answer the page can't read** has the code `imported_unreadable`: the card shows **Reload** and takes no second file, which would import the layout again (R2-17).
- **Import cards link their editors** (Oryx, the MoErgo Layout Editor). The MacBook card's **Read it from this Mac** reads `~/.config/karabiner/karabiner.json` through `GET /api/system/karabiner-config` (404 `karabiner_missing`, 413 `karabiner_too_large`, 403 `karabiner_denied` when macOS refuses the read, 422 `karabiner_not_a_file` for anything but a regular file, so a FIFO is never opened) and imports it like a dropped file, because a file picker hides that folder (R1-24).
- **A place named in copy opens it** (R1-23). "in Settings" (the key's LED popover, the layer's look) and "Ask the chat" (a setting the sliders can't show) are links. They dispatch `openPlace` (`src/web/shell/open-place.ts`), which the Viewer turns into the same sidebar change its own buttons make, instead of a callback threaded through LayerTabs and the key panel. A full conversation offers **Start a new conversation**.
- **No offer that can't be taken** (R1-25, R1-26, R1-30). The setup card hides "Put one of my configs on it" when there are no configs. KeyPanel shows why Apply is disabled beside it, not only to screen readers. The keyboard welcome and collection describe sharing a keyboard through **Prepare contribution** in Manage keyboards, not `/add-keyboard` in a clone.

### D-507: The desktop app supervises its server and never ends at a menu-bar line. Replaces the "keyloom could not start" summary of D-454 as the only output. (Claude, from the round-2 dead-ends review, R2-03, R2-09, R2-15)

- **A problem window.** When the server can't start, or keeps stopping, the shell opens a window (`desktop/tauri/ui/problem.html`, `src/problem.rs`) that says what happened in plain words, shows the server's last lines under "What keyloom said", and offers **Try again** and **Show log**. The menu bar adds **Try starting keyloom again**, and the CLI's replace offer (D-454) is a button too. The page has no Tauri IPC: the shell writes its state into the URL fragment, and each button is a `keyloom-desktop://` link the window's navigation handler catches and cancels. One click starts one attempt (`awaiting`).
- **A newer keyloom in the way** (the CLI offers no replace, D-454) checks for an app update at once. The window then offers **Install VERSION and restart keyloom**, or, with no update or no updater in the build, **Download the latest keyloom** (the releases page) and **Check for updates**.
- **A server the shell started is started again when it exits** (R2-09), through the same `sidecar::start` path: after 1 s, 5 s, then 15 s, at most three times in ten minutes (`src/restarts.rs`). A fourth stop in that window opens the problem window ("keyloom keeps stopping") instead of looping, and a restart that fails to start opens it at once. An attached server that stops answering counts the same way. The supervisor (`supervise` in `lib.rs`) is the one place that starts the server, at launch and after a stop. Quitting sets a flag first and wakes a restart that is waiting, so the supervisor never starts the server Quit is stopping; a server that starts as Quit begins is stopped by the launch itself. An exited server leaves the shell's state as soon as the watcher sees it go, so Quit never signals a PID the system may have reused. A launch that prints no URL in time is sent SIGTERM, so Try again never starts a second server beside it. An automatic restart points the window at the new server without showing a window the person closed. The page's "Reconnecting…" banner (D-494) stays as the fallback for a browser tab.
- **The shell keeps a log** at `~/Library/Logs/dev.planetaryescape.keyloom/keyloom.log`, moved aside past 1 MB at launch: its own lines and the server's output, minus any line with the session token. **Show log** opens it. An app opened from Finder has no terminal, so before this the only record of a failed start was lost.
- **A finished update check leaves an enabled item** (R2-15): **Check for updates** after an empty check, **Couldn't check for updates. Try again** after a failed one. While an install downloads, the problem window shows it in progress instead of an Install button that would do nothing. A failed install checks again at once, which offers the same version, so the install item comes back.
- **Not exercised live.** The window, the restart and the update items were checked by `cargo clippy` with and without the updater, `cargo test` on the view table and the restart policy, and by rendering `problem.html` with the shell's fragment in Chrome. The `.app` itself is built by CI's release job, not here.

## 2026-11-11 round 2: flash and boards (dead ends)

### D-506: A flash can't be stopped once the keyboard is in its bootloader, by Cancel, a sync's stop or a pause. Corrects D-501's Cancel and D-497's stop for the Moonlander. (Claude, from the independent review of #280)

- **Zapp writes without a word.** BK's three hands-free Moonlander flashes (`~/.local/share/keyloom/logs/flash-fl_01M5GPYDR82XT7CVG754SGXNTG.log`, `…JC2HWQDWYDPPGE5BN3KK1C`, `…K41CQ8N3DZ5E8HKDYVHP44`, 2026-10-22 and 23) each log `Firmware loaded`, then keyloom's own "the keyboard is in its bootloader", then `zapp exited 0`. Zapp prints nothing while it writes. keyloom waited for a line after `Firmware loaded` before it called the flash `writing`, so the dialog showed **Cancel** and "Nothing has been written to the keyboard yet" for the whole write, and a Cancel sent SIGTERM to Zapp mid-write. On the manual path the sync row stayed `needs_hand`, so `SyncEngine.stop()` and pause aborted it too.
- **The bootloader is the write.** `flashWithZapp` calls the flash `writing` as soon as the hands-free restart succeeds, or, on the manual path, as soon as the board's bootloader is on USB: it looks every 250 ms from `Firmware loaded` (`bootloaderPresent`, from the revision's `flash.bootloader`). An abort, from Cancel, a CLI Ctrl-C, a sync's stop or pause, or the 2-minute bootloader timeout, stops Zapp only after the flasher has looked at USB itself and seen no bootloader. One that comes while keyloom's restart command is in flight waits for its result. A refused abort is logged, and the flash finishes.
- **UF2 boards.** Once keyloom has restarted a half itself, that half is written whoever asks to stop, because it won't type until it is. The flasher says so with an `inBootloader` note, and an abort that came during the restart stays refused for the rest of the flash. A half already in its bootloader, or one the person put there, can still be cancelled before its drive appears, as before.
- **Cancel's answer is the flasher's.** `cancelFlash` refuses once the flash is writing or has said `inBootloader`. When it aborts, it waits for the flasher to stop or to say it is writing, and answers `409 flash_writing` for the second. A sync whose stop the flasher refused records the write's own failure, not "cancelled".
- The fake Zapp in the tests is now silent after `Firmware loaded`, as the real one is. Tests cover the manual reset, a cancel that comes before keyloom's own look at USB, a sync's stop signal, a Glove80 cancel during the restart, and the sync engine's record.
- **After a second re-check, no stop cuts off a write, from a terminal or a shutdown either.**
  - **Flash tools run in their own process group.** Zapp, dfu-util, dfu-programmer and avrdude start with `processGroup: true`. Before, a terminal's Ctrl-C went to the whole foreground group, so it reached the flash tool directly and killed it mid-write, whatever keyloom decided.
  - **keyloom stays up while a write runs.** The flashers mark the write window in a process-wide guard (`src/firmware/toolchain/write-guard.ts`): from keyloom restarting the keyboard, or the bootloader appearing, until the flash tool exits or the half's drive unmounts. The CLI's `untilInterrupted` and `keyloom serve` keep catching SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP while it is held, and print "keyloom is writing to the keyboard, and stopping now would leave it half written. It stops when the write ends." With no write under way, the first signal stops the work and a second ends keyloom at once, as before.
  - **The DFU tools refuse a cancel once they write,** as Zapp does. Only the write timeout stops them.
  - **A keyboard that only left USB is not a write.** `rebootToBootloader` now says `leftUsbOnly` when it saw the keyboard go but not its bootloader arrive. That keyboard may have been unplugged, so the flash stays cancellable until the bootloader is on USB or Zapp prints. Before, Cancel, stop and pause were refused, and the 3-minute write timeout ended with a false "left in its bootloader".
  - **A cancel before Zapp's `Firmware loaded` is read never restarts the keyboard.** Zapp is already stopped, so a restart would strand the keyboard in its bootloader.
  - **A reset pressed while keyloom stops Zapp is reported.** After a cancel or the bootloader timeout, the flasher looks at USB again. A bootloader there means the result says the keyboard is in its bootloader and must be flashed again, not "Nothing was flashed". The page's **Flash to keyboard** follows it.
  - **A ZMK cancel while keyloom checks the halves restarts nothing.** The flasher checks the cancel before the restart hook, and `restartHalf` checks it again just before the 1200-baud touch.
  - **Shutdown cancels flashes that still wait.** `drainManualFlashTasks` aborts every flash that hasn't committed, and the flasher's own USB check decides, as for Cancel. Before, a flash waiting for a reset held the shutdown with no limit, and the desktop app's SIGKILL after 30 s left Zapp running with no parent, to flash whenever the keyboard next entered its bootloader.
  - **A UF2 copy that has started finishes.** A stop during a manual half's copy waits for its drive to unmount, then ends the flash before the next half. Before, it ended the wait at once with "the bootloader didn't take the file".
  - **A Cancel that didn't stop the flash says why.** The dialog shows the `flash_writing` refusal in plain words, and a Cancel still settling says keyloom writes a keyboard that has just entered its bootloader first.
  - **A USB read that fails proves nothing.** If keyloom can't read USB after three tries, it doesn't stop Zapp, which may be writing to a keyboard keyloom can't see. A Cancel then leaves the flash running, and the 2-minute timeout gives Zapp the 3-minute write window and then stops it with the write timeout's message.
  - **A sync's stop that the UF2 flasher refused is not recorded as a cancel.** The engine keeps the flasher's latest state, so a half keyloom restarted as the stop arrived records the copy's own failure. A later half that waits for a hand can still be cancelled.
- **After the final review of #280:**
  - **A ZMK restart holds the write guard.** From the moment the restart hook announces it is restarting a half until it returns, keyloom holds the guard, so a second Ctrl-C in `restartHalf`'s 15-second wait for the drive no longer ends keyloom with the half in its bootloader. The checks before the announce don't hold it, so a second Ctrl-C still leaves a check that hangs.
  - **A ZMK stop says where the half is.** `restartHalf` marks a failure that came after its 1200-baud touch with `touched`. A stop then says keyloom had already restarted the half, with the reason, and to flash again if it doesn't type. A stop as a half's drive mounts says the half is in its bootloader now. The DFU flasher looks at USB again after a cancel in the same way. Before, all of these said "Cancelled. Nothing was flashed."
  - **Zapp with USB unreadable holds the guard.** When a cancel can't read USB and leaves Zapp running, keyloom holds the guard until Zapp ends, so a second Ctrl-C can't orphan Zapp in its own process group. The bootloader and write timers still end it within a fixed time. A result that can't read USB after the stop says keyloom couldn't check, not that nothing was flashed. The guard is released in a `finally`, so a flash that throws can't leave `serve` ignoring Ctrl-C.
  - **Shutdown follows the flasher's current state.** `drainManualFlashTasks` skips only a flash that is writing or has a half in its bootloader now, as the sync engine does, so a split flash whose next half waits for a hand is cancelled and doesn't hold the shutdown for that half's whole wait. The page's **Cancel** still refuses once any half was written (D-501).
- **After the bot review of #280:** **Check the keyboard again** answers `missing_halves` before `later_build`, so a split board says to connect every half before it says there is nothing to flash. A check that answers after another flash started is dropped, and the check button waits while a flash starts. The Apply card's **Build and flash** no longer passes the click as a revision. The mismatch page asks for the fitting revision again when the head changes, and a failed lookup says so and offers **Look again**.
- **After the second bot review of #280:**
  - **Nothing changes the write guard after Zapp exits.** A USB read that returns after Zapp has exited no longer starts a write, takes a hold, arms a timer or sends a state.
  - **A cancel holds the guard from its USB check.** The hold starts before the check and lasts until Zapp stops or finishes. Before, a second Ctrl-C during the check ended keyloom and orphaned Zapp in its own process group. A child-process test sends both signals.
  - **The DFU flasher's writing callback runs inside the guard's `finally`.** A state listener that throws can't leave the guard held.
  - **A shutdown that finds a flash writing cancels it at its next wait.** `drainManualFlashTasks` never cuts off a write, as before. It marks that flash, and the flash is cancelled when it next waits with nothing in its bootloader, such as a split board's next half. Before, a shutdown during the first half's write waited out the second half's whole wait.
  - **Written but not confirmed can be checked again.** When Zapp exits 0 and only the check that the keyboard came back as this build fails, the flash fails with `errorCode: "not_confirmed"`. That flash is `checkable`. **Check the keyboard again** and `keyloom flash --check` read it. A keyboard on this build turns the flash into the verified success it was and records the version. The dialog shows the check with **Flash again** beside it, and the header says "Flashed, not verified". A flash that failed before its write finished stays ineligible. The CLI's failure names `keyloom flash --check <flash>`.
  - **A check never records a stale reading.** It is refused while another flash runs. A flash that started during the read discards it: the log and History stay as they were, and the person is asked to check again. The dialog drops a Cancel answer for a flash that another flash has replaced on screen.
  - **Kept:** after an Apply to a config that isn't on the keyboard, a physical keyboard keeps **Build and flash**, not **Put this layout on it**. A standalone flash is a choice D-300 and chapter 25 keep, and the keyboard isn't left untracked without the person seeing it: the header reads `behind: other_config`, says the keyboard runs a different config, and offers to put the stored one on it.

### D-502: A layout that no longer fits its board offers the last revision that did. (Claude, from BK's dead-ends request, R1-20)

- History lives in the editor, and the editor can't draw a layout whose layers don't fit the board, so **Open History** can't work from the mismatch page. The page asks `GET /api/layouts/:ref/fitting-revision` for the newest earlier revision whose every layer has one binding per key, and offers **Go back to the last revision that matched**. That is the existing undo with `to` (D-170): a new revision, checked against the rules, so the broken one stays in History. A board whose own drawing disagrees with its key count fits no revision, so the page keeps only **Show your other layouts** and **Archive this config**.
- Not in the CLI yet (D-030): `keyloom undo` steps back one revision and `keyloom version restore` goes back to a named version, but no command finds the fitting revision or goes back to an arbitrary one.
- **After review:** a refused **Go back** is not offered again as it was. A `409 stale_base` (the layout gained a revision since the page loaded) turns the button into **Reload this layout**; any other refusal, such as the rules refusing that revision, removes the button and leaves its reason on the page.

### D-501: A flash's problems are codes with buttons, a waiting flash can be cancelled, and an unconfirmed one can be checked again. The MacBook keyboard never opens Build and flash. (Claude, from BK's dead-ends request: R2-01, R1-14, R1-15, R1-29)

- **The board revision question has its own code.** `startBuild` throws `BoardRevisionNeededError`, which the editor route answers `400 board_revision_needed`. Only that code makes the dialog ask "Which Moonlander is this?"; any other `usage` refusal shows its sentence. Before this, a kanata board's refusal was read as the question, and the MacBook keyboard looped on it.
- **The MacBook keyboard is never built.** Its page mounts no Build and flash: no header button, no menu item, no dialog and no Docker readiness request (`ReleaseHost` is not mounted for a kanata board). The line after an Apply says what really happens on the keyboard: **Apply now** for a MacBook without auto-apply, "Auto-apply puts it on…" with it, "Auto-sync puts it on…" for a board that auto-syncs, **Put this layout on it** when the keyboard runs another layout, and **Build and flash** otherwise. Apply from chat closes the chat, so the same line and button ride on the Applied toast, and the proposal card's own block says the same. The Proposals panel no longer tells a MacBook owner to Build and flash.
- **Permissions are codes.** A UF2 write macOS refuses (EACCES or EPERM on macOS) fails with `errorCode: "permission_files_and_folders"`, stored in `flashes.error_code` (migration 0020).
