Grammar and the reading rule are one pure module, `src/layout/references.ts`: three layers read it and none of them may import another's folder. A string that is not a marker — an unknown kind, a name, a key with no position, a brace that never closes, an id longer than any real one — stays exactly as the model wrote it.
- **The stored text keeps the markers.** Nothing rewrites a stored message (`src/persistence/conversations.ts`); no sanitiser pass runs over assistant text. Each client parses where it renders, which is why the marker stays short: an older client shows the marker itself rather than losing the sentence.
- **Resolved by the client, with a words fallback.** A marker that names something live becomes the thing's own name; one that names nothing becomes plain words, never a dead link. Where no name exists the words are the id the marker carried (a deleted config, a proposal this conversation does not hold, a layer gone after a head change), and a proposal that is no longer waiting reads as its title with where it stands (`applied`, `superseded`, `rejected`). A key whose position is past the layer's keys reads `key 73 on Base`, as the review's cleaner already wrote it.
- **Each kind does one thing, through a seam that exists.** Config → the config opener that already carries the thread (D-317, D-336); the config already on screen reads as words, so the page never reloads onto itself. Proposal → the reply's own card, or the conversation's, pinned on the board (D-452). Layer → the layer switch the layer strip uses, which keeps an open key's draft (D-317). Key → that layer switch and then the pulse a real key press lights, so the key is found in place; the key panel does not open and the composer's selection does not change. A name is only a link when its action exists.
- **A carried read-only thread keeps only its config marker live.** Its layers, keys and proposals belong to a layout that is not on screen: pinning one would preview another config's change on this board. Opening a config it names is exactly what the thread's config cards already offer. Those markers read as words ("that layer", "that key", "that proposal") rather than as ids, because they name things that exist and the page simply cannot draw them; a thread handed back to the config it is about (D-317 keeps `from`) resolves everything again.
- **Markdown-lite grows one inline kind.** `src/web/chat/markdown.ts` parses `reference` after inline code, so a marker in backticks stays literal, and bold keeps a marker inside it as a reference. The component builds a `<button>`, never an `<a href>` and never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, and the name is drawn as a link inside the sentence: no border, fill or padding at rest, the sentence's own colour and font, a 1px underline, and the quiet fill plus the visible ring on hover and focus. It wraps like a word, so two names share a line at phone width and a long config name breaks inside the paragraph. Review corrected the first build, which drew a bordered chip per name: four of them in one sentence read as a form. A `<button>` is computed as an inline-block by Chrome even with `display: inline` (the HTML rendering model for the element), so what the screenshot gate checks is the effect — inline-level, no box of its own, one line tall, wrapping inside the paragraph — not the keyword. Labels, actions and fallbacks are unchanged.
- **The terminal resolves and lists the routes.** `keyloom chat` writes the name on the stream and holds only an open `{`, or a backtick that may still open a code span (at most 64 characters each; an unclosed brace or backtick is printed as it came; a marker split across deltas reads once; a marker inside backticks stays literal, and prints no route either, because the scan that collects the routes reads code spans the same way the stream does). After the reply, one line per reference that has a command — `to open it: keyloom config use <id>`, `to review it: keyloom change show <id>` — and nothing for a layer or a key, because the board is their surface and a terminal has nothing to run. Lookups are the ones that exist: the config list, the conversation's change sets, the head layout. `--format json` keeps `text` verbatim, markers included: a machine reader can resolve them and the stored reply is the same text.
- **The prompt teaches four forms, briefly.** Config and layer ids from `list_layouts`/`get_layout`, positions from `get_layout`/`find_function`, a proposal's id from the `propose_changes` result or the conversation; write the marker where the name belongs and keep the noun; a marker naming nothing reads as words; never a URL, a link or HTML.
- **Screens stay out.** History, the typing lab and Decisions have no marker, no client-intent protocol and no acknowledgement: rung 28's typed client intents (29) are a later stage, and this decision adds no wire field, no migration and no server behaviour.
- **Built in** `src/layout/references.ts`, `src/app/references.ts`, `src/cli/reference-stream.ts`, `src/cli/commands/chat.ts`, `src/web/chat/markdown.ts`, `src/web/chat/Markdown.tsx`, `src/web/chat/reference-view.ts`, `src/web/chat/ChatPanel.tsx`, `src/web/chat/Turns.tsx`, `src/web/chat/CarriedThread.tsx`, `src/web/chat/chat.css`, `src/web/Viewer.tsx` and `src/agent/prompts/system.md`; tests in `test/layout/references.test.ts`, `test/web/chat-markdown.test.ts`, `test/web/chat-references.test.ts`, `test/cli/reply-references.test.ts` and `test/agent/reply-references.test.ts`. Captures and the keyboard check are in [the stage C evidence](../research/ui-audit/c-reply-references/README.md).

### D-469: Open one of four existing views through the initiating send's browser, and report success only after its React render acknowledgement. Extends D-403 and D-468. (2026-10-31, stage D)

`open_view` accepts only `history|lab|decisions|settings` on the current config. History is layout version history. There is no screen marker, generic navigation registry, URL, selector, setting change, recording or hardware action. The model receives `opened`, `already-open` or a bounded `unavailable` result before answering; it must not turn navigation into a claim that data loaded or anything was saved.

A browser opts in on each new durable request with a fresh owner UUID. Accepted snapshots echo protocol 1, but never the owner. Only that send's live observer may consume its run/request/config-bound intent. Reload, history and foreign tabs are read-only observers. CLI, legacy streams, automatic reviews and copied configs' autonomous child runs return unavailable immediately.

Use one process-local waiter per capable run and the existing bounded presentation row, with at most eight intent receipts, one pending action and a five-second deadline. React acknowledges the actual committed destination. Editor/composer drafts, recording, navigation away, foreground loss, Stop, New and unmount prevent further actions. Exact duplicate acknowledgements return the saved result; wrong ownership, contradictory duplicates and late replies cannot upgrade timeout/cancellation. Restart makes saved pending intents inert, never executable. The wider chapter 29 capability proposal is not implemented by this decision.

Release approval, 2026-11-01: BK explicitly waived the unfinished independent review and Computer Use/visual checks after local tests and CI passed. Those checks remain unverified, not passed. [Stage D evidence](../research/ui-audit/d-open-view/README.md) records the limits.

## Deterministic workload evaluation (2026-11-01)

### D-470: Test an existing config against named workloads, and verify proposed gains independently of the AI. (BK's request; first implementation slice)

- **The question:** how much physical work does this config require for a particular activity, and does a small change reduce it without breaking another required task? A prose or programming workload is not a universal ideal layout.
- **Separate planning from proof.** A deterministic, bounded planner proposes physical key events. The existing QMK/ZMK emulators establish what those events send (D-075); there is no second firmware interpreter. Unsupported mechanisms and exhausted search do not prove that a task is impossible.
- **Separate the measurements.** Report physical activations, control use and concurrent keys for the same witnessed route. Do not turn them into a claim about human speed, comfort or injury risk. The declared host layout and synthetic timing are assumptions, not observations of the user.
- **Compare without applying.** Validate supplied change ops, evaluate the original and candidate snapshots under the same workload, and keep correctness regressions and tradeoffs visible. Comparison never writes a revision or starts sync. Ordinary Apply remains governed by D-014.
- **Start narrow.** The first slice is CLI diagnosis and supplied-patch comparison with original short US-layout workloads. Automatic redesign, application/editor outcomes, other locales, Kanata production emulation and integration into automatic board review remain later work. No model sees workload text or traces.
- **Keep evidence scoped.** Emulator limitations remain in the report; an unknown output-affecting warning prevents a verified claim. A result describes its snapshot and evaluator version, not an approval that survives later config changes.

### D-473: Let people choose activity tests during setup, and independently measure automatic review suggestions. Extends D-340 and D-470; replaces D-470's board-review integration deferral. (2026-11-01, BK: "do it")

**The panel's immediate-check display is replaced by D-475; API/CLI checks remain available immediately.**

- **Choose, do not infer.** Import and new-board copying offer writing, TypeScript, Python, JSON, terminal and shortcut samples. No choice means no workload builds. The choice belongs to a config, persists across its revisions, and does not silently follow a copy to another board. The review panel and `review --run --activities` can change or disable it.
- **Measure before suggesting.** The existing automatic connect/import/apply review measures the selected suite before calling the model. No provider is needed for those measurements. The model receives task IDs, verified counts, layer/modifier entry counts and the six most-used physical positions by count, not sample text, event order or HID reports. These are short US-ANSI samples, not activity frequencies, human performance or a universal layout.
- **Test the actual proposals.** Up to two visible suggestions are compared independently against the same process-local baseline, after ordinary proposal validation. Pins are hard constraints, including when replay is unavailable. Surplus or pin-breaking proposals are withdrawn. Every selected task remains protected: partial evidence cannot produce `verified-improvement`. Equivalent, regression, tradeoff, inconclusive and not-tested results stay explicit. The app writes the suggestion's measurement claim, not the model.
- **Bound the work and bind the evidence.** One baseline per run, sequential candidate replay, an eight-minute workload-review deadline, and the existing review claim prevent unbounded background work. Baseline reuse checks the snapshot, board, source, suite, policy and evaluator identities. Reports belong to one review/revision; they are never Apply approvals or persisted replay caches. A head change withdraws the old run's proposals. Changing activities starts a fresh look, not another firmware revision.
- **Reuse the existing surfaces.** Checks still arrive immediately. The review's optional `workload` field stores compact baseline/candidate results in SQLite; the panel shows coverage and expandable before/after counts, and CLI JSON/table output carries the same evidence. Selected tests may fetch pinned sources/images and need Docker. Kanata and Windows report unavailable without manufacturing evidence. Tests never discover, record, restart, sync or flash a keyboard.

Automatic optimization, representative corpora, editor/application outcomes, non-US locales and real human performance remain outside this integration. The validator and explicit Apply remain unchanged (D-014).

### D-474: Ground automatic recommendations in source facts, preserve existing behavior, and invalidate them at reconnects and input changes. Replaces D-340's once-per-revision reuse, D-343's cached-run/reconnect rules and D-344's one cached run per revision; extends D-473. (2026-11-01, BK: "fix all this. fully to completion" and "Let's not cache recommendations if the keyboard has changed")

**D-475 extends the rerun triggers to outdated reviews on already-connected keyboards, including after a contract upgrade.**

The failure was not just poor wording. The reviewer lacked the custom tap's definition, ordinary validation accepted a model-authored permission to drop it, and an old review stayed current because its revision still matched. A prompt warning alone would leave those paths open.

- `get_layout` exposes source-derived custom behavior and transitive macro definitions, modeled actions and explicit unknowns. A misleading legend is not intent. `retarget_layer_hold` prepares only a proven layer-argument edit; it does not approximate a custom key with a plain layer hold.
- Automatic review has a stricter preservation policy than explicit editing. Existing functions, custom/compound key actions, combos, encoder actions and unmodeled source/settings cannot be removed on the model's authority. Pins and tried-and-helpful decisions are hard gates, with or without activity tests. Ordinary editor/chat validation still permits explicit choices.
- The app writes proposal effects, tradeoffs and published suggestion text from the actual patch. Automatic proposals carry no decision draft, so Apply cannot supersede a user's saved reason with a generic hypothesis. It independently derives links to source checks that changed. Unsupported free-form diagnoses are withheld rather than displayed as facts.
- Source preservation, affected-behavior verification and workload improvement are separate evidence. Explicitly selected activity tests permit bounded real-core tap/hold/roll/release and layer-target witnesses. A failed behavior assertion withdraws the candidate. Unsupported custom effects remain unknown; no HID report proves an RGB-status tap works. None of these results measures human speed or comfort.
- Freshness covers the review contract and saved config, board, decision constraints and activity profile, not outcome-question timestamps. Every genuine reconnect and observed firmware-identity/half change starts a fresh analysis. The first observation after upgrade establishes a persisted device baseline; subsequent restarts compare it, so changes made while keyloom was closed also invalidate recommendations without reanalyzing unchanged devices. Disconnect/change immediately withdraws recommendations, revokes the old writer and prevents stale Apply. Stable polling makes no new model call. Kanata's changed loaded-revision marker counts as an observed change, even after our own reload; replacing a review that started before the reload can make another provider request. Hardware changes not observable through the existing sync identity are disclosed, not inferred.
- Migration 0016 retires old recommendations without a provider call or config edit. A deliberate rerun or genuine connection can request current suggestions. D-475 also permits automatic refresh of outdated connected reviews after the quiet period. One row per revision remains a storage detail, not a recommendation cache guarantee.

The tests and explicit limits are in [grounded board review](../research/grounded-board-review.md). No permission to sync or flash hardware follows from this decision.

### D-475: Refresh outdated connected reviews automatically and withhold unfinished result controls. Extends D-474; replaces D-340/D-473's immediate interactive panel checks. (2026-11-01, BK: "i don't want to have to click a button. this should just happen automatically if the conditions are met" and "it's loading a fresh review but I can still interact with the previous one")

- An active connected config with outdated review inputs, including a legacy contract, refreshes after the quiet time without a button press. The first device observation still establishes a baseline; it does not block this eligible refresh. An absent keyboard waits for a connection or explicit request. Stable polling and unchanged restarts reuse a current review, including its unavailable-provider result.
- The panel and marker show a dedicated loading state during initial reads, refresh requests and pending/running reviews. Results, counts and their controls stay out of that view; Close remains available. Stale findings are read-only and stale/unfinished board marks are withheld. Source checks remain available immediately through CLI/API.
- A review event locks the previous results before the replacement GET returns. Request ordering and config identity prevent delayed GET/POST responses from restoring an old snapshot. Events for other configs neither reload settled results nor release an unresolved read lock. A failed read, including a head-change read, keeps the old results locked and offers a read-only retry without a running spinner. The marker remains openable after an initial read failure. Dismiss/restore requests disable actions without replacing the results subtree or discarding an activity draft.

This changes review scheduling and presentation only. Provider terms still apply; no Apply, sync or flash is authorized. Shipped and locally installed in v0.68.2, 2026-11-02; its [evidence](../research/grounded-board-review.md#automatic-refresh-and-loading-follow-up) is separate from the v0.68.1 receipt.

### D-477: Review suggestions show source-derived changes, not stored model prose. (2026-11-02, rung 1)

The review panel derives a linked suggestion's presentation from its validated proposal ops and current layout, including for reviews stored by older releases. Missing proposal data is shown as unverifiable; stored body text is not a fallback. Changed profile options use named values and units, and affected key positions are shown as keycaps. Source preservation, runtime evidence and typing outcomes remain distinct. CLI JSON remains the machine-readable representation.

### D-478: Display top recently opened keyboards on a SKÅDIS pegboard home page with a 3D flight transition to the desk configurator. (2026-11-02, BK: "design a home landing page that shows a pegboard with my top four keyboards on display on it... when you click one there's an animation to transition into the configurator page and when you click home there's an animation to transition back to the pegboard")

- **Landing surface:** A dedicated home view styled as a SKÅDIS pegboard panel matching the room's palette. Slots use the standard 5×15 mm rounded slot pattern with a 40 mm pitch and 20 mm offset.
- **Selection rule:** Displays up to four unique keyboards corresponding to the user's most recently opened configurations (falling back to `updatedAt` for existing layouts). Archived layouts are omitted.
- **Physical representation:** Each board is rendered in accurate geometry and keycap colourways, resting on contoured ledge mounts fitted to the board or split halves, tilted back 12° in 3D perspective.
- **Flight navigation:** Selecting a board lifts it from the pegboard cradle and flies it into the flat desk editor using View Transitions and 3D transforms (`rotateX`, `translate3d`, scale). Navigating "Home" via the top bar triggers the reverse flight back to its pegboard slot. Reduced motion preferences disable the 3D movement and use an instant crossfade.
- **Data storage:** Added `opened_at` to `layouts` table via migration 0018, updated whenever a layout is opened in the configurator, and surfaced via `keyloom layout recent`.

### D-479: Plan a repair for one review finding, then send and apply deliberately. Supersedes the blanket GUI action in D-461; preserves its global CLI command. (2026-11-02, rung 2a)

The review offers a per-finding repair action for a current visible problem or hint. It opens an editable request that names the finding and its keys/layers; opening it does not start an agent run or alter the layout. Explicit Send starts a separate conversation only when the current run is idle. The agent is asked to work on that finding, but the request is not a hard tool-scope boundary. The person reviews the ordinary proposal and explicitly applies it. The pending request and edits are kept in App-owned state for that config across Home navigation, not persisted or shared across configs. Existing hidden findings and suggestions retain their meanings.

`keyloom review fix [layout] --finding <id>` explicitly requests a proposal for one current visible problem or hint using the same formatter. `--draft` prints the request without a model call or writes. The no-flag command remains the legacy broad request for compatibility. No request schema or persistence protocol changes.
### D-480: Recover a refused proposal through an editable original-goal request. Extends D-479; replaces generic automatic Ask Again. (2026-11-02, rung 2b)

The selected saved proposal or recorded unsaved `propose_changes` validation refusal supplies the source. Use its original request when known; label a model-authored saved goal or explanation as a fallback. Code-only historical observations explain the validation category and admit that the exact affected target was not recorded. Generic failures, truncated observations and partial acceptance do not prove an unsaved refusal.

Reuse the App-owned config-keyed repair request. Opening sends nothing, overwrites no pending request and cancels no work. Explicit Send starts a separate ordinary chat request after source/head and composer checks. A definite refusal keeps edits; uncertain acceptance remains observation-only. The validator and explicit Apply still decide. No wire/storage expansion or raw tool results are needed.

`agent replan <config> --proposal <id>` or `--conversation <id> --turn <user-message-id>` resolves the source read-only before starting work. `--draft` prints the shared request without writes. A different base alone is not a refusal: disjoint proposals may still validate. Long requests retain the full goal and require shortening rather than truncation.
